Substack help

Substack help

This guide holds Substack’s own help for readers and for writers, gathered on 18 August 2026 from the publisher’s own listing, so every article arrived with its title, its section and its body. A reader looking only for how to manage subscriptions can skip the sections written for publishers, which are named as such.

Chat

Can I direct message another reader or creator on Substack?

The direct messaging (DMs) feature lets you connect with other creators and readers on the Substack app or Substack.com. With DMs, you can connect with others by easily sharing notes and posts, sending images and links, and reacting to messages.

DMs live in the Chat tab alongside chats from publications you subscribe to. DMs differ from Chat because you can privately message one person rather than hold a public conversation with your subscribers.

How can I start messaging someone on Substack?

You can initiate a DM from a Substack profile, from the Chat tab, or by sharing a post or note.

To send a DM, follow these steps:

  • From a profile: On a profile, click the arrow icon, and a DM window will open.
  • From the Chat tab: Click on the compose icon and enter the Substack handle or the name of the person you’d like to message in the search bar.
  • From a note or post: On the Substack app, tap the share icon and and enter the Substack handle or name of the person you’d like to share the note/post with into the search bar. Click the share icon on the web and select “Send as message.” In the search bar, search for someone by name or Substack handle. Tip: At this time, read receipts is not available.

How do I start a group chat?

You can create a group DM with up to 50 people on iOS, Android, and web. On web, head to your Chat tab and select the pencil icon. Select “New group chat”.

Things to note:

  • As the Admin, you can rename the group DM, invite or remove members from the group chat, and if you leave the group, an existing member will take the lead.
  • Group DM members can invite others and leave whenever they’d like. Who can I receive messages from?

By default, you can receive DMs from anyone with a Substack profile. People you follow or subscribe to on Substack can always message you directly.

Message requests appear in an Unread folder, and here, you can filter by subscribers or all users. You can accept or reject a message request– senders will not be notified if you reject their request.

To change who you receive message requests from, head to your Substack account settings and scroll to the Messaging section. Next to “Allow message requests from”, select from the drop-down menu who can request to message you on Substack.

  • Everyone: Any user with a Substack profile can send you a message request.
  • Free subscribers: If you have a Substack, readers who are on the free plan will be able to send you a message request.
  • Paid subscribers: Readers who are subscribed to a monthly, annual, or founding members plan can send you a message request.
  • Founding subscribers: Only readers on a founding members plan will be able to send you a message request.
  • No one: Direct messaging will be disabled, and no one can send you a message request. However, you can message other readers and creators on Substack. Tip: To change these settings on the app, tap the Unread folder and select the Settings icon.

On web chat, if you receive a message from a subscriber and want to see more details about them, click on the three dots at the top of the chat. Then select “Subscriber details” which will send you to their specific subscriber page that you’d find on the Subscribers tab on your Dashboard.

How do I change my notifications settings?

When someone sends you a message, you’ll receive a push notification from the Substack app or an email, depending on your notification settings. By default, notifications for new messages and requests will be turned on.

Click on your profile picture on the Substack iOS app and select the Settings gear icon. Tap on Notifications and select Messaging. Uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “New messages” and “New message requests” if you don’t want to receive any DM notifications.

Can I block someone from messaging me?

People you’ve blocked on Substack or banned from your publication cannot direct message you. If you’ve been messaging someone and then block them on Substack, your DM thread with them will be removed from your Chat tab.

You can mute a DM thread if you’d prefer to silence notifications instead of blocking someone. Click the three dots on a DM thread on the Substack app. From the menu, select the duration for how long you’d like the muting to last.

If you’d like to report a message, click on the three dots next to the message on web or hard press the message on the Substack app. Fill out the form, and our Standards & Enforcement team will review the message and your report.

Can I delete a DM?

You can delete a DM thread by hard pressing on the thread on the Substack app and tapping “Leave” or by clicking on the three dots on a DM thread on the web and selecting “Leave” from the menu.

You can also delete individual messages on the Substack app by tapping the message and selecting “Delete”. This will remove the message for you and the person you’re messaging. Deleting individual messages on the web is not possible at this time.

How do I encourage readers to DM me?

On a draft post, click on Buttons in the editor toolbar. Select “Send a message”.

Clicking on the button will open the chat in a web browser or open a new message on the Substack app.

If your message requests are set to paid subscribers or founding subscribers, free readers may see a prompt to upgrade their subscription when they click the message button in the post.

Readers who try to message you on the Substack app will see a paying subscribers only message if your message request setting is set to paid or founding subscribers.

Can I search in a Chat on Substack?

On the web, navigate to a chat, then click on the search icon in the top-right, next to the three-dot menu. Then, type in your search term in the popup window.

In the app, navigate to a chat, then click on the search icon in the top-right corner.

How can I moderate my Chat?

Substack Chat is available on the web and the Substack app.

Here are several options to moderate your Chat:

  • Lock a chat thread by turning off replies on the Substack app.
  • Delete any reply by long-pressing it and tapping, “Delete” on the app or clicking on the three dots and selecting “Delete” on the web.
  • Ban any subscriber from your Chat by long-pressing their reply and tapping, “Ban” on the web. Note: To manage users who’ve been banned from commenting, head to the Blocked section on your Substack account settings page.

If subscribers are posting images or videos you’d prefer not to have in your Chat, you can remove their ability to post images.

For the Substack app

Long-press your Substack on the main Chat dashboard and select “Chat settings”. Move the toggle next to “Photos and videos” to the left. Turning this off affects all your chat threads and subscribers will not be able to post an image reply.

For the web

On your publication’s Settings page, head to the Chat section. Slide the toggle to the left next to “Enable media attachments”.

How do I view and manage reported chats to my publication?

On your publication’s Settings page, go to Community. Click on Moderate next to “Moderate reported content”.

Subscribers can report platform level content violations to Substack staff, however, they also have the option to report unwanted chats to publication admins.

Reported chats will appear in the “Reported” tab. Review the chat and select “Approve” or “Remove”.

  • If you choose to remove the chat, it’ll appear in the “Removed” tab and won’t be visible to other readers.
  • If you choose to approve the chat, it’ll appear in the “Approved” tab and remain visible on your publication. To see why a reader reported a chat, select “See report reasons”.

How can I mute or leave a Chat?

Substack Chat is available on the web and the Substack app.

If you’d like to stop receiving push notifications for Chat on the Substack app, here are several options:

Turn off notifications for a publication for iOS

On the Substack iOS app, tap and hold on a publication and select “Mute”.

Select how long you’d like to mute the chat for: 1 hour, 8 hours, 24 hours, or until you manually unmute the chat.

Turn off notifications for a publication for Android

Tap on a publication on your Chat dashboard and select “Mute”. Select how long you’d like to mute the chat for: 1 hour, 8 hours, until tomorrow, or until you turn it back on.

Turn off all notifications for Chat on the Substack app

Tap your profile picture and select Settings. In the Settings menu, tap Notifications.

For iOS

On your Notifications, make sure to uncheck each item listed in the Chat section. You will not receive any push notifications about Chat.

For Android

Tap your profile picture and select Settings. Tap Notifications then Settings and uncheck each item in the Chat section. This means that Chat notifications to your Android device will stop immediately.

To leave a Chat

To leave a chat, tap and long press a publication and select “Leave chat”.

How can I participate in a Chat?

Substack Chat is available on the web and the Substack app.

Once a creator starts a thread, you can participate by tapping on the Chat icon on the Substack app or on the web. Substacks you subscribe to will appear showing the most recent threads.

Tap on a thread to start interacting with the creator and other readers. You can reply to any thread with text, image, or video as well as react to other readers’ replies.

You can also tap on another reader’s message to react or to leave a reply.

How am I notified about Chat?

You can select which Chat push notifications to receive on the Substack app.

  • New threads: When a creator you’re subscribed to starts a new Chat thread.
  • Replies: When other readers leave a reply in a Chat thread.
  • Reactions: When readers emoji-react to your reply. To turn on or turn off Chat notifications, follow these steps:

  1. Tap your profile picture and select Settings. In the Settings menu, tap Notifications.

  2. On your Notifications, make sure that the boxes are checked next to each item listed in the Chat section. If the boxes are unchecked under the Push column, you will not receive any push notifications about Chat.

Who can see my replies?

Any subscriber of the publication will be able to see your reply in a Thread. If they react to your message, you’ll see a notification in your Activity section (click on the bell in the top right corner of the Substack app).

Tip: Don’t see a thread? Check to see if you’re on the latest version of the Substack app and check your profile picture to see if the publication is listed in your subscription library.

Can I start a thread?

If the creator has enabled reader participation for their publication’s Chat, you’ll be able to start a conversation with other subscribers. Just enter what you’d like to share where it says “Start a new thread…”.

If you don’t see the option to start a new thread on the web or on the Substack app, it’s possible the creator has disabled the reader participation feature.

How do I adjust my Chat notifications?

If you have Chat notifications enabled on the Substack app, you can receive push notifications when a reader replies in a thread, shares an image reply, reacts to a thread, or starts their own thread.

To turn off reply notifications for a specific Chat thread on the Substack app

If you don’t want to receive push notifications about replies or reactions in a Chat thread, you can turn them off.

For authors:

On iOS, long press on your initial message and tap “Turn off notifications” so that it is grayed out.

On Android, tap into the thread and click on the activity bell at the top of the screen.

For readers:

On iOS, tap on a chat thread and tap “Turn off notifications”.

On Android, tap on the activity bell at the top of a thread, and a confirmation that “reply notifications off” will apear.

To turn off Chat replies and reactions for all Substacks on the app

  1. Tap your profile picture and select the gear icon at the bottom of your profile. In the Settings menu, tap Notifications then Preferences. Select Messaging.

  2. In the Chats section, make sure that the “Push” box is unchecked next to each item listed in this section. You will not receive any push notifications about Chat moving forward.

Learn more here on how to mute a publication’s Chat.

How do I paywall my Chat on Substack?

Paid publications can gate access to their Chat to only paid subscribers or founding members. There are three options for paywalling your Chat:

Paywall individual chat threads

Similar to paywalled posts, you can paywall individual chat thread replies. When starting a thread via the app, click settings icon and tap “Audience”. Select whether you want to gate the audience to paid subscribers only or all subscribers.

If you paywall an individual chat thread, only paid subscribers can reply but will be able to see your initial message– this preview cannot be removed.

Paywall the entire chat

In your publication’s Settings, you can paywall access to your entire chat space rather than on a thread-by-thread basis. First click “Enable chat paywall,” then select whether to gate access to paid subscribers or just founding members next to “Who can bypass the paywall”.

Subscribers without access cannot view or reply to any chat threads. If they try to navigate to your chat, they’ll be prompted to upgrade to participate.

Paywall permission to start new threads

You can limit the ability to start new chat threads to a specific subscriber tier via your chat settings. All other subscribers can view and reply to threads but not start them.

How do I start a Chat thread?

Substack Chat is available on the web and the Substack app.

The easiest way to start a chat thread on web head to Chat and click on the pencil icon.

Tap your row and then select “Start a new thread”. Each chat thread you start can include text, links, video, or up to six images.

Type a message you’d like to share with your subscribers.

  • To add an image: Tap on the picture icon to select photos from your device.
  • To add a video: Tap on the video icon to upload from your gallery. If you’re using the Substack app, you can tap “Camera” to record a video directly from your phone’s camera. Videos in Chat can be up to five minutes long and the recommended resolution is 1080p or lower. If you allow subscribers to start their own chat threads, they can share their own images and videos with the community.
  • Choose your audience: If you hav payments enabled, you can select if you’d like any subscribers, paid subscribers, or founding members to view and reply in the thread. Note that all subscribers will be able to preview your initial message but the reply function and viewing replies will be limited to your select audience.
  • Send as email: Check the “Send as email” box letting subscribers know you’ve started a new Chat thread.
  • Schedule a thread: Click the calendar icon to pick a date and time for a scheduled chat to go live. Tip: If you’ve started a chat thread for your Substack and would like to edit it, you can do that on the web version of Substack. Click on the three dots next to the thread and select “Edit”.

How are subscribers notified about Chat?

When you post your first chat, we’ll notify your subscribers via email. Your subscribers won’t be emailed about chat threads after that unless you’ve selected to send the thread as an email. If they have notifications enabled on the Substack app, they’ll receive a push notification about any new threads.

Can I paywall my entire chat?

Slide the toggle next to “Enable chat paywall” on your Settings page and select whether you want to gate access to paid subscribers or just founding members.

Subscribers without access cannot view or reply to any chat threads. If they try to access your chat, they’ll be prompted to upgrade to participate.

Can my subscribers start a new thread?

You can let other members of your Substack community initiate a new thread in your publication’s Chat.

On your desktop, head to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Chat section.

  • Payments enabled: Next to “Who can start new threads”, select which type of reader you’d like to be able to start a new thread on your Chat. The options are everyone, paid subscribers, founding members, or only publication contributors. If you’ve paywalled your entire chat, you will not see the option for free subscribers.
  • Free publications: Free publications will see the options for publication contributors and subscribers. Note: Publication contributors, founding members, and paid subscribers can start chat threads, but any subscriber, free and paid, can participate and respond to the threads unless your entire chat is paywalled to just paid subscribers or founding members. If you do not want your subscribers to start a new thread, select “Only publication contributors” next to “Who can start new threads.

Invite your readers to join

The most important step in launching your Chat is personally inviting subscribers to join the community.

Go to your chat settings, then open the “Getting started” section. Next to “Tell your subscribers,” we’ve included an editable post template that makes it easy to instruct subscribers how to join your Chat.

In addition to the template, we recommend adding a personal note about how you hope to use the chat. For example, you can specify if the chat is restricted to paid subscribers, if you plan to host AMAs, or if readers can start their chat threads to discuss shared interests.

How do I tag other subscribers in Chat?

Substack Chat is available on the web and the Substack app.

On a Chat thread, you can mention another subscriber or the writer in a reply by “@” their Substack profile name.

If the subscriber or writer has Mentions disabled for their Substack profile, their Substack profile name will not appear in the search results.

How do I turn off direct messaging notifications on Substack?

When someone sends you a direct message on Substack, you’ll receive a push notification from the Substack app or an email, depending on your notification settings.

By default, notifications for new messages and requests will be turned on.

You can change DM notifications in the Notifications section on the Substack iOS app (Messages section on Android) or your Substack account settings page.

On the web

In the Messaging section on your Substack Settings page, uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “Messages” and “Message Requests” to disable receiving notifications for every new message and new message request.

On the Substack app

Click on your profile picture on the Substack iOS app and select the Settings gear icon. Tap on Notifications and select Messaging. Uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “New messages” and “New message requests” if you don’t want to receive any DM notifications.

How do I turn off reader replies on a Chat?

Substack Chat is available on the web and the Substack app.

If you’d like subscribers to stop replying in a thread, you can turn it off by locking it on the Substack app.

On the app, long press your initial message and tap “Turn off replies”.

You can always resume replies on a locked thread by tapping “Turn on replies” which will unlock the thread to your subscribers.

Frequently Asked Questions

Can I add an image to a comment on Substack?

At this time, Substack comments support text only. You cannot attach images, GIFs, or video to a comment on a post. To share an image publicly, you can post it as a Note, add it to a chat thread, or include it in a new post.

Where to share images instead

Notes — for readers and creators

Notes support images, GIFs, links, and video. Anyone with a Substack account can post a note or reply to a note based on a creator’s settings.

Chat — for readers and creators

Chat threads support images. Chat must be enabled on your publication and media attachments allowed.

Posts — for creators

Posts support images, galleries, GIFs, and video.

Getting started on the Substack app

Connect directly with the creators behind your favorite publications and fellow subscribers.

The Substack app puts new posts in a reading queue and pairs it with a discovery feed that showcases the vitality of the Substack network to help readers find things they love and value.

In this guide, we’ll show you how you can:

  • Discover notes and new creators on Home
  • Filter to find saved and audio posts in your Inbox
  • Connect with fellow readers in a Substack Chat
  • View notes, posts, and followers on your Substack profile

On the Home tab, you’ll be greeted by a prominent queue of posts from creators you subscribe to and see notes, posts, recommendations, and conversations with creators and readers you follow.

See notes, comments, and likes from people you follow on Substack. For creators, the home feed prioritizes showing your notes, likes, and comments to your paid and free subscribers. Your notes may also be recommended to others, such as readers who subscribe to creators who recommend you, in their Home feed.

Interested in learning more about notes? Check out a dedicated section here.

Things to note about Substack notes:

  • If you’d prefer to hide your post likes, edit your Substack profile and toggle off next to “Your likes”.
  • You can “mute” or “block” users to stop seeing their notes and activity. Mute prevents you from seeing them on your Home feed and block also prevents them from seeing/interacting with you.
  • If you mute or block an author, we won’t recommend their notes or posts to you.

Inbox

The Inbox tab brings together all of your subscriptions in one place which can be sorted by media, saved, and paid posts.

New posts are shown in chronological order and you can swipe right to save, or left to archive.

To see posts that you’ve saved from your Home feed or Inbox, select the “Saved” tab. To listen to podcast episodes, select the “Audio” tab. To see your paid posts, select the “Paid” tab.

Saved is designed to send posts to your Inbox that you might discover in the Home feed through a note or recommendations or to help prioritize the unread posts already in your Inbox. When you save a post from the feed, it goes to your Inbox and bumps it to the top of the reading queue. When you archive a post, it removes it from both places.

Tip: Head to your app Settings to choose between the classic inbox design or a more compact dense version.

Chat

Chats is a space for creators to host conversations with their subscribers and cultivate a community around their publications. These are private messages viewable only by subscribers, which are meant to encourage discussion or conversation.

We hope it’ll allow you to share quick updates, photos, and discussion prompts on the go; and make reader engagement more vibrant through features like image replies and emoji reactions.

Check out frequently asked questions about Substack chat here.

Your Substack profile

At the top of the Substack app, you’ll find your notifications and your profile picture.

If you have notifications toggled on for things such as likes, replies, mentions, restacks, and new notes, you’ll receive push notifications on your device. If you have these toggled off, you can still see this activity by tapping on the Bell icon.

Tapping on your profile picture will open a menu of your subscription library, archive, and app settings.

In your Settings, you can change things on the app such as:

  • Notifications: Adjust your push notifications and how you receive new posts.
  • Edit Profile: Change your name, bio, and select whether to display what publications you read on your profile.
  • Inbox appearance: Choose between a classic (social preview images will appear) or a dense view (title and subtitle will be smaller and social preview images will be hidden). What appears on my Substack profile?

Your profile will display:

  • Your photo and bio
  • Any social links you’ve connected
  • What publications you write for
  • What publications you read (this can be hidden)
  • Any Substack posts you’ve published or notes you’ve posted
  • Your subscribers (if you have a publication), followers, and who you’re following on Substack
  • Your likes Note: For creators, the subscriber list only includes a small subset of your subscribers who have Substack profiles and have their subscription visibility set to public. This is a reader-side setting they adjust and control.

If you tap “View Creator Stats” on the three dots on your profile, you can access a streamlined view of key metrics like total free and paid subscribers and total revenue, with charts over time. If you have multiple publications, only the stats for your primary publication will be displayed. Creator stats is only visible to you.

Click on “Total earnings” to see a total net revenue and a breakdown of your payouts after all applicable fees (Substack and Stripe fees). These payouts will mirror the payouts on your Stripe account and may include activity from sources other than Substack if you use your Stripe account for multiple projects or businesses.

How can I navigate my Substack on desktop?

When you sign into Substack.com on desktop or mobile web, the first page you’ll see will be the Home page for your Substack account.

On Home, we’ll show a feed of posts from Substacks you subscribe to and a Notes feed.

At the top, click on categories such as New Bestseller, Culture, or Sports to discover new Substacks and notes related to that category.

On the left side of the page, we’ll show you quick shortcuts to Home, Subscriptions, Chat, Activity, Search, and the option to publish a note or to go directly to your draft editor.

Tip: If you want to view any of the sections such as your Notes feed or posts feed in dark or light mode, click on your profile picture and select “Appearance”. Then select Auto, dark, or light.

To access your Substack or publication, click on the graph icon (Dashboard) above your profile picture on Home.

Once you click on the dashboard icon, you’ll land on your publications’s Home. On your publication’s homepage, you’ll see a sidebar on the left where you can manage your content and your Substack. If you prefer to view your Substack in dark or light mode, click on the sun and moon located in the bottom right corner of the page.

How can I publish on Substack?

On Substack, you can publish from your profile and website. You can select whether you want people to view and read your posts from your profile or access a custom website.

In this guide, we’ll show you how to navigate your Substack profile so that you can publish posts, get subscribers from the Substack network, and get paid for what you create.

How do I get started?

You’ll be asked to select a handle when you sign up for Substack and create a new profile. Handles are unique, making it easy for others to mention you in notes, posts, and profile sharing. Your handle will also determine your Substack profile URL: substack.com/@handle.

Your Substack profile is public and will show posts you’ve written, your notes or restacks, and things you liked and currently read on Substack.

To edit your profile, sign into your Substack account and on your profile, click “Edit profile”. You can change your profile name, handle, and bio and add social media or website links here.

If you want other users to view your posts on your profile, ensure that your Substack isn’t in private mode and that you’ve selected to display Substacks you write on your profile.

How do I manage my Substack?

You can access your publisher dashboard on the web by clicking on the three dots on your profile and selecting “Publisher dashboard”.

On the publisher dashboard, you can do the following:

  • Publish text, video, and audio posts and manage your drafts.
  • Manage your subscribers (add, remove, export)
  • See essential stats such as traffic and annual revenue
  • Enable pledges, which allow your subscribers to pledge to become a paid subscriber if and when you turn on paid subscriptions.
  • Recommend other Substacks and see who’s recommending your Substack How do I know if people are engaging with my Substack?

Turning on notifications is a key way to get alerted on how people in the Substack network are engaging with your Substack.

Click “Publisher dashboard” on your profile and head to the Settings tab. Ensure that these settings are toggled to “On”.

  • Enable comments, likes, and restacks: If this is toggled on, subscribers can like, comment on, and restack your posts.

  • New free subscriber: If this is toggled on, you’ll be notified by email and see an Activity notification about new subscribers. Examples of free subscriber notifications

  • Likes and comments: You’ll be notified by email and get an Activity notification when someone likes your post or leaves a comment. Tip: If you have the Substack App installed and notifications enabled, you’ll also receive a push notification when someone engages with your Substack, such as subscribing, liking, or re-stacking a note or commenting on a post.

How do I publish a post on my Substack?

You can publish a post directly from your profile on the web or on the Substack iOS and Android apps. Learn more on how to publish a post from the web.

Tip: If you want to publish a podcast or video post, head to your publisher dashboard and select “Create” from either the Home or Posts tabs.

Tap the plus icon on the Substack iOS app and select “Post”. The Substack mobile editor will open. Learn more on how to publish a post from the Substack app.

Can I make my Substack profile private?

At this time, you can’t disable people’s ability to subscribe or follow your profile. Notes you post will be public and appear on your profile.

However, you can make your Substack posts private and they will not appear in the Posts tab on your profile.

On your Settings page, scroll to the Privacy section and slide the toggle next to “Private mode”. After putting your Substack in private mode, readers will see a “This Substack is private” message on the Welcome page when they go to your custom website.

You can choose if you want your likes and what Substacks you read to be shown on your profile.

On your profile, in the Activity privacy section, tap the toggle next to “Your likes” to the left if you want to hide what posts and notes you like on Substack.

In the Subscription privacy section, slide the toggle to the left next to the Substacks you do not want to appear on your profile’s “Reads” tab.

How do I switch between my profile and my custom website?

Sign into your Substack account on the web and click on Dashboard. Select Website editor from the left sidebar.

In the Website editor, go to the Home page tab and under “Theme” select whether your want your Substack URL (https://subdomain.substack.com) to match your Substack profile or Custom theme.

  • If you select “Profile”, when viewers go to subdomain.substack.com, they’ll see a mirror of your Substack profile.
  • If you select “Custom”, when viewers go to subdomain.substack.com, they’ll see a full website where the advanced layouts, branding, and logos that you’ve selected will be on display.

How can I recover my Substack account if I lose access?

If you’re unable to log in to Substack because you’ve lost access to your email address or can’t remember the address used to create your account, please visit our Support page here. If you’ve lost access to your two-factor authentication app, you can reset two-factor authentication on your Substack account by answering your recovery questions.

We strongly encourage our entire Substack community to enable account security questions by following these steps. Enabling this security feature makes it much more likely that we’ll be able to grant you access again so you can get back to reading and writing.

How can I sync my contacts and follow my friends on Substack?

The best way to find remarkable writing is through recommendations from trusted friends and other readers. If you want to find your friends and see what they’re reading, publishing, and liking on Substack, the easiest way is to sync your device’s contact book via the Substack app.

  1. Install the Substack app for iOS or Android and sign into your Substack account.

  2. Tap your profile picture and select “Find friends” on iOS and “Add friends” on Android.

  3. Tap the “Connect” button next to “Contacts”.

  4. You’ll be prompted to first verify your phone number via text message then grant permission for the Substack app to access your device’s contact book.

Note: All contacts are stored securely as hashed and not in plain text– your contacts data will never be shared with third parties.

  1. If you have any contact matches, you’ll see a suggested list of their Substack profiles and the option to follow them. Contact syncing is a two-sided match: by syncing your contacts and verifying your number, whenever a friend who also has your number or email address syncs their contacts, both of you will then be prompted to connect on Substack.

Tip: If a friend is already on Substack and you follow them, you’ll see “Following” once you sync your contacts. If you don’t have any matches now, we’ll notify you as soon as one of your contacts joins Substack and sync their contacts–you’ll also see the option to invite friends to join you on Substack!

Other than contact matching, you can find people you know on Substack via Facebook connect or by searching for their name on Substack.

How do I delete contacts I synced to Substack?

  1. Log into your Substack account on the web and head to your Account Settings: substack.com

  2. Select “Edit” next to “Phone”.

  3. Click on “Remove” next to the phone number on file. Your phone number will automatically be deleted from your Substack account.

How do I delete my phone number on Substack?

If you’ve connected your Contacts on the Substack app to find your friends, you can delete it from your Substack account by following these steps.

  • Log into your Substack account on the web and head to your Account Settings: substack.com
  • Select “Edit” next to “Phone”.
  1. Click on “Remove” next to the phone number on file. Your phone number will automatically be deleted from your Substack account.

How do I edit my profile theme on Substack?

You can customize your Substack profile by uploading a header to display at the top of your profile and choosing an accent color and background tint. If your Substack website is in private mode, you will not see the Branding option when you’re on the edit profile page.

On the web

  1. Sign into your Substack account and click on your profile picture. Select Edit Profile.

  2. Scroll down to the Branding section to customize your profile.

Header: Upload an image that has a minimum of 3:2 aspect ratio and is at least 1344x256 pixels. This image will appear at the top of your Substack profile and appear as the wordmark on your Substack website.

Accent color: Select a color for the buttons on your profile that users will see when they visit your profile or your Substack website.

Background color: Select a color for the background of your profile that users will be see when they visit your profile or your Substack website.

Note: The option to upload a cover photo to your Substack profile has been deprecated. If you’ve previously uploaded a cover photo to your Substack profile, these photos have been removed.

How do I listen to episodes on my podcast app?

You can listen to episodes directly on your favorite podcast app if you’re subscribed to a Substack that publishes podcasts.

Using a link sent via email (on your phone)

  1. In the podcast setup email you receive from Substack, click on “Set up podcast” and a new tab will open on your browser. Alternatively, you can sign into your Substack account and access the private podcast feed on the manage subscription page of the Substack you want to listen to.

Note: If you tap your preferred podcast app and it does not load, please try manually adding the podcast feed URL to the app itself.

You can also add podcast episodes by scanning a QR code. Select your preferred podcast app, and if a QR code is available, it should appear automatically.

  1. Select your podcast app, and your unique private RSS feed should automatically populate in the show URL box.

  2. Follow the show, and you can now access episodes in your Library.

Manually add a private RSS feed to your podcast app

On your account Settings page:

  1. Click on the publication you’d like to listen to on your podcast app.

  2. On your manage subscription page, scroll to the Private podcast feed section. Click “Set up podcast”.

  3. Copy the feed URL and paste it into your podcast app.

  • Apple Podcasts: Click on Library and the three dots in the top right corner. Select “Add a Show by URL” and enter the link you copied from Substack.
  • Overcast: Click on the plus sign and tap Add URL. Enter the link that you copied from Substack.
  • Pocket Casts: Enter the link you copied from Substack into the search bar. The show will appear. Tap on the plus sign to add the Substack podcast to this app. Tip: Typically, you can enter the URL into the podcast app show search bar or in an “Add URL” section.

For listeners with paid subscriptions

The publication you’re subscribed to may offer exclusive audio or episodes for paying subscribers only. If you’re a paid subscriber, paid episodes will appear in your private RSS feed. If the podcaster publishes these, free listeners may see free previews of paid episodes in their feed.

If you cancel your paid subscription, your subscription status will change to a free subscriber, and your feed will return to the same as a free feed.

On the Spotify app

If you’re a paying subscriber to a Substack podcast, you can now listen to both free and paid episodes on Spotify. For a more convenient listening experience, you can learn how to link your Substack and Spotify accounts to access free and paid episodes on Spotify.

How do I log into the Substack app? I haven’t received the login email link.

For current subscribers

Enter the email address you use to subscribe to publications on Substack i.e. the same email address you receive newsletters at on the login screen, and we’ll send you an email with a verification code to sign into the Substack app.

Once you get the Substack email, enter the verification code on the Substack app or tap the “Verify email” link and you’ll be automatically signed into the Substack app. This link can only be used once and will expire after ten minutes.

If you’ve already set up a password and would prefer to log in that way, click “Log in with password” on the Substack app login page and enter your email address and password.

For new readers

If you’re new to Substack, you can just enter an email address you’d like to use to sign up for a Substack account for free. Once you sign into the app, you can create a profile and become a free subscriber to any publication you’d like.

I’m not seeing the login email link– what can I do?

If you’re not receiving an email from Substack with the verification code and have checked your Promotions and Spam folder, it’s possible that emails from Substack have been previously marked as spam, and your email provider has blocked us.

Add no-reply@substack.com to your email contacts list and try requesting a new login email again.

How do I reset my password on Substack?

If you can’t remember your password to your Substack account, we can send you an email to set a new password.

Visit the Forgot Password page and we’ll email you a link that you can use to set your password.

I haven’t received an email– what can I do?

We recommend trying these troubleshooting tips:

  • Check your spam folder to see if our emails have gone there. If so, make sure to “mark as not spam” in your email client.
  • If you are on a corporate or government email account, talk to your IT department and tell them to whitelist emails from Substack.com. They often have email filters that are out of your individual control and can quickly resolve the issue. If you’re still not getting our password reset emails, just get in touch with us and we can help!

How do I stop receiving emails from Substack?

When you subscribe to a publication on Substack, free or paid, we automatically create a Substack account for you.

We’ll send occasional marketing emails about new features or recommend other publications to read.

To stop receiving emails from Substack, follow these steps:

  1. Click on your profile icon and select Settings (https://substack.com/settings) from the drop-down menu.

  2. On your account Settings page, head to the Marketing section.

  • To stop receiving emails about new features and updates, uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “Marketing”.
  • To stop receiving reading suggestions, uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “Reading suggestions”.
  • To stop receiving emails about replies, mentions, or notes you may’ve missed, uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “Activity Digests”. If you’re receiving email or push notifications about “New followers” for your Substack profile, head to the Connections section on substack.com and uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “New followers”.

How do I stop receiving live video notifications on Substack?

Substack will send you live video notifications when someone you follow or subscribe to on Substack does a live video.

On your Substack account Settings page in the Live section, you can control what notifications to receive about live video on Substack. On this page, you can select whether to enable or disable notifications as a subscriber or as an invited guest.

To stop receiving email and push notifications when someone you subscribe to starts a live video on Substack, uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “New live videos”.

To stop receiving email and push notifications when someone schedules a live video on Substack, uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “Live video calendar invites”.

To stop receiving email and push notifications when someone invites you to be a live video guest on Substack, uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “New live guest invites”.

To customize who can invite you as a guest on their live stream select from the drop-down menu next to “Allow live video guest invites from”.

  • Everyone: Any Substack app user can send you an invite to join their live video
  • People I follow or subscribe to: Only people you follow or subscribe to on Substack can send you an invite to join their live video
  • No one: Being a guest on someone’s live video will be disabled and no one can send you an invite

How do I translate a note or post on Substack?

Note: If you’re a creator, you can now add versions of the same post in different languages. Learn more at: How do I publish a Substack post in a different language

If you see notes or posts on Substack written in a different language, you can now read them in your preferred language. You can translate notes into the following languages:

  • English
  • English (British)
  • Catalan
  • Danish
  • German
  • Spanish
  • French
  • Italian
  • Japanese
  • Norwegian
  • Dutch
  • Polish
  • Portuguese (European)
  • Portuguese (Brazilian)
  • Swedish
  • Turkish
  • Arabic On web
  1. Go to the notes feed on your Home or a creator’s profile (look for the Notes tab). You may see a note that’s been automatically translated into your device’s preferred language.

Bien ou Bien writes in French on Notes

  1. Click on “Translated” to give feedback, check your translation settings, or revert the note back to its original language.

Note: Only you can see the translated note. Using the translation feature will not change the original language for other readers.

If you don’t want your notes or posts to be automatically translated, you can turn off automatic translations on your Substack Account Settings page: substack.com

Scroll to the Translations section and slide the toggle next to “Automatically translate” to the left.

If you never want a specific language to be translated, select “Manage” and choose the language(s) from the list shown to you.

On the Substack app

Translated notes are available on the Substack iOS and Android apps. Follow the steps below for the Substack iOS app.

  1. Go to the notes feed on your Home or a creator’s profile (look for the Notes tab). You may see a note that’s been automatically translated into your device’s preferred language. If you’d like to read a note in your device’s preferred language, tap on “Translate”.

Maduca shares notes in Portuguese on Substack

  1. Tap “Translated” to give feedback, adjust your translation settings, or change the note back to its original language.

  2. To stop notes from being automatically translated, tap “Translation settings” and slide the toggle next to “Automatically translate”. To stop a specific language from being automatically translated, tap “Never translate” and choose a language from the list.

How do I translate a post?

To read a translated post on web:

  • Click any post in your Subscriptions inbox or click a post shared in a note.
  • The post will open in the Substack reader and translate automatically if you have automatic translation enabled.
  • To adjust translation settings, give feedback, or view the original language, click “Translated” and choose the desired option. You can also translate a post to your device’s preferred language on the Substack iOS and Android app.

How can I change my device’s language settings?

  • On Windows: Follow these steps here.
  • On Macbook: Go to the Apple Menu > System Settings. Select General in the sidebar, then click Language & Region. In the list of languages, drag your preferred language to the top to make it the primary one.
  • On Chrome: Chrome usually follows your computer’s system language automatically. For Windows, follow these steps here.
  • On iOS: Go to Settings > General > Language & Region. Tap Add Language, pick your choice, and set it as your primary language.
  • On Android: Go to Settings > System > Languages & input > Languages. Tap Add a language and select the one you want to use.

How do I update my Substack profile?

When logged into your Substack account on the web, click on your profile picture. Select “Edit Profile”. You can also update your profile on the Substack app by clicking on your profile picture and tapping “Edit”.

Your profile will display:

  • Your photo and bio
  • Your profile theme
  • Any social links you’ve connected
  • What publications you write for
  • What publications you read
  • Any Substack posts you’ve published, notes you’ve posted, and posts and notes that you’ve liked
  • A subset of your subscribers (if you have a Substack publication) or your followers (people who can follow your profile)

How do I make changes to my Substack profile?

To make changes on the web, head to your profile and click on “Edit profile”.

  • To change your photo on substack.com: Click the current profile image and select a new image from your device.
  • To show or hide what publications you read on substack.com: Scroll to the “Subscription Privacy” section and adjust the toggles next to the Substacks you want to display or hide from view on your profile.
  • Activity privacy: If enabled, your profile may appear in the Likes list on a post and posts and notes you’ve liked will appear on the Likes tab on your profile. To disable your likes from appearing, toggle this setting to off. To disable your paid subscriber badge from appearing on your profile, click on the toggle to the left. This will immediately hide the paid subscriber badge from your profile. Learn more here on how to hide the subscriber count on your Substack profile.

Note: Need to change your email address? Find steps here on how to make that update.

How do the top publication lists on Substack’s homepage work?

“Top paid” are ranked according to the number of paying subscribers a publication has.

“All” refers to all the publications in that category.

How long can I keep my Substack subdomain or handle?

Writers can keep a Substack subdomain or handle indefinitely so long as their publication or profile remains active.

Can a Substack subdomain or handle be given to someone else?

If a publication or profile has been inactive for a significant period, we may reassign the corresponding subdomain or handle.

We may reassign a subdomain or handle at any time at our discretion if we believe that its use poses a security risk to our platform, employees, or users.

Please note that we may decide to reassign a subdomain or handle without a holder’s explicit permission if we believe the holder is exploiting the subdomain with no intention to become an active platform participant or publisher.

How can I keep my Substack subdomain or handle?

In order to keep any subdomain or handle, activity on your publication should be authentic. We reserve the right to use our discretion when evaluating whether content has been published for the sole purpose of domain squatting.

Note: At this time, we are unable to accept any requests to take over or claim a Substack subdomain or handle that’s already been taken by another user.

How much does Substack cost?

For readers:

Subscription plans, such as monthly and annual, are set by creators. Substack won’t charge you any additional fees and we accept most major credit cards such as Visa, MasterCard, Discover, and American Express.

Readers in Europe may see the option to pay using direct debit and non-credit card methods such as iDEAL, Bancontact, and Sofort.

For creators:

Publishing is free on Substack– no matter how many subscribers you have!

If you enable paid subscriptions on your publications, the following fees will apply:

From Substack:

10% of each transaction

From Stripe:

Credit cards

A credit card fee (2.9% + $0.30 per transaction fee) and a Billing fee for recurring subscriptions (0.7% for recurring payments as of July 2024).

On your Stripe account, click on the Transactions tab and select any “Succeeded” line item. Scroll to the Payment breakdown and here, you’ll see Substack’s cut and Stripe’s fees (the Billing fee is not included in the “Stripe processing fee” amount).

To see Stripe’s Billing fee, either click on “All Activity” on the Transactions tab and you’ll see a line item labeled “Stripe fee”. Alternatively, head to the Balances tab and select “Payouts”. Click on a payout and scroll to Summary. You’ll see the amount Stripe has deducted for it’s Billing fee under “Stripe fee”.

Note: Existing creators who have created a Substack and enabled payments on their publication prior to July 10, 2024 will keep the previous 0.5% billing fee pricing until June 30, 2025. If you’ve enabled payments on your Substack publication and have connected to Stripe prior to July 10, 2024, you should receive an email from Stripe about the billing fee change.

Non-credit card methods

New subscribers can pay for their subscription using alternative methods such as direct debit or paying directly via a bank’s payment portal. Stripe’s Billing fee will also apply for non-credit card methods.

Stripe fees for these payment methods will apply. The fees are dependent on the country where you registered your Stripe account in.

The following fees below are an example for a US Stripe account:

  • iDEAL: 80¢
  • Bancontact: 1.4% + 30¢
  • Sofort: 1.4% + 30¢
  • SEPA Direct: 0.8% + 30¢ capped at $6.00. Stripe charges $10.00 for failed or disputed SEPA Direct Debit payments.

The Substack app is free and designed to read your favorite creators in one dedicated space.

Many creators have free posts or free previews of paid posts and the only cost on Substack is if you’d like to read premium content and become a paid subscriber to a publication.

How to connect Substack to your AI Assistant

The Substack MCP server connects your publication to AI assistants like Claude and ChatGPT. Once connected, your assistant can read your publication data such as subscriber counts, post performance, revenue, and retention, directly in a chat, without you opening Substack or exporting a file.

Before you start

  • You must be an Admin on the publication you want to connect.
  • Your publication must be a Bestseller publication.
  • Your AI client must support MCP (Model Context Protocol) connectors. Claude, ChatGPT, and Cursor all support this. The Substack MCP server URL is mcp.substack.com

You can add this URL as a custom connector in any MCP-compatible client. The steps below cover Claude.

Connect Claude to Substack

  • Open Claude desktop or web and click Customize in the left dashboard.
  • Click Connectors, then Add custom connector.
  • Enter mcp.substack.com in the MCP Server URL field.
  • Click Add.
  • Sign in to your Substack account when prompted.
  • Click Allow access when Claude requests permission to read your publication data. If you have multiple publications on your Substack account, select the Bestseller publication.
  • Return to Claude and start a new chat about your publication. Note: If you try to connect an ineligible Substack publication, you’ll see an error message stating so. If you’re already signed into Substack, ensure that you’re on the account that’s linked to your Bestseller publication before connecting.

What you can ask

Once connected, you can ask your AI assistant questions about your publication directly, without opening Substack or exporting data. The assistant fetches the data through the MCP server in real time.

Examples of things you can ask:

  • “What’s my current subscriber count and revenue?”
  • “How is my paid subscriber retention trending after 6 months?”
  • “Which traffic source drove the most views on my last post?”
  • “How many free subscribers have I gained since January?”

What data the Substack MCP server can access

The server provides read-only access to publication data such as dashboard metrics, traffic data, and publication settings. It cannot publish posts, send Notes, or modify your account.

The server cannot access profile data or Notes activity. If you ask the AI assistant for stats on your Notes activity, it will not be able to return them.

How to contact Substack Support

At Substack, we’re building a world-class support system that makes it easy for creators and subscribers to find quality answers from various channels. We’re focused on ensuring creators have the tools they need to make money from the work they believe in.

Before reaching out, we recommend searching the Substack Help Center. If you still need assistance, here are the best ways to contact Support.

  1. Sign into your Substack account

Signing in is the quickest way for us to verify your identity, surface your account details, and resolve your inquiry. If you can’t sign in, don’t worry—you can still reach out using the options below.

  1. Chat with the Substack Support bot

For the fastest response, start with the chatbot, which resolves about 90% of issues.

Why we use chatbot support:

  • When you have limited time with an urgent product question or need to refund a subscriber, the chatbot can help instantly.
  • The Substack Support chatbot is powered by everything it has learned since day one—from Help Center articles, blog posts, and the latest product updates. Ask it anything and these resources can help you get unstuck.
  1. Escalation to a human voice

If the bot can’t answer your question, it will automatically create an email ticket for our in-house Support team. Our experienced team (Betty, Brock, Cantrell, Lena, Mac, Sylvia, and more) has worked closely with Substack creators for years.

User Type

I’m having trouble loading the website. What should I do?

If you’re having trouble loading the website, it might be due to:

  • Internet connectivity issues on your computer. Click here to test your connection.
  • A local firewall blocking our website. Make sure to whitelist “substack.com” (without quotes) in your firewall configuration.
  • Substack might be experiencing service issues. You can always check the status of our platform at substack.statuspage.io.

What are Reply Rules on Substack?

Reply Rules automatically hide comments and note replies that break your community standards — without deleting them or notifying the commenter. Available on the web version of your Substack and the Substack app.

What are Reply rules?

Reply rules let you describe your community standards and have them enforced automatically on post comments and note replies. Comments that don’t meet your standards are hidden — not deleted — and can be reviewed or restored at any time.

If you’ve previously moderated post comments or note replies, the automatic moderation feature i.e. hiding certain replies is automatically enabled on your publication. An example of moderating a post comment or note reply can be deleting it and then submitting feedback like “Spam” or “Slop” when prompted.

How Reply rules works

When a comment or note reply doesn’t meet your standards, Substack hides it without notifying you or the commenter. Hidden comments appear behind a “Replies hidden” label and are never permanently deleted unless you manually delete the comment yourself.

Note: If you see a comment that wasn’t hidden and it violates your Reply Rules, we recommend selecting “Delete”. If you notice a comment that was hidden and you don’t think it violates your Reply Rules, we recommend clicking “Unhide”. The Reply Rules feature will learn and adapt to what you hide, unhide, or delete and be less likely to make the same mistake in the future.

When a reader comments on your post, they can expand your Reply Rules in the comment text box before posting.

How to set up Reply rules

Reply rules can be set directly on your publication or on the Substack app. On web, follow these steps:

  • Go to your publication’s Settings page.
  • Scroll to the Community section. Click the toggle next to “Enable automatic moderation”.
  • In the Reply rules textbox, enter your community standards and click “Save”. To disable certain replies from being automatically hidden, slide the toggle to the left next to “Enable automatic moderation”. You can still show your readers your Reply Rules. If you want to remove your Reply Rules, delete the text in the textbox and click “Save”.

How to moderate hidden comments

You can manually unhide a post comment by clicking the three dots next to it and select “Unhide.” To permanently remove it instead, click the three dots and select “Delete.”

If you’d like to see a list of hidden comments on your Substack, head to Moderation section on your publication: your.substack.com

Click on the “Hidden” tab and for each comment in that section, you can either unhide the comment and make it visible on that post, remove the comment which permanently deletes it, ban the Substack user who left the comment, or see all the hidden comments for that particular post.

Who is excluded from Reply rules?

The following community members will not be filtered:

  • Paid subscribers and accounts you follow on Substack (unless you’ve previously deleted their comments).
  • Contributors on your Substack.

What are Substack Handles?

Substack Handles are unique identifiers that can appear on many surfaces such as your Substack profile or Notes. Handles are unique in that they’re selected by you which makes it easier for mentions and profile sharing.

If you have a publication, your Substack subdomain will be reserved as a handle for you. If you do not have a publication, you’ll see a suggested handle. You can always choose a different handle.

To claim or change your Substack handle, follow these steps:

  1. Head to your Substack account Settings page and select “Claim handle” next to Handle.

  2. If you have a publication, you’ll see your Substack subdomain reserved as a handle. If you’d like to change your handle, enter a new username in the text box and click “Save”.

  3. Once your Substack handle has changed or been claimed, your Substack profile URL will automatically change as well.

Things to note:

  • If a handle you’ve entered is unavailable, a “handle is already taken” message will appear. At this time, we are unable to accept any requests to take over or claim a Substack subdomain or handle that’s already been taken by another user.
  • If you change your handle, your old handle will be free for any user to claim. However, your subdomain handle will always be reserved for you to claim if you’d like to revert back to that handle.
  • With Substack handles, your profile has changed from substack.com to substack.com.

What happens if I delete the Substack app?

Deleting the app will remove the app from your device but will not close your Substack account nor unsubscribe you from publications.

If you delete the Substack app, your email newsletters will automatically resume for publications you’re subscribed to.

To close your Substack account, please follow these steps.

What is Substack’s refund policy?

Publisher Refunds

Substack Publishers may issue refunds at any time, at their discretion, as permitted by the platform’s functionality and Stripe, our payment processor.

Publishers can find instructions for canceling subscriptions and issuing refunds here.

Subscriber Refunds

Refunds are ultimately at the discretion of the Publisher, and refund requests should be made to them directly. To contact a publisher, follow these steps: How do I contact a creator on Substack?

To support Publishers in managing requests, Substack will honor refund requests according to (1), (2), (3) of our Refund Policy below. To submit a request to Substack, contact Support here.

  1. We will issue a refund if a request is submitted to Substack within 7 days of payment, unless a subscriber perk has been claimed or a publisher has marked the subscription as non-refundable.

Requests submitted more than 7 days from the payment date will be left to the publisher’s discretion. If one subscriber has submitted more than 3 refund requests, further requests may also be left to the publisher’s discretion.

  1. We will issue a refund if a publication is dormant.

A dormant publication is one that has been inactive for at least 6 months for yearly subscribers or at least 1 month (from the date of payment) for monthly subscribers. Inactivity means the publication has not published any posts in that time.

  1. We may issue a refund for payment processing errors, such as duplicate charges.

Duplicate charges must have been made under the same account at the same time and must have been caused by a payment processing error. Charges made under different email addresses will be left to the creator’s discretion.

Note: Refunds may take up to 10 business days to appear on your bank or credit card statement depending on your bank.

To learn more, please read our Terms of Use.

Refunds for subscriptions made on the Substack iOS app

Readers may be able to pay for subscriptions to select publications directly on the Substack iOS app.

These subscriptions are purchased directly through Apple’s App Store, and refund requests are up to Apple’s discretion. To request a refund, follow these steps: Request a refund for apps or content that you bought from Apple

Where can I find the Substack app?

  1. Open the App Store Play and search for “Substack”.

  2. Tap the app and “Get” on iOS or “Install” on Android.

  3. Once installed, open the app.

Tip: Substack will request permissions for push notifications and this is optional.

What can I do if the download stalls or fails?

First, ensure that you have a strong internet connection. If connected to Wifi, turn off and on your Wifi on your phone.

A download may fail if your device is full. Head to your device Settings and check that there’s enough free storage.

Which web browsers does Substack recommend using?

To ensure the best possible experience on Substack, we recommend using the most recent version of one of the browsers listed below. Newer versions offer better performance, security, and compatibility with all of Substack’s features.

  • Google Chrome
  • Mozilla Firefox
  • Safari
  • Microsoft Edge

Why is Substack asking to verify my age?

New regulations in some regions are requiring online platforms to implement age verification for their users.

  • Learn how the UK Online Safety Act may impact content here.
  • Learn how the Australia Online Safety Act may impact content here. Because of these laws, you may encounter restricted content and be prompted to verify your age on Substack.

In this guide, we’ll walk you through the age verification process so that you can unlock the full Substack experience.

Note: Subscribers with a credit card associated with their Substack account will be automatically verified, and do not need to complete the Persona verification process.

  • Who does this apply to
  • What do I need
  • What happens after I verify my age

Who does this apply to

If you reside in the UK or Australia, you may potentially encounter content that appears blurred or blocked from a Substack you subscribe to, on Substack.com, or on the Substack app. These include but are not limited to:

  • DMs on Substack
  • Comments on a post
  • Notes on your Home feed The UK Online Safety Act and the Australian Online Safety Act requires restricted access to content that could be considered sensitive for younger audiences. If you see blurred or blocked content, this doesn’t necessarily mean that the content is harmful, it just may fall into a category that must be age-restricted per the requirements of the OSA.

Verification in the UK is optional, however, without verification, you may continue to come across blurred content or be blocked from accessing certain features such as DMs and livestreams with a prompt to verify your age thus limiting your Substack experience.

Note: Australian users who are not verified as being over 16 cannot hold a Substack account.

What do I need

The selfie verification process is quick and secure and takes only a few minutes. Powered by Persona, the leading secure identity verification platform, you’ll need a stable internet connection and enough time to complete the process. If you exit the verification process, it won’t save your spot and you’ll need to start from the beginning. We also recommend having a government issued ID on hand in case the selfie verification fails.

On desktop

  1. When prompted, click on “Begin verifying”.

  2. Enable camera permissions to Persona for your device and/or browser.

  3. Follow the steps on the screen and Persona will begin verifying your age immediately.

  4. You’ll see a confirmation screen if your age is successfully verified by Persona.

Note: If you’re seeing a “Can’t access your camera error” during the selfie verification process, follow these troubleshooting steps on Persona’s support article.

What happens if the selfie verification fails?

If you’ve made several attempts at the selfie verification and are unable to submit a valid selfie, you’ll have the option to upload a government issued ID such as a driving licence or passport. Click on “Verify with Gov ID” and from the drop-down menu, select which country your government ID is from.

You’ll then be asked to take a clear photo of your government issued ID using your device’s camera. The age verification process will begin immediately.

Prefer to verify your age at a later time? Head to your Substack account settings page and scroll to the “Age Verification” section. Here, you’ll see the status of your age verification status and options on how to start the verification flow.

What happens after I verify my age

Once you’ve successfully verified your age as an adult on Substack, you’ll immediately be able to view any previously restricted content blocked by the UK Online Safety Act or the Australian Online Safety Act, on Substack.com or on the Substack app. You will not be prompted to verify your age again in the future.

Frequently asked questions

I use a card to pay for my subscriptions. Why am I still being asked to verify my age?

If you’re using a card to pay for Substack subscriptions and are still being asked to verify your age, confirm that you’re using a credit card. Subscribers with a credit card associated with their Substack account will be automatically verified while subscribers using a debit card may need to verify their age in the UK. To update your payment method on Substack, follow these steps here.

What information does Substack retain?

Substack retains an estimate of your age following the verification process.

What if I don’t have a Substack account? Do I still need to verify my age to view content blocked by the UK Online Safety Act or the Australian Online Safety Act?

If you don’t have a Substack account, you won’t be able to verify your age in order to view content blocked by the UK Online Safety Act or the Australian Online Safety Act. Some content will remain hidden.

What happens if I’m unable to verify my age?

If you’ve made several unsuccessful attempts to verify your age using the selfie method, you may see the option to verify your age with a government issued ID.

If you’re unable to successfully complete age verification with a government issued ID, our team will review your verification request.

What if I encounter content on Substack that I think is not part of the Online Safety Act?

We’re continuing to refine the labelling system, and we appreciate any feedback that helps guide that work. If you find that content you subscribe to, may have been mislabelled as part of UK Online Safety Act or the Australian Online Safety Act, you can request a manual review by reaching out to the Standards & Enforcement team. They will evaluate the content according to our 18+ Content Policy Guidelines or the Australian Online Safety Act, and if they determine that the content was mislabelled, they can manually update or remove the label as appropriate.

Will I still get emails if I have the Substack app?

On the Substack iOS and Android app, you can choose to receive push notifications about new posts, receive email newsletters, or receive notifications about new posts both via push and email.

If you’re not receiving email newsletters, it’s possible that when you downloaded the Substack app, you selected “Smart notifications”. With smart notifications, you’ll primarily receive push notifications about new posts in the app and may receive email notifications about posts you’ve missed if you haven’t opened the Substack app in a while.

How can I resume email deliveries of new posts from Substacks I’m subscribed to?

If you’ve stopped receiving emails since downloading the Substack app and would like to resume email delivery, you can change your settings on your desktop or directly on the app itself.

On Android

Tap your profile picture and select Settings.

On Settings, tap Notifications and scroll to the Newsletter Delivery section. Tap “Prefer email”. New posts will now be delivered via email and can be read directly in your email provider or in Inbox on the Substack app.

On iOS

Tap your profile picture and select Settings.

On Settings, tap Notifications and scroll to the Newsletter Delivery section. Tap “Prefer email”. New posts will now be delivered via email and can be read directly in your email provider or in Inbox on the Substack app.

On desktop

Head to your Substack account settings page and scroll to the Notifications section.

Under Newsletter delivery, select “Prefer email delivery”.

Tips for any device:

  • If you’d prefer to receive both email newsletters and push notifications, tap the “Both email and push” option on the app or “Both email and app” on desktop.
  • If you’d prefer to only receive push notifications from the app, tap “Prefer push” on the app or “Prefer app delivery” on desktop. If app delivery fails, posts may resume sending via email.

General

Can I edit my comment on a Substack post?

Yes– if you’ve left a comment on a post and noticed a typo or want to change what you wrote, you can edit the comment.

On web:

  1. Select the three dots underneath your comment and select “Edit”.

  2. Once you make your change, an edit status with a time stamp will appear next to your name on the comment.

On the iOS app:

  1. On the iOS app, tap the three dots below your comment and select “Edit”.

Note: Edits can only be made shortly after posting a comment. If you do not see the “edit comment” option, the window to make changes has passed. At this time, editing comments on a post on the Substack Android app is not available.

Can I pledge a Substack?

If you’d like to show your support for a Substack but the writer hasn’t enabled paid subscriptions, you can pledge a future subscription.

Once you click on a Subscribe button and enter your email address, you’ll see the pledge options: yearly, monthly, or founding member. To continue without pledging, select “No pledge”.

Note: If you don’t see any pledge options, the writer may not have enabled this feature on their Substack.

Select a plan and click on “Pledge a subscription”. On the next page, you’ll be able to write a message to the writer of the publication about why you’re pledging a future subscription.

After pledging a future paid subscription, you’ll receive a confirmation email about how to manage your pledge. Your card will not be charged and your paid subscription will not begin until the Substack turns on paid subscriptions.

How do I cancel a Pledge?

If you’ve pledged a paid subscription to a Substack and want to cancel, follow these steps:

  • Sign into your Substack account and click on the hamburger menu.
  • From the drop-down menu, select Settings.

  1. Under Subscriptions, click on the publication where you want to cancel your pledge.

  2. This will land you on your account page for that Substack. Scroll to the Account actions section and click on, “To cancel your pledge, click here”.

You’ll still be subscribed as a free subscriber and will not be charged if the writer turns on paid subscriptions.

What happens if the writer enables paid subscriptions but they’re higher than what I pledged?

If a writer enables payments and the subscription plans are higher than the amount you pledged, then we’ll notify you via email about the price change.

For example, if you pledged at $8.00/month but the writer enabled a $10.00/month subscription plan, your pledge won’t automatically turn into a $10.00/month subscription.

You’ll have the option to become a paid subscriber at the higher rate by clicking on “Subscribe” in the notification email and then you’ll immediately become a paid subscriber.

Can I see one section on its own on the Substack app?

At this time, sections are not separated on the Substack app. When viewing a publication, you’ll see every post from that publication. You can view an individual section of a publication on the web version of a publication.

If you’d like to only receive notifications about a particular section, head to your Manage Subscription page for that publication on the web version and make your selection.

Tip: If you have notifications off for a section, these posts will not appear in your Inbox.

How do I cancel my paid subscription on Substack?

If you’d like to cancel a paid subscription to a publication, follow these steps:

  1. Navigate to your Substack account Settings page at this link: www.substack.com/settings

  2. Under Subscriptions, click on the paid publication you want to cancel your subscription to.

  3. Scroll to the Account actions section and click on the link next to “To cancel your paid subscription, click here”.

  4. On the next page, select “Cancel Subscription”.

Once you cancel your subscription, it will terminate at the end of the billing period and you will not be charged again. If you’re on a 7-day trial, the trial will end as soon as you cancel the subscription.

Note: Your paid subscriber benefits will continue until the end of the time period you’ve paid for. Once your paid subscription ends, you’ll become a free subscriber. To completely unsubscribe, turn off notifications for that publication by following these steps.

Can I pause my paid subscription instead?

Yes! If you’d like to pause your subscription, click “Pause Subscription” and select from the drop-down box how many months you’d like charges to be stopped for.

Your subscription to the publication will be paused immediately. We’ll display a date when your subscription will resume as well as send you a reminder email three days before your subscription will be unpaused.

How do I change my email address on Substack?

Signed up for one or more subscriptions with the wrong email address or want to transfer your existing subscriptions to a different Substack user?

To change your email address on your Substack account, you’ll need to log into the web version of Substack.com and access your account Settings page (https://substack.com/settings). At this time, changing your email address on the Substack app is not an option.

Select “Edit” next to your email address and enter the new desired new email address. You’ll then receive emails from Substack to your current email address and your new email address asking you to confirm the change. You will be logged out of your Substack account on the web and the Substack app and need to sign in with your new email address.

Note: If you enter an email address for an existing Substack account, note that all your subscriptions, publications, and other data will be transferred to that account. You’ll still need to confirm the email change request in the emails that’ll be sent to your current email address and the email address you’d like to change your account to.

How do I change my subscription plan on Substack?

Subscribed to a publication and want to change your plan from monthly to annual or annual to a founding member? Follow these quick steps on how to do that directly on your Substack account.

  1. Navigate to your Substack account Settings page: substack.com

  2. Under Subscriptions, click on the paid publication you want to update.

  3. Click “Change” next to Plan in the Subscription section.

  4. Choose from the following plans you’d like to switch to and select “Change plan”.

Note: If you want to cancel your paid subscription, follow these steps here.

What happens if I upgrade my subscription plan from annual to founding member?

If you upgrade your subscription plan from annual to founding member, you’ll be charged a prorated amount, and your renewal date will change to the date you made the upgrade. You’ll receive a payment receipt email showing the pro-rated amount. The renewal date will not stay the same as the original date for the annual plan. For example, if it’s January 8th, 2024, and you change your plan to founding member, your next renewal date will be January 8th, 2025.

This scenario shows what could happen if you upgrade your annual plan ($100) to a founding member ($200):
