  • If you purchase an annual plan on January 1st and immediately upgrade to a founding membership, you’ll be charged an additional $100.00 to upgrade.
  • If you upgrade on July 1, you’ll be charged $150.00.
  • If you upgrade on December 31, you’ll be charged $200.00.

How do I contact a creator on Substack?

If you’re looking to reach the creator of a Substack publication, you may do so by responding to a newsletter via email. This is the most common way subscribers communicate with creators. If you’ve received a newsletter from them, hit reply and type your message.

You can also send them a message via email at @substack.com (replace subdomain with the name of their Substack publication).

If there’s no reply option i.e., the creator has replies to their Substack email turned off or the creator hasn’t provided any contact information on their About page, we unfortunately don’t have a way to facilitate direct contact.

You can also try direct messaging a Substack creator or reader by direct messaging them. Click on their profile and select “Message” or head to your Chat tab and search for the person you’d like to contact. If the “Message” button is missing on their profile or they don’t appear in the Chat search, it’s possible they have disabled anyone from messaging them on Substack.

How do I create a password on Substack?

To create a password on Substack, follow these steps:

  1. Head to substack.com

  2. Click “Sign in with password”

  3. Select “Set a new password”

  4. Enter your email address and click “Send Email”

What happens next?

You’ll receive a password reset link to your inbox and can use that email/password combination to sign in to any of your Substack subscriptions.

Tip: In order to see the “Set a new password” page, you will need to be signed out of your Substack account. Click on your Substack profile icon and select “Sign out” from the menu. Once you’re signed out, head to substack.com to set a new password for the first time or reset your Substack password.

How do I donate a subscription?

To donate a subscription, navigate to subdomain.substack.com (replace “subdomain” with the name of the Substack publication) and enter the requested details.

Alternatively, if you have a specific person in mind, you can give a gift subscription.

How do I find a Substack publication?

Searching for a creator or Substack? Check out the Explore page and search for new writers based on categories you’re interested in.

If you’re looking for a specific creator, publication, post, or topic, use the search bar at the top of the page.

How do I get an invoice for my subscription?

Need a detailed receipt of your paid subscription to your favorite Substack newsletter?

  1. Log into your Substack account and head to your account Settings.

  2. Scroll down to Subscriptions and click on the publication you’d like an invoice for.

  3. In the payment information section, select “View” next to Billing history.

  4. On the Payment & receipt history page, select the hamburger menu next to the subscription you’d like an invoice for. From the drop-down menu, select “View invoice.”

  5. A new browser tab will open which will show your subscription details from Stripe (our payment processing partner). Select “Download invoice”.

  6. A PDF version of your invoice will be downloaded to your device.

How do I add billing details to my subscription invoices?

If you need billing details such as a company name, address, or Tax ID on your subscription invoices, head to the “Payment Information” section on the subscription management page and select “Edit” next to Payment method.

On the billing details page, enter a billing email, name/company name, or billing address. Select from the drop-down menu various types of tax categories such as European VAT or US EIN and enter the corresponding information.

Click Save and your billing information will appear on any invoices which can be downloaded following the steps above.

Note: If the publication has enabled Stripe Tax, then tax will be collected at the time you select a paid subscription plan (monthly, annual, or founder). The tax amount will appear as a line item on your invoice.

How do I get my emails out of the spam folder?

Email providers assign our emails to spam based on the sender and content, among other signals.

We recommend:

  • If the newsletter is in the Spam or Promotions folder, make sure to “mark as not spam” in your email client and move it to your Inbox.
  • Add the publication’s Substack email address to your contact list. All posts will be sent from this address: [subdomain]@substack.com. Replace ‘subdomain’ with the name of the publication.
  • Send an email to the publication’s Substack email address. These steps can help your email client recognize newsletters from publications you’re subscribed to as not spam.

How do I give a paid subscription as a gift on Substack?

To gift a paid subscription to a Substack publication, navigate to subdomain.substack.com (replace “subdomain” with the publication name) and enter the requested details.

If you’d like to send the gift subscription at a later time, check the box next to “Schedule for later” and select your preferred date from the date picker.

What happens next?

Once you purchase a gift subscription, you’ll receive a payment receipt confirming your purchase.

The gift recipient will receive an email and be able to access paid content immediately. If they don’t have a Substack account, one will be created for them automatically, and they can sign into the Substack publication to start reading.

Once the subscription is ending, the gift recipient will receive reminder emails where they’ll have the opportunity to purchase a paid subscription plan or subscribe as a free reader.

How do I hide posts or notes that I liked on my Substack profile?

When you create a Substack profile, by default, posts and notes that you like will appear on your profile.

Tip: Posts you’ve liked may not appear if the post is on an invite-only publication or if you’ve chosen not to display the publication on your Substack profile.

How do I make my likes private?

On the web

  1. Sign into your Substack account and click on your profile picture. Select Settings.

  2. Scroll down to the Privacy section and slide the toggle to the left next to “Show likes on profile” . A grey toggle means that posts and notes you’ve liked will not be displayed on your Substack profile and your profile won’t appear on the Likes list on a post.

On the Substack iOS app

  1. Tap on your profile picture and then tap on the Settings icon at the bottom of the screen.

  2. Scroll to the Privacy & Safety section.

  3. In the Privacy section, tap the toggle next to “Show likes on profile” to disable this function.

  4. Moving forward, posts or notes that you’ve liked will not appear on the Likes tab on your Substack profile.

How do I listen to a Substack post?

(For listeners)

With text-to-speech, you can listen to posts in the Substack app in just two steps.

Note: Text-to-speech is available on most but not on all publications.

  1. Open the Substack App and tap on the play icon at the top right of your screen on a post.

  2. The audio will automatically start playing in the app player.

Tips for text-to-speech:

  • Just like a podcast episode or voiceover, the audio will play in the background unless you tap pause or fully exit/fully close the app i.e. remove it from your device’s menu of open apps.
  • If a Substack doesn’t have text-to-speech available or on some older posts, you may see a message about how the post doesn’t have audio when you tap the play icon. Tip: Text-to-speech is only available on the Substack App and posts that are written in English.

(For creators)

If your publication has the text-to-speech feature (this feature is enabled on select Substacks), you can select from a variety of voices that listeners will hear when your post is being read aloud.

Head to your publication’s Settings and scroll to the Basics section. Under “Read-aloud voice”, select from a variety of voices.

How do I listen to paid episodes on Spotify?

If you’re a paying subscriber to a Substack podcast, you can now listen to both free and paid episodes on Spotify. For a more convenient listening experience, learn how to link your Substack and Spotify accounts to listen to access paid episodes on Spotify.

You can link your Substack account to Spotify:

  • From your Substack account
  • Directly from Spotify Tip: Once you link your Substack account to Spotify, you will get access to all your paid Substack podcasts in Spotify. Linking these accounts will also automatically follow and save any subscribed podcasts in Spotify.

From your Substack account

  1. Click on your profile picture and select Manage Subscription from the drop-down menu. Alternatively, you can access a complete list of your Substack subscriptions at substack.com.

  2. Scroll to the Private podcast feeds and click on Set up podcast. On the manage subscription page, you can change your plan notification settings and edit your profile.

  3. Click Listen on Spotify. Linking your Spotify account does not mean adding a private RSS feed.

Tip: If you don’t see the Listen on Spotify button, the Substack publication hasn’t synced to Spotify yet. You can still listen to free episodes by following the show on Spotify or the Substack app, or add your private RSS feed to Apple Podcasts and listen to paid episodes there.

  1. A new browser will open where you can click Link Account. You can connect your accounts to start accessing paid episodes.

  2. Enter your Spotify login details and click Log in. Substack will not have access to your Spotify login details. If you’re having trouble logging in, we recommend contacting Spotify.

  3. Click on the Substack podcast which will open your Spotify player

  4. Paid episodes are now unlocked and you can listen to current and past episodes on Spotify.

From the Spotify app

  1. Click Get access on a paid episode. A lock icon on an episode indicates that it’s for paying subscribers only.

  2. Select Link account.

  3. Sign in to your Substack account and click Subscribe. If you’re already a paying subscriber, you can skip to Step 5.

  4. Select a monthly, annual, or founding member plan.

  5. After subscribing to a paid plan or signing into your Substack account, enter your Spotify login details.

  6. Review the Spotify access details and click Agree.

  7. Click on the podcast and the show will open on the Spotify app.

  8. Paid episodes are now unlocked, and you can listen to current and past episodes on Spotify.

What happens if I cancel my Substack subscription?

Your paid subscriber benefits will continue until the end of the time period you’ve paid for, so you can still listen to paid episodes on Spotify. Once your paid subscription ends, you’ll become a free subscriber, and paid episodes will be locked.

How can I unlink my Substack account from Spotify?

Head to content-access.spotify.com and click “Unlink” next to Substack.

Once you unlink your Substack account from Spotify, you’ll no longer have access to paid subscriber only episodes.

How do I log into my Substack account?

Log into your Substack account either on Substack’s homepage or through your favorite publication.

  1. Navigate to the Sign in button in the top right corner of the page or head directly to this link: substack.com

  2. Select whether you’d like a verification code emailed to you or log in using your password.

  3. If you select the email option, find the email from Substack in your inbox and copy and paste the code onto Substack. You can also log in by selecting the login verification link in that same email.

Tip: Haven’t set up a password yet? Follow these quick steps to get you started.

If you’re not receiving the sign in emails from Substack, here are some steps to troubleshoot:

  1. Check your spam or junk folder for the email.

  2. Ensure you’re entering the correct email address on the Substack sign on page.

  3. Add no-reply@substack.com to your email contacts list.

  4. If using Gmail, check the Promotions tab. Email providers like yahoo.com, verizon.net, and aol.com may be experiencing some delays. If you use these, contact your provider for more information.

How do I redeem a free trial on Substack?

Want to redeem a 7-day free trial on Substack and access a paid post? With a free trial, you’ll be able to unlock paid posts on the web and app version of that particular Substack publication. At this time, trial access to all paid posts on Substack is not available. However, if there are multiple publications you’d like to read and they each offer a free trial, you can redeem as much trials as you’d like at one time– you just cannot redeem a trial to a publication more than once under the same Substack account.

When redeeming a free trial subscription, you must enter your payment details to complete the signup process.

Once the free trial is over to that publication, you’ll be automatically charged for the subscription. You can choose to cancel your free trial before the billing date.

How do I report a comment to publication authors and admins?

See a comment on a post that doesn’t follow a publication’s rules or norms? Report the comment and the author of the publication will see your report if they have reporting features enabled.

  1. Click on the three dots next to the comment and select “Report”.

  2. Describe why you think this comment should be reviewed by publication admins.

  3. Once you submit the report, the author will be able to review the comment and decide whether to remove, ban the commenter, or approve the comment from the post.

How do I see a list of all my subscriptions on Substack?

Interested in seeing what Substacks you’re subscribed to in one place?

  1. Navigate to your account Settings page by clicking on this link: substack.com

  2. View all the Substacks you’re subscribed to under Subscriptions.

Tip: Need to unsubscribe from a publication? Learn how to unsubscribe from a free publication or cancel your paid subscription.

Alternatively, view a list of your free and paid subscriptions on desktop in your Library: substack.com

How do I subscribe to or unsubscribe from a section on Substack?

If you’re a new subscriber to a publication on Substack, you’ll receive all sections by default.

If you’re already a subscriber and the creator has created a new section or sections, you’ll need to subscribe to them in order to receive a new email newsletter or see a post in your app Inbox.

To subscribe to a section:

  1. Navigate to your account Settings page via www.substack.com/settings. Or when logged into your Substack account, select “Settings” from the drop-down menu.

  2. Under Subscriptions, click on the publication that has sections you’d like to subscribe to.

  3. On the next page, slide the toggle next to each section you want to receive notifications about.

To unsubscribe from a section:

  1. Navigate to your account Settings page via www.substack.com/settings and click on the publication you want to make changes to (as seen in steps above).

  2. Slide the toggle next to each section you’d like to stop receiving emails or app notifications from. A gray toggle indicates that notifications will be off for that section.

How do I unsubscribe from a free subscription?

Unsubscribe within an email newsletter

The easiest way to unsubscribe from a free publication is to scroll to the bottom of any email you’ve received and select “Unsubscribe”.

Once you click “Unsubscribe” a new window will open and you’ll need to select “Unsubscribe from all”.

Unsubscribe directly from your Substack account

  1. Navigate to your account Settings page via www.substack.com/settings or select your profile icon and select “Settings” from the drop-down menu.

  2. Under Subscriptions, click on the publication you’d like to unsubscribe from.

  3. On the next page, head to the Notifications section. Click on “Unsubscribe”.

Unsubscribe from a section

When you subscribe to a publication that has sections, you’ll automatically be subscribed to every section.

Follow these steps here on how to unsubscribe from a section.

Unsubscribe from all publications and Substack emails

If you’d like to unsubscribe from every Substack publication you’re subscribed to and also stop receiving Substack emails, you can do this in your Danger Zone section of your Settings page.

Note: Disabling all emails will completely unsubscribe you from a publication. This means you will not receive email newsletters, see posts in the Substack app, or see the publication under your Subscriptions.

How do I update my payment information on Substack?

Need to change your payment method for a paid subscription?

  1. Navigate to your Substack Account Settings by clicking on “More” at the bottom left of your home page. Select “Settings”. You can also go to your Account Settings using this link: substack.com

  2. Under Subscriptions, select the publication where you’d like to make a change.

  3. In the Payment information section, click on “Edit” next to Payment method.

  4. Enter your new card details and click Save.

Things to note:

  • At this time, there is no option to delete a payment method on Substack without replacing it with a new payment method, but you can reach out to the publication owner to have them delete your payment method from their Stripe account.
  • If you update the payment method on one subscription, your other paid subscriptions will also be updated to that payment method.
  • Publication owners cannot update credit card information on behalf of a subscriber. The subscriber must update it for themselves.

How do I upgrade from being a free subscriber to a paid subscriber?

If you’ve initially signed up as a free subscriber to a publication and would like to view paid posts, you’ll need to change your subscription plan.

To become a paid subscriber, follow these steps:

  1. Log into your Substack account at substack.com and click “Sign In”.

  2. Click on your profile icon and select Settings from the drop-down menu.

  3. Navigate to the Subscriptions section and click on “Edit” next to the publication you’d like to make changes on.

  4. On the Manage Subscription page for that Substack, click on “Upgrade to paid subscription”.

  5. Select which paid plan you’d like to subscribe to.

Note: If you’re a paid subscriber to at least one publication on Substack, you can subscribe with just one-click and will not need to re-enter your credit card details. If you’ve only been a free subscriber on Substack, you’ll be prompted to enter your credit card details.

How does following work on Substack?

For readers who want to find the best things written online today, following on Substack enables you to discover new creators in your Home feed via the people you trust and admire—whether it’s your contacts or other readers on the Substack network.

With the following, you can:

  • Find contacts: Sync phone contacts to see which of your friends are on Substack
  • Follow each other: Follow friends, other readers, and discover new creators
  • See activity: See profiles, notes, and reading activity such as likes from your follows
  • Invite friends: Invite your contacts to join Substack What is the difference between a follower and a subscriber?

When you follow a creator or reader, you’ll see their notes and some reading activity such as post likes in your Home feed. You won’t get emails from profiles you follow and you won’t get posts in your Inbox.

When you subscribe to a Substack publication, you share your email and receive their posts via email and/or the Substack app. If you’re a paying subscriber, you can view exclusive posts, notes, and chat threads that the creator wants to share just for paid readers.

If you’re a creator with a publication, your profile will display the options for readers to follow you or subscribe to your publication. If you’re a reader, your profile will have the “Follow” option.

How can I see who follows me and how many followers I have?

On your Substack profile, anyone can see your followers list to see who follows you and who you are following–this is public by default and cannot be hidden. You can also view your exact follower count on your profile which is only visible to you.

If you’re a creator with a Substack, we’ll also display a small list of your subscribers. The exact subscriber count will not be shown to readers and only an estimated count will be displayed (100+, 1K+, etc.).

Note: We only show your public subscriptions on your Substack profile. To edit your subscription privacy settings, follow these steps here.

How do I follow or unfollow someone on Substack?

If you see an interesting like or restack in your Home feed and want to see more from that person, you can click on the three-dot menu and select “Follow”.

To see more details about a creator or reader on Substack, head to their profile. On their profile page, you can click on “Follow”.

Tip: To unfollow a reader on Substack, click on the three dot menu and select “Unfollow ” and you’ll stop seeing their notes and reading activity. If you’re subscribed to a publication on Substack, you’ll automatically follow them. If you unsubscribe from the publication, you’ll also unfollow them.

How do I manage my Home feed and who appears there?

By default, you follow all the Substacks that you’re subscribed to. If you connect your contacts or connect your Facebook to your Substack account, you can follow your friends and discover new creators.

You can follow someone without subscribing to their Substack and likewise, you can unfollow someone without unsubscribing from their publication.

How do I know when I get a new follower on Substack?

When someone on Substack follows you, you’ll be notified by email and also see a notification in your Activity bell.

To stop receiving emails about new people who follow you on Substack, click “Unsubscribe” in the email itself or head to your Substack account Settings and click the toggle to the left next to “New followers”.

How do I remove a follower?

On your Substack profile, select “See followers”. On the Followers tab, click on the user that you’d like to remove.

On their Substack profile, click the three-dot hamburger menu and select “Block”. They’ll be removed from your followers list and you won’t be able to see their notes. They’ll also be blocked from subscribing to your publication.

Note: Substack has a system-imposed limit on the number of people a user can follow. This is designed to prevent spam and maintain the quality of interactions on the platform. If you reach this limit and need to follow new people, consider managing your current follows by unfollowing profiles that are no longer active or of interest.

I can’t use Stripe. Do you accept PayPal, crypto, clamshells, etc?

At this time, we do not accept any other forms of payment besides a credit card. Readers in Europe may see alternative methods of payment such as a direct debit option. If you’re using an Apple device, you may see the option to use Apple Pay.

We integrate with Stripe so our ability to accept a subscriber’s payment depends on what’s supported by Stripe.

I subscribed to a publication, but they haven’t published anything in awhile. What’s going on?

Sometimes, Substack publishers go on vacation, take a hiatus, or are otherwise unable to publish something new. Hopefully, they’ve informed you of their plans beforehand. If you haven’t received anything in awhile, you can always reach out to them by replying to the last email you received or send them an email to their Substack email address.

To get in touch with a creator, you can email them at [subdomain]@substack.com. Just replace ‘subdomain’ with the name of their publication. If there’s no reply option i.e., the creator has replies to their Substack email turned off or the creator hasn’t provided any contact information on their About page, we unfortunately don’t have a way to facilitate direct contact.

You can also try direct messaging a Substack creator or reader by direct messaging them. Click on their profile and select “Message” or head to your Chat tab and search for the person you’d like to contact. If the “Message” button is missing on their profile or they don’t appear in the Chat search, it’s possible they have disabled anyone from messaging them on Substack.

If you no longer want to subscribe to their publication, you can also cancel your subscription.

I subscribed to a Substack publication but am not receiving any emails. What can I do?

If you’ve signed up for a newsletter and haven’t received your first email or you’ve been receiving emails and suddenly they’ve stopped coming, don’t fret! Here are some troubleshooting tips to help you get emails again from your favorite Substack creators.

Tip: Posts can always be viewed on the web version of the publication or the Substack app.

Try testing your email address

To test whether your email delivery is working for a publication:

  1. Navigate to publicationname.substack.com

You’ll need to replace “publication name” with the name of the creator’s Substack publication.

For example, if a newsletter you subscribe to is “Skyline Trails”, enter the following URL:

skylinetrails.substack.com.

  1. Sign into the publication and select, “Send test email”.

If you see a “You have disabled email delivery for this account” message when testing the email delivery, this means that you’ve unsubscribed from the publication. To receive emails again, you’ll need to re-subscribe to the publication. You can do that by going to publicationname.substack.com

You’ll need to replace “publication name” with the name of the creator’s Substack publication.

Check your email settings

  • If you have Gmail, check your Promotions tab. If a newsletter is in that section, move it to your Primary inbox.
  • Make sure that your email address on file is correct.
  • Check your spam folder to see if emails have gone there. If so, make sure to “mark as not spam” in your email client.
  • Add the publication’s Substack email address to your contact list. All posts will be sent from this address: [subdomain]@substack.com. Replace ‘subdomain’ with the name of the publication.
  • Send an email to the publication’s Substack email address. This can often cause intermediate email filters to allow incoming messages.
  • If you are on a corporate or government email account, talk to your IT department and tell them to whitelist the publication’s Substack email address. They often have email filters that are out of your individual control and can quickly resolve the issue.

There may be an email block

Go to your Substack account and see if there’s an “Email block” message from Substack.

Follow the instructions on how to clear the email block.

Check your Settings on the Substack App

If you have the Substack app installed, head to the Notifications section in your Settings.

If you have “Prefer push” selected, this means that you’ll only receive push notifications to your phone when there’s a new post. Select “Prefer email” or “Both email and push” to resume email delivery of new posts.

I’m part of a group that wants to subscribe to a publication. Can we sign up for a group subscription?

A group subscription allows you to purchase in bulk for any group, organization, or company. You will be able to modify group membership at any time. At this time, group subscriptions are only available for annual plans.

Navigate to subdomain.substack.com (replace “subdomain” with the name of the publication).

An administrator can sign up, add the email addresses for the subscribers in the group, and pay with a single credit card.

Learn more on how to add members to your group subscription.

I’m the administrator of a group subscription. How do I add members?

As the group subscription admin, you’re able to add seats to your current group at any given time.

To add members to your group subscription:

  1. Log into Substack with the email address used to purchase the group subscription.

  2. Navigate to your Settings page by clicking on your profile icon and selecting “Settings” from the drop-down menu.

  3. Under Subscriptions, click on the publication you’d like to add members to.

  4. Click “Manage” next to your subscription plan and on the group subscription page, enter the email addresses of each member.

  5. Once you add a member, they’ll receive a Welcome email from the publication’s creator.

Can I add more seats to the group subscription?

If you’ve filled all of the slots in the group subscription, you can add more seats to the group plan in the Group Subscription section. You’ll immediately get charged a one-time pro-rated amount for the time that’s remaining on your billing cycle for a seat(s). We’ll display the pro-rated amount, the new yearly subscription rate that reflects the total number of seats, and the date of your next billing cycle.

For example, if you originally signed up a group of 10 seats and would like to add 1 additional seat five months before your renewal date, you’ll be charged a pro-rated amount for five months. On the next annual renewal cycle, you’ll be charged for 11 group seats.

Note: If you’re on an unlimited group subscription plan, you won’t see the option to purchase additional seats.

Can I cancel subscriptions during the year?

Cancelling subscriptions during the annual plan isn’t possible. However, as a group admin, you can remove people from the plan which will open up seats for other people.

For example, if you have 10 people in your group subscription and want to remove 5, you can do so from your Substack account. If you remove 5 people, this opens up 5 seats in your group plan.

Tip: You can change who the subscribers are in your group subscription plan an infinite amount of times.

Can individuals who already have a subscription be added to a group subscription?

It’s not possible to convert an individual subscription to a group subscription. They’ll need to cancel their subscription in order to be added to a group.

Once the individual has canceled their paid subscription, the group administrator can do the following:

For a new group: Subscribe a group, keeping the number of seats in mind, and in the Group Subscription section add the individuals using the same email addresses that the individuals use for their current subscriptions.

For an existing group: In their Group Subscription section, add the individuals as new members using the same email addresses that the individuals use for their current subscriptions.

A reader’s guide to Substack

Welcome to our guide for troubleshooting any technical issues you may encounter as a free or paid subscriber on Substack. In this guide, you’ll find helpful tips on how to resolve problems such as:

  • What is Substack
  • How to manage your subscription
  • How to unsubscribe from a Substack as a free reader
  • How to cancel your paid subscription
  • What to do when you stop receiving emails
  • What to do when you see a paywall but have paid We recommend starting with the FAQ that matches your specific issue. If you need further assistance, our support team is just a click away at substack.com

What is Substack?

Substack is where you can read posts, listen to podcasts, watch videos, or chat with your favorite creator on the web or the app with no noise.

When you subscribe to a Substack, an account is automatically created for you using the email address you enter. This is a private login that helps you manage subscriptions and payments, but is not visible to others. You can choose to create a Substack profile, which is a public-facing page that can include your bio and the publications you subscribe to, and unlocks features like publishing posts, posting Notes, or going live with others.

You can sign into Substack at this link and see a list of Substacks you’re subscribed to on your account Settings: substack.com

Select whether you’d like a secure access link emailed to you or log in using your password.

If you’re unable to sign in, you may have entered an incorrect email address during subscription.

For troubleshooting tips, learn more here: How do I log into my Substack account?

How do I manage my subscription?

Sign in via a Substack publication

To manage your subscription to a Substack, sign into your favorite Substack and select “Manage subscription” from the profile menu. At this time, you can only fully manage your subscription on the web version of Substack and not on the Substack apps.

On your Manage Subscription page, you can:

  • Unsubscribe as a free reader or cancel your paid subscription
  • Change your password
  • Troubleshoot any email delivery issues Sign in to your Substack account

Alternatively, you can view a list of all your free and paid subscriptions on your Substack account Settings page at substack.com

Sign into your Substack account and click on your profile icon. In the drop-down menu, select Settings and scroll to the Subscriptions section.

How do I unsubscribe from a Substack as a free reader?

Head to the Notifications section on your Manage Subscription page.

To stop receiving specific emails, slide the toggle next to a section. To completely unsubscribe from a Substack, click the red “Unsubscribe” button to stop receiving all emails moving forward.

How do I cancel my paid subscription?

If you wish to cancel your paid subscription:

  1. Head to the Account Actions section on your Manage Subscription page. Select “To cancel your paid subscription, click here.”

  2. You can either pause your subscription or cancel it completely by selecting “Cancel Subscription.”

Your paid subscriber benefits will continue until the end of the time period you’ve paid for. Once your paid subscription ends, you’ll become a free subscriber.

I used to receive emails but they’ve stopped– what happened?

Check your Promotions or Spam folders for the emails.

If found:

  • In Promotions: Drag the email to your Primary or Inbox.
  • In Spam: Mark the email as “Not Spam” and add the creator’s Substack email address to your contacts. If you’re not receiving email newsletters, it’s possible that when you downloaded the Substack app, you selected “Smart notifications”. With smart notifications, you’ll primarily receive push notifications about new posts in the app and may receive email notifications about posts you’ve missed if you haven’t opened the Substack app in a while.

To re-enable emails, follow the steps here: Will I still get emails if I have the Substack app?

I paid for a plan but I see a paywall– what can I do?

If you’ve paid for a subscription but see a paywall or your status says “Free subscriber”, follow these steps:

  • It’s possible you mis-typed your email or you have multiple accounts. Try to remember if you have another email address and sign into Substack.
  • If you’re still not seeing a paid subscription, contact the publication owner with the following information so they can locate your paid subscription.

What are the badges on my Substack profile?

On Substack.com or on the Substack app, you may notice a badge next to a creator or subscriber’s name. Whether it be on a Note, in the comments section of a post, or on a Substack profile, our team designed symbols for people who create on Substack and those who support them.

In this guide, we’ll show you the different types of badges and how to get them by:

  • Having a certain number of paid subscribers
  • Being a paid subscriber to multiple creators Our Bestsellers Badges

Substack Bestseller badges are for creators and are based on the number of paid subscribers a Substack has at that time. Bestseller badges appear as different colored checkmarks next to a creator’s name.

Learn more about how Bestseller badges work on Substack.

Paid subscriptions

If you’re a paying subscriber to a creator on Substack, a flower badge will appear next to your name.

The type of subscriber badge you see depends on how many paid subscriptions you have on Substack. The different types of badges range from 1+ paid subscriptions, 5+ paid subscriptions to 10+ paid subscriptions.

Gifted and comped subscriptions are not included in the count for each paid badge tier.

This subscriber has the 10+ paid subscriptions badge

How do I hide who I subscribe to or how long I’ve been a paid subscriber?

When someone taps on your name, we’ll display your paid subscriptions and the length of your subscription to a Substack (example: 2 weeks, 3 months, 1 year). At this time, it’s not possible to hide how long you’ve been a paying subscriber to a Substack when someone taps on your name.

However, if you’d like to hide certain subscriptions from being visible on your profile, follow these steps.

On the Substack iOS app

  • Tap on your profile and click “Edit”.
  • Tap on “Subscriptions”.
    1. Slide the toggle to the left next to the Substack you want to hide. You can also hide your subscriptions by tapping on your paid subscriber badge on the Substack iOS app. Once you tap on your paid subscriber badge, select the three dots in the upper right corner and click on “Edit subscription visibility”.

On the web

  • Click on your profile and select “Edit profile”.
  • Scroll down to the “Subscription privacy” section and slide the toggle to the left next to the Substack that you want to hide from being public. You can also hide your subscriptions by clicking on your paid subscriber badge on the web. A list of your subscriptions will appear where you can either “Hide all” or “Make all public” or individually choose which Substack to hide or make visible.

To disable your paid subscriber badge from appearing on your profile, click on the toggle next to “Your likes” to the left. This will immediately hide the paid subscriber badge from your profile.

What if I don’t have any paid subscriptions on Substack?

If you’re not on a monthly, annual, or founding member plan on a Substack, when someone taps on your name, we’ll display a “No paid subscriptions yet” message.

What credit cards can I use to pay for a subscription?

At this time, we accept most major credit cards such as Visa, MasterCard, Discover, and American Express.

Some readers in Europe may see the option to use alternative non-credit card methods such as direct debit.

At this time, there is no option to delete a payment method on Substack without replacing it with a new payment method, but you can reach out to the publication owner to have them delete your payment method from their Stripe account. Payment card details themselves are stored by Stripe, not directly by Substack.

The publisher’s Stripe account is the merchant of record for subscriptions, which means reader card/bank payment methods are tokenized and stored in Stripe’s systems.

You can have different payment methods for new subscriptions on Substack. If you update the payment method on an existing subscription, your other paid subscriptions will also be updated to that payment method. Learn more here on how to update your payment method on Substack.

What is the bell icon on Substack?

The bell on your Substack account is the Activity section.

Here, you’ll find likes and replies to comments you’ve made on Substack, likes and replies on notes you’ve posted, mentions and restacks in notes, notifications for new subscribers and followers, and recommended publications that creators you subscribe to make.

You’ll also be able to like, reply, restack, or share a note straight from the Activity Tab.

No one but you can see the items in the Activity section but the content that appears there can be things that have happened publicly on a publication.

Note: Your Activity section cannot be cleared nor notifications disabled.

Why am I listed as a free reader when I paid for a subscription?

Seeing your subscription status as “free subscriber” on the manage subscription page of a publication when you’ve paid for a monthly, annual, or founding subscription?

Most likely you paid for the subscription under one account, and are currently signed into Substack with a different account.

We recommend signing out of your Substack account and signing in with any other email address you may have.

Why is my payment to renew a subscription failing?

Card payments can fail for a variety of reasons, so you’ll want to contact your credit card issuer directly to see why they declined our transaction attempt.

Your card issuer may believe that the transaction might be fraud and they’ll be able to tell you more about this and help allow these charges to be accepted in the future.

What do I do next?

Each time a payment fails, you’ll be notified via email with instructions on how to update your card.

We’ll provide you a two-week window to get this resolved with your bank. During the two weeks, you’ll still have access to the paid subscription.

Note: If we’re unable to collect payment, your subscriber status will be converted to a free subscription.

Will my paid subscription automatically renew on Substack?

If you’re a reader with a monthly or annual paid subscription to a Substack publication, your subscription will automatically renew. You’ll receive an email reminder about a week before your subscription is set to renew.

To stop your paid subscription from renewing, you’ll need to cancel your subscription before the renewal date by following these steps: How do I cancel my paid subscription?

Once you cancel your subscription and it expires at the end of the billing cycle, you won’t be charged on the next billing date. Your paid subscriber status will automatically change to a free subscriber.

Tip: If you bought a gift or donation, those subscriptions will not automatically renew.

Intellectual Property 101

How can creators avoid copyright infringement?

Whenever copying or adapting creative works made by someone else, creators should be aware that copyright law might restrict how or whether the creator can lawfully proceed.

In general, lawfully copying, distributing, displaying, performing, or adapting the copyrighted work of another person — or even only a small portion of such a work — will require either:

  • Permission from the copyright owner (that is, a license).
  • An applicable legal limitation of or exception to copyright law that allows the use (such as fair use or fair dealing). Some creative work is in the public domain, and not subject to copyright restrictions — for instance, where the copyright has expired, or (in the United States) where the work was made by the U.S. Government.

Rules surrounding copyright expiration can be complicated and are very often country specific, so it’s worth taking a moment to doublecheck that a given work’s copyright has actually expired where you are.

What are limitations and exceptions to copyright?

Every country will have its own rules about when permission is not required to make use of a copyrighted work, or where a work may be used on terms laid out in the law.

Some of these exceptions and limitations are quite narrow and apply to very specific situations — such as copyright exceptions applicable to libraries, schools, or churches — while others, like fair use or fair dealing, tend to apply much more broadly.

Always take care to doublecheck the applicability of any given exception or limitation to copyright and ensure that you stay in compliance with any associated rules or requirements.

What is a copyright?

Copyright owners receive a set of exclusive rights over their original, creative works.

Generally speaking, copyright owners have the legal right to:

  • Prevent others from copying, distributing, displaying, or performing their works.
  • Control the creation of adaptations and “derivative works” — Note: These rights are subject to important exceptions and limitations.

Creators can use their own copyrights to prevent others from copying their original writing. Similarly, creators must take care when using others’ original works to avoid infringing those other parties’ copyrights.

How can I protect my copyrights?

Creators of original creative work, or their employers, receive copyright protection automatically when creating new works — creators do not need to apply for copyright protection.

For creators in the United States, however, registering copyrighted works with the United States Copyright Office is necessary before taking certain legal enforcement actions, and can be an important part of a copyright enforcement strategy.

Learn more here on how to avoid copyright infringement.

What is fair use?

“Fair use” is the single most important limitation on copyright in the United States on which creators are likely to rely. Several other countries have fair use rules of their own, or related-but-different “fair dealing” rules, but keep in mind that how fair use works in practice is likely to vary from place to place.

Creators can make fair use of a copyrighted work without getting permission from the rightsholder or paying for a license. But fair use is always case-specific, meaning there are no hard or universal rules setting out what it does or does not allow. Instead, fair use requires careful, individualized, consideration and balancing of four factors:

  • The purpose and character of the use.
  • The nature of the copyrighted work.
  • The amount and substantiality of the portion used in relation to the copyrighted work as a whole.
  • The effect of the use upon the potential market for or value of the copyrighted work. In the event of a legal dispute, fair use determinations are ultimately made by courts. Over time, these court decisions have provided more guidance as to what is or is not fair use — consulting these decisions, or a lawyer familiar with them, can provide significantly more clarity as to whether a given use would be considered fair.

Lear more here on what you should know when relying on fair use.

What should I know about fair use?

When relying on fair use, creators should keep a few things in mind:

  • There may be best practices guidance available to help you make fair use determinations. The Center for Media and Social Impact offers codes of best practices in fair use for various fields, including journalism and poetry, that might be a good place to start. While instructive, remember that best practices guides and similar resources are not legal advice. In all cases, you should always look for reputable, authoritative sources for guidance.
  • Your fair use determination can always be disputed by the copyright owner. You should consider the risks involved in a dispute, including the risk of litigation, when making a judgment on when to rely on fair use.
  • Even if your use would be considered fair, it may make sense to secure permission from the rightsholder anyway. Permission can sometimes be easy and cheap to secure — if so, it’s a fact that courts are very likely to consider when making fair use determinations.
  • Acting in good faith supports your case. Being honest and acting in good faith tends to support fair use arguments. Where possible, citing the original works you’re making fair use of and their authors helps demonstrate your good faith.
  • Professional legal advice is often a good idea. Copyright lawyers familiar with fair use court decisions can provide additional guidance on whether a given use would be considered fair and can help in the event of a dispute with the copyright owner. It might be cheaper to consult a lawyer than you realize, and it’s very likely a good idea if you’re intending to make a controversial or difficult fair use decision.
  • Fair use is a normal thing, and an everyday part of many creators’ practices. While familiarizing yourself with the ins and outs of how to make fair use determinations can be intimidating, it’s helpful to remember that many fair uses are unremarkable and uncontroversial: including quotes from books in the context of book reviews, for instance; or providing a copy of a newsworthy image in the context of providing commentary on the image. These kinds of uses won’t always be without controversy—they won’t even always be fair. But they are among the many kinds of fair use that creators make every day without incident, and perhaps without even realizing it.

Intro to Substack

Are there any countries or geographies you don’t support?

If you have a free Substack publication, you can start from anywhere. If you’re planning to add paid subscriptions, you can view a list of countries supported by Stripe, our payments provider.

India, Brazil, Indonesia, Mexico, Malaysia, and Thailand are exceptions to this list for additional technical reasons and the Substack application fee does not apply.

At this time, creators based in India or Brazil who’ve connected to Stripe can still enable paid subscriptions, but Substack does not currently charge a fee (you’ll still be charged the usual payment processing fee). As of May 2024, creators based in India who haven’t connected to Stripe but would like to will not be able connect and sign up for a new Stripe account via Substack. You will need to request an invite to directly from Stripe.

Please note that Substack’s 10% fee will eventually apply, and plan accordingly. This won’t apply to customers of your publication, but only to creators who would like to set up paid subscriptions via Stripe accounts based in these countries.

Can I create multiple publications under the same account?

Follow these steps to create a new publication under your Substack account.

  1. Head to your Substack account Settings page and scroll to “Publications”.

  2. Click on “Create another publication” and start the process of creating a new publication. You’ll be asked to:

  • Enter the subdomain you’d like for your new publication: Example newpublicationsubdomain.substack.com
  • Import your mailing list (if you’ve come from a different platform)
  • Add subscribers How do I switch between publications?

When you’re signed into your Substack account, click on the down arrow next to Dashboard. Select which publication you’d like to view or publish posts from.

You’ll see the Dashboard menu at the top of the page regardless of whether you’re signed into a publication or on Substack.com.

How do I choose which publication I want to appear next to my name in comments or Notes?

If you’ve selected two or more of your Substack publications to appear on your profile, then you’ll see the option to select your primary publication.

Head to your Substack profile and scroll to the Primary Publication section. From the drop-down menu, select which publication you want to appear besides your name. If one of your publications is private, it will not appear in the primary publication drop-down menu.

I have two Substacks– how do I merge them?

Substack publications can’t be combined or merged together. As a workaround, you can export the publication you’d like to consolidate and import it to the publication that you’d like to keep.

Is there a maximum number of Substacks I can create?

Substack allows up to 10 publications per account. If you encounter an error stating ‘You have created too many publications,’ this indicates you have reached this limit.

Can I use affiliate links or advertising in my emails?

Substack is entirely focused on subscriptions, so we don’t build any additional functionality to support affiliate links or advertising.

Per our Content Guidelines, we don’t permit publications that are set up with the sole purpose of advertising an external product or service, distributing offers and promotions, or similar activity.

Can I write anonymously on Substack?

When you start publishing on Substack, you can choose to keep private information anonymous.

When you create a publication, you’ll be given an option to decide what information you make publicly available on your profile.

Here are some tips to help you get started:

  • Sign up for Substack with a specific email account for your newsletter so there’s no attachment to your personal email address or name.
  • If you enable paid subscriptions on your publication, Stripe does require business and personal details to comply with regulations. Learn more about how to keep those details private from your subscribers. Tip: Check out our Resources post here which has several tips and strategies on how to write anonymously on Substack.

Can I write in a language other than English?

You’re welcome to write in any language on Substack as long as your device is set in the language you want to write in.

Localization support for certain languages is available on all Substack publications. This means that some readers may see a Substack publication in a different language.

How do I get featured on Substack?

Our team amplifies and elevates the rich kaleidoscope of writing on Substack. We look for creators who are going deep into a clear topic and exemplify best practices, like posting regularly and engaging with readers. We rotate our features regularly and aspire to give exposure to a wide variety of publications.

We welcome creators and readers to recommend another creator or content on Substack they love via regular callouts on Notes and in posts on Substack publications.

Tip: We prefer these not to be self-nominations.

As a reminder, the most powerful engine for growing on Substack is ongoing recommendations from fellow creators, not one-off features from Substack. A creator who makes a recommendation is 3x more likely to receive one. When you refresh your recommendations, readers who discover your work will also learn about some of the other great creators on Substack—specifically, fellow creators that you admire. Recommendations are part of the Substack model where creators promote and discover each other on their own terms.

Read more: How can I recommend other publications on Substack?

How do I get my email out of the Promotions tab?

We take steps on our end to try to ensure Substack emails reach the reader’s main inbox but over time the reputation that your newsletter builds up with readers it the most important input.

Asking your readers to drag the emails back to the Inbox helps. Also, you can encourage them to write to you at your @substack.com address, which helps inbox providers help recognize your emails.

Tips for readers on how to move emails:

For Gmail

Move the email newsletter to the Primary inbox by selecting the email and dragging it to the Primary tab.

For Apple Mail

Tap on the sender name and select “Add to VIP”.

For Yahoo

Follow the steps here to add @substack.com as a contact.

How do I move from Ghost to Substack?

If you’re brand new to Substack and haven’t yet created a publication, you can start your move directly at substack.com

If you’re ready to move from Ghost to Substack and experience growth with Recommendations, engage with your subscribers in Chat, or start a paid podcast, this guide will show you what you need to do to transition your members and posts to your new publication seamlessly.

In this guide, we’ll explain:

  • What you need from your Ghost account
  • How to get started on Substack

What do I need to do on Ghost?

To get started, you’ll need to export all Ghost members and export your content.

Exporting all members

On your Ghost dashboard, select Members. Click on the Settings icon at the top of the page and select “Export all members”.

A .csv file labeled Members and the date of export will be downloaded to your device.

Exporting all content

On your Ghost dashboard, select the Settings icon at the bottom of the page. In the Advanced section and select “Import/Export”.

In the Migration tools section, click on “Export content”. All your posts will be downloaded into a JSON file.

How do I get started on Substack?

How to import your Ghost members

Start your move to Substack by first creating an account. Head to the Import/Export section on your publication’s Settings page: your.substack.com

Scroll to “Import email addresses” and click on “Import emails”. Upload the .csv file of subscribers you downloaded from your Ghost account. Subscribers will have a “free” status on your Substack Subscribers dashboard.

Upload the .csv file of members you downloaded from your Ghost account and select “Import”.

Tip: After you’ve added your members, you can view their Substack status on your Subscribers page.

How to import your posts

On your publication’s Settings page, select “Import posts”.

Enter your Ghost URL and click “Get started”. Once the Substack importer has found your archive, select “Import”. If you have payments enabled on your Substack publication, you’ll see the option to import the posts as paid posts.

Imported posts may appear with Ghost’s subscribe options at the bottom of the post. To remove the unnecessary text, head to your Posts page and select the three dots next to your imported post.

Delete the selected text or image and choose “Continue” to update your changes. Select your audience and if you’d like to send the imported post as an email to your Substack subscribers and the Substack app.

How to import your Ghost paid members

You can transfer your paid Ghost members and set them up as paying subscribers on Substack in one click.

When you’re signed into your Substack account, head to your publication’s Settings page. In the Payments section, click on the “Connect with Stripe” button.

Using this tool, you’ll be able to connect your Ghost Stripe account to Substack and transfer any paid members.

  1. Sign in to Stripe using the same login details you use for Stripe on your Ghost account and select “Continue”. You do not need to create a new Stripe account. On the next page, select your Ghost Stripe account and then “Connect”.

  2. Once connected, you’ll return to your Substack account and be able to set up subscription plans for your Substack publication as well as opt into other offerings such as Substack Boost and the referrals program.

  3. Select “Enable Payments” and we’ll begin scanning your Stripe account to see how many paid subscriptions can be brought over to Substack.

  4. Once the scan is complete, which can take up to 30 minutes, you’ll receive a confirmation email letting you know how many paid subscriptions can be imported. In the Payments section, you can begin the import process by selecting, “Import paid subscriptions”.

  5. Before the import begins, you’ll need to disconnect Ghost from your Stripe account. This ensures that your newly transferred Substack subscribers aren’t double charged for their subscriptions.

In a new internet tab, head to the Installed apps page on your Stripe account. You’ll find your Ghost account in the “Connect Extensions” section: dashboard.stripe.com

Click on the Revoke access button and you’ll see a confirmation that “Access for Ghost has been revoked”.

  1. Once you’ve revoked Ghost’s access to your Stripe account, return to your Substack account and check the box next to “I’ve disconnected other services from the Stripe account”.

  2. Once the import is finished, you’ll receive another confirmation email. You can now view your paid subscribers on your Subscribers dashboard.

How do I move from Mailchimp to Substack?

If you’re brand new to Substack and haven’t yet created a publication, you can start your move directly at substack.com

If you’re ready to move from Mailchimp to Substack and experience growth with Recommendations, engage with your subscribers in Chat, or start a paid podcast, this guide will show you what you need to do to transition your subscribers and posts to your new publication seamlessly.

In this guide, we’ll explain:

  • What you need from your Mailchimp account
  • How to get started on Substack

What do I need to do on Mailchimp?

To get started, you’ll need to retrieve your campaign archive URL and export your Mailchimp Audience and campaigns.

Exporting all members

Select Audience on your Mailchimp dashboard and “All contacts” from the drop-down menu.

Click on “Export Audience” and once the export appears under Audience Exports, select “Export as CSV”. A .zip file will be downloaded to your device.

Retrieve your Campaign Archive URL

On your Mailchimp dashboard, head to Forms and click “View” on a form. In the Form builder section, select “Campaign archive page” from the drop-down menu.

In the “Campaign archive page URL” section, you’ll find the URL you need for the Substack import process.

Exporting your campaigns

Click your profile icon and select “Account & billing”.

Select “Manage my data” from the Settings tab and check the box next to “Emails”. A .zip file of your export will be downloaded to your device.

How do I get started on Substack?

Start your move to Substack by first creating an account. Head to the Import/Export section on your publication’s Settings page: your.substack.com

How to import your mailing list

Unzip the audience zip file that you downloaded from Mailchimp. Upload the .csv file of your Mailchimp audience when prompted and select “Import”.

How to import your posts

On your publication’s Settings page, select “Import posts”.

Enter the Campaign archive page URL from your Mailchimp account (example: us12345678910.campaign-archive.com) and upload the export of your campaigns.

Note: Please do not unzip the .zip export of your Mailchimp campaigns. If you have payments enabled on your Substack publication, you’ll see the option to import the campaigns as paid posts. It is possible that Mailchimp may include only up to 20 emails in your campaign archive.

Confirm that you own your Mailchimp account and agree to Substack’s Terms of Use and Privacy Policy. Select “Next” and your campaigns will immediately appear on your Substack publication.

Imported posts may appear with Mailchimp’s email footer and banner in the post. To remove the unnecessary text, head to your Posts page and select the three dots next to your imported post.

Delete the selected text and image and choose “Continue” to update your changes. Select your audience and if you’d like to send the imported post as an email and the Substack app.

How do I move from my current platform to Substack?

If you’re ready to move to Substack, experience growth with Recommendations, or engage with your subscribers in Chat, this guide will show you how to seamlessly transition your paid members and posts to your new publication.

If you’re moving from Ghost, Beehiiv, or Mailchimp, follow the detailed steps here:

How do I move from Beehiiv to Substack?

How do I move from Ghost to Substack?

How do I move from Mailchimp to Substack?

In this guide, we’ll explain:

  • Migrating off your current platform
  • How to get started on Substack

What to get from your current platform

First, you’ll need to export your email list and paid subscriber list on your current platform.

Exporting all subscribers

If you have paid subscribers or members on your current platform, ensure that the .csv file contains their subscription expiration date (or renewal date) and email addresses.

Here, we’ll use Patreon as an example:

To export your email list on Patreon, click Audience and download the .csv file.

Pause billing on your current platform

After you export your email list, you’ll need to pause billing on your current platform.

Here, we’ll use Patreon as an example.

  • Navigate to your Settings Page on your Patreon Dashboard
  • Click on Billing and Payouts
  • Scroll down to “Pause upcoming billing cycle”
  • Click on “Pause billing” Note: If you are on Patreon, this pauses your billing cycle for one month.

Getting started on Substack

On Substack, you’ll need to:

  • Create a new Stripe account
  • Import your members
  • Comp your paid members
  • Import your posts
  • Notify your members of the next steps Create a new Stripe account on Substack

After signing into your Substack account, head to your Settings page. Select Payments in the left navigation bar and once in your Payments section, click on “Connect with Stripe”.

You’ll be taken to Stripe’s website to complete the required details to receive payments. Once connected to Stripe, you can set up your paid tier plans on Substack.

Tip: Learn more here on what information Stripe requires.

Import your members onto Substack

Click on the Subscribers tab and select “Upload a CSV” from the following options:

Scroll to the bottom of the page and import a mailing list by dragging, dropping, or selecting “browse” to upload a CSV or TXT file containing the emails.

Once you upload a .csv file, confirm that these subscribers have opted into receiving emails and select their subscription type if you have payments enabled.

Tip: When you import an email list to Substack, we recommend you do not select the option to send them a welcome email– this will prevent spoiling your move to Substack before your publication is ready for prime time.

What do I do with my paid subscribers or members?

If you’re importing paid subscribers and would like to ensure they don’t lose access after your transition to Substack, you can offer these readers a trial i.e. a comp to your new publication.

Once you set up your Stripe account, it’s time to comp the paid subscribers you’re moving from your current platform to Substack. If you have annual subscribers, make sure that you have their renewal dates so that you can comp them for the correct time period.

On your Subscribers page, scroll to the All Subscribers section.

Select which readers you’d like to give a paid subscription to and click on the three dots in the banner after selecting at least one reader. Select “Comp”.

You’ll be prompted to comp the selected reader for a certain amount of time to your paid posts: 7 days, 30 days, 90 days, 6 months, 1 year, or forever.

Import your posts to Substack

Substack enables seamless content imports from the following services: Medium, Ghost, WordPress, Mailchimp, Beehiiv, Tumblr, and Blogspot. To get started, just paste the URL of your blog or newsletter.

If you use a platform other than the ones listed, check if it has an RSS feed. Our post importer also takes RSS feeds from sites like Squarespace and ConvertKit. Some platforms only include the most recent posts, such as the latest 20 posts, in their RSS feed.

Go to your publication’s Settings page, click Import/Export in the left navigation bar, and click on “Import posts.”

Once the Substack importer has found your archive, select “Import”. Alternatively, you can upload a .csv file of your posts. If you have payments enabled on your Substack publication, you can import the posts as paid posts.

Please note that when using the importer tool, you won’t be able to select and choose which posts to show on your Substack. Imported posts will automatically appear as published posts on your Substack, however, you can always delete any individual posts.

Imported posts may appear with a platform’s subscribe options or a banner at the bottom. To remove the unnecessary text, head to your Posts page and select the three dots next to your imported post.

Delete the selected text or image and choose “Continue” to update your changes. Select your audience and let us know if you’d like to send the imported post as an email to your Substack subscribers and the Substack app.

Note: If you’re moving from Patreon to Substack, you must manually copy and paste each post into the Substack editor.

Once each post has been published:

  • You can go into that post’s settings and change the publication date to match the date of its original publication.
  • You can automatically paywall your entire archive so only paying subscribers can access your posts. Notify your subscribers about the transition

The last step is to publish a free post from Substack so subscribers’ payment details can be saved in your new Stripe account, ensuring their subscriptions remain uninterrupted.

We recommend sending a post with instructions on how subscribers can subscribe on Substack, such as the one below:

“We’re transitioning from [OLD PLATFORM] because Substack offers the tools to bring you even more valuable content and community features. While [OLD PLATFORM] has been a fantastic home for us up to this point, Substack is built with creators like us in mind, allowing us to enhance your subscription experience and grow our community in meaningful ways.

I’ve provided paying members a complimentary one-month subscription to Substack, allowing you to enjoy uninterrupted access. Your subscription to paid content will expire after 30 days, so be sure to click the “Subscribe” button below to continue your access.”

To add a Subscribe button inside a post, head to your post editor and follow these steps:

  1. Click on “Button” in the editor toolbar and select “Subscribe” or “Subscribe with caption”.

  2. Your readers will be directed to your subscription plans page at this URL: subdomain.substack.com (replace subdomain with the name of your Substack)

Once the reader’s complimentary trial is ending, Substack will send them reminder emails that their trial is expiring and prompt them to continue with a paid subscription.

If I set up a paid publication, does all my content have to be paid?

No, you can publish a mix of free and paid content. Each time you publish a post, you can decide whether to make it free for everyone, or only for paying subscribers.

What are Substack leaderboards?

Substack leaderboards rank publications by category, helping creators get discovered and readers find new voices. There are two types of category leaderboards—Rising and Top Bestseller—plus dedicated country leaderboards launching in June 2026.

How leaderboards work

To explore a category—such as sports, business, or fiction—tap a category tab at the top of the Explore page in the Substack app or on the web. Each tab shows two leaderboard views: Top Bestsellers and Rising.

  • Rising – Highlights the fastest-growing publications in a category, based on paid subscription growth.These are updated every few hours.
  • Top Bestsellers – Highlights the highest-earning publications, ranked by Annual Recurring Revenue (ARR). A publication can appear in both views and can only be listed under one category at a time. These are updated daily. How to get on a leaderboard?

All categories are automated based on total paid subscribers or recent paid subscription growth. You’ll receive a notification when your publication appears on a leaderboard.

Are there country specific leaderboards?

At this time, readers and creators based in the UK, France, Italy, the Netherlands and Spain can see leaderboards (50 publications on Top Bestsellers and Rising lists) for the respective countries they’re currently located in. For example, if you’re reading Substack while in Madrid, you’ll see the Spain leaderboards.

You can opt out of your publication appearing on country leaderboards by heading to your publication’s Settings page. Scroll to the Privacy section and slide the toggle to the right next to “Opt out of country leaderboards”.

If your publication appears on the UK, France, Italy, the Netherlands, or Spain leaderboards, this means that your Stripe account is set to this specific country. Your country rank will appear on your profile if it’s higher than your category rank.

If your publication is on the wrong country’s leaderboard, you can request a change but only if it’s currently assigned to a country with leaderboards enabled (e.g., the UK, France, Italy, the Netherlands, or Spain). Click Learn more next to Opt out of country leaderboards in your publication’s Settings, then enter the correct country and your reason.

Substack Around the World

At Substack Around the World, you can select from more than 50 countries to see the top publications and posts in that country. Top posts are based on what trending free posts readers in a specific country are reading and top publications (free and paid) are based on the location of the publication.

What is the difference between Notes and Chat?

Notes

Notes are short-form posts published to your Substack profile and shared with subscribers in the Home feed.

With notes, creators might:

  • Write short-form posts (like microblogging)
  • Restack posts they enjoyed and think others might like
  • Promote a recent post by sharing a quote
  • Tag a creator they admire to start a conversation
  • Reply to the notes they find most interesting Who can start a note?

Any creator or reader on Substack can start a new note– a publication isn’t required.

In the Substack app or on the web, visit the Home tab and click the “+” button. Then tap “Note”.

Who can see a note?

Notes are public and available for anyone in the Substack network to view. They can appear in the Notes feed of people who subscribe to your publication or follow you on Substack. Notes also appear on your profile.

Can I paywall a note?

It isn’t possible to paywall an entire note where only paying subscribers can read what you’ve posted. However, when composing a note, you can select who can reply to your note: everyone or only paid subscribers.

Learn more about Notes here.

Chat

Chats is a space for creators to host conversations with their subscribers. These are private messages sent only to subscribers, which are meant to encourage discussion or conversation.

We hope it’ll allow you to share quick updates, photos, and discussion prompts on the go; and make reader engagement more vibrant through features like image replies and emoji reactions.

Who can start a chat thread?

The default is that only publication contributors can start chat threads. Creators have the option in settings to let subscribers (founding members, paid subscribers, free subscribers) participate by starting top-level chats. This empowers subscribers to help steward the community around your publication.

Who can see my chat?

Only subscribers can access your publication’s chat. When you start a new thread, you can also send it as an email and subscribers can view the chat on the web or on the Substack app.

Can I paywall a chat?

When you start a new chat thread, you can select the option to have only paid subscribers be able to reply. Free subscribers will see a “Subscribers only” lock screen on paid threads.

Read more about Chat here.

Notes

Can I add images, GIFs, or video to a Substack note?

To add visuals to a Substack note, click the note composer on the Substack app or web.

To add an image or GIF

You can post an image or GIF directly to Notes by selecting the Image icon and tapping the image or GIF from your device’s gallery.

You can add up to 6 images or GIFs to your post.

Note: GIFs are available on the web only at this time.

To add a video

You can post a video directly to Notes by selecting the Video icon and tapping the video from your camera roll or opening your camera to record a video directly.

There is a five minute upload limit and you can only upload one video per note.

Can I disable Substack Notes?

Notes will primarily appear in several places:

  • The Home tab in the Substack app
  • The Home feed on the web
  • On a Substack profile Notes can’t be disabled or removed from your Substack account.

If you’ve posted notes and would like to remove certain ones, you can individually delete them. Head to your Substack profile and under “Notes”, find the note you’d like to delete.

If you’d like to remove all notes and restacks, head to your Substack account Settings page.

In the Danger Zone section, select “Delete notes” and all your notes and restacks will be permanently deleted.

If you don’t want to show your notes in a new tab on your publication website, then toggle “Enable notes tab” on your publication settings page to off.

Can I format text on Substack Notes?

In addition to the many things you can do in a note such as linking to Substack posts, mentioning other creators, or adding an image, you can easily format text.

When composing a new note on the web, type and then highlight the text. A toolbar will appear and you’ll see options to bold, italicize, strikethrough, quotes, bullet lists, and more.

Check out an example of how a formatted note appears in the Home feed:

Can I limit note replies to paying subscribers?

You can control if only paid subscribers or everyone on Substack can reply to a note.

When composing a new note on the Substack iOS app, click on the default “Everyone can reply” and a menu of two choices will appear: Everyone or Only my paid subscribers.

Select “Only my paid subscribers” and the ability to reply to your note will be locked to subscribers with monthly, annual, or founding member plans only.

If a viewer of the note tries to reply, they’ll see a prompt that replies are for paid subscribers only.

Anyone who is @mentioned in the post has the ability to reply.

Tip: You can edit the reply settings after the note is published by clicking on the three dot menu and selecting “Edit”.

Can I lock or turn off replies on a Substack note?

At this time, replies to notes cannot be turned off on the web or the on the Substack app. Unlike a Chat thread where you can turn off replies or a publication post where you’re able to lock comments, users can continue to leave replies on your note.

However, you can delete a reply by clicking on the three dot menu and selecting “Delete”.

Note: If you block a user, their replies will be hidden from your view. However, other users will still be able to view their replies.

Can I post a private note on Substack?

Notes on Substack are public and a way to interact with the greater Substack network.

This means that they can appear in your subscribers’ Home feed or be restacked by other people and appear in their subscribers’ Home feed.

Notes will also appear on your public Substack profile.

Do my subscribers get notifications about my notes?

When you post a new note, subscribers will not get an email as they would do with newsletters.

For the web:

Subscribers and followers can receive a notification in their activity bell with new notes. This can be disabled in their Substack account Settings page in the Connections section. To stop receiving notes notifications, subscribers and followers can uncheck the “Email” and “Push” boxes next to “New notes”.

For the Substack app:

We’ll send your subscribers and followers a one-time push notification when you publish your first note. We may also send them push notifications for notes they haven’t seen but push notifications won’t be sent for every note you publish.

Subscribers and followers can disable push notifications for new notes by signing into their Substack account on the web and disabling “New notes” notifications on their account Settings page.

Getting started on Substack Notes

Notes is a feature where creators can publish short-form posts and share ideas with each other and their readers. Through Notes, creators can share posts, links, images, quotes, and comments with their subscribers and the greater Substack network.

In this guide, we’ll show you:

  • How Notes works
  • Who can see Notes
  • How to manage Notes

How Notes works

As a creator or reader, let’s get you orientated with Notes on the web or the Substack app.

On the Home tab, you’ll see short-form content from Substacks you subscribe to and the extended Substack network.

In Home, you’ll see notes and activity from:

  • People you’re subscribed to or follow on Substack
  • Other notes from publications recommended by Substacks you subscribe to
