If you synced your feed to Spotify directly on Substack, head to your podcast settings page and select “Disconnect from Spotify”. To remove your podcast distribution from Apple Podcasts, the steps can be found here.

How do I distribute my podcast to Substack if it’s hosted elsewhere?

You can add Substack as a listening destination for a podcast hosted on another platform. Substack reads from your existing feed and publishes new episodes automatically — your audio, ads, and analytics stay on your current host. This works the same way distributing a Substack podcast to Spotify or Apple Podcasts does, in reverse.

Before you start

  • You need an active podcast with an RSS feed on an external hosting platform.
  • You need a Substack publication with owner or admin access.

How to distribute your podcast to Substack

  1. Sign into your Substack publication and click Podcast in the left dashboard.

  2. Click “I have a podcast hosted elsewhere”, then click Save.

  3. Click Distribute to Substack.

  4. Paste your podcast’s RSS feed URL, then choose whether to create a new podcast section or post episodes to your main Substack.

  5. Review the show details pulled in from your RSS feed — title, description, artwork, and settings are prefilled. Edit any field, then click Continue.

  6. Choose whether to auto-publish new episodes and whether to import your back catalog.

What happens next

New episodes from your RSS feed sync to Substack automatically. If auto-publishing is turned on, they publish live. If it’s turned off, synced episodes still appear on your publication but are saved as drafts for you to publish manually.

Frequently asked questions

Does Substack create a new RSS feed or store my audio?

No. Substack reads from your existing RSS feed. Your audio, ads, and analytics stay on your hosting platform — Substack works as another listening surface, not a separate audio path.

Do I have to move my podcast to a new hosting platform?

No. Your hosting platform doesn’t change. Substack reads from it.

Can I keep dynamic ads?

Yes. Synced episodes keep using your external audio URL, so ads configured in your hosting platform continue to run on Substack.

Can I add distribute multiple RSS feeds to my Substack?

Yes. To add a new podcast feed to your Substack, head to the Podcast tab on your publication’s Settings page. Click “Add” next to publication podcasts and go through the distribution flow again.

How do I move my podcast to Substack?

If you already have a podcast hosted elsewhere, you can import past episodes and create a new home on Substack for your podcast. If you’d prefer to stay with your current host but still have episodes synced to Substack, follow these steps: How do I distribute my podcast to Substack if it’s hosted elsewhere?

To move an existing podcast:

  • Click Podcast on the left hand column on your Dashboard and click “Create podcast”.
  • Select “Import an existing podcast.”
  • Copy and paste your current RSS feed URL and click “Submit.”
  • Review the imported episodes and click “Import all episodes.” Once imported, you can view your episodes on your Posts column anytime.

Note: If you’ve already created a Podcast section, head to your Sections on your publication’s Settings and click on the three dots to “Edit”. Move an existing podcast RSS feed by clicking “Import podcast”.

What happens after I import my episodes?

After you’ve imported episodes to Substack, make sure to complete these two things:

  • Submit your new feed link to podcast directories (this can help new listeners find your podcast).
  • Tell your previous podcast host to use your new feed link and redirect so your existing listeners can follow you (also commonly called a 301 permanent redirect). You can find your Substack feed link by clicking Settings on your Podcast column. Scroll to the distribute your podcast section and see specific steps to submit your new link and redirect with your previous podcast host.

When you import your podcast to Substack, Substack becomes your podcast host. If you import a podcast archive to Substack, but then continue creating new episodes on your old hosting site, your Substack publication will not auto-populate with those new episodes. If you’d prefer to stay at your current podcasting platform, you can distribute your episodes to Substack automatically by following the steps here.

How do I make changes to the details of my podcasts that appear on apps and players?

Details such as the show description, categories, and logo displayed in podcast apps and players come from what’s on your Podcast Settings.

To change these details, click Settings on your Podcast column.

How do I publish video podcast episodes to Apple Podcasts?

Substack podcasters can deliver video episodes directly to the Apple Podcasts app. Video is delivered in HLS (HTTP Live Streaming), the video format Apple Podcasts supports, and appears alongside your existing show—no separate feed or listing required.

How do I set up video distribution to Apple Podcasts for my Substack-hosted podcast?

You can set up video delivery via your Substack podcast settings. Follow these steps to get started:

  • On you podcast settings, scroll to the Distribution section and click on Apple Podcasts.
  • Click “Set up video delivery.”
  • Enter your Apple Podcasts show URL. You can find your show URL by searching for your show on the Apple Podcasts web player and copying the link from the show page.
  • Next, generate a private API key in Apple Podcasts Connect. See the steps below. How do I generate an API key in Apple Podcasts Connect?

The API key is a unique identifier that authenticates the connection between your Apple Podcasts Connect account and Substack, and allows Substack to publish episodes to Apple Podcasts on your behalf. Substack will not have access to your listening analytics, subscription reporting, or banking or tax information.

  • Log in to Apple Podcasts Connect or create an account. If you don’t see your show in your account, claim it.
  • Select your show and go to “Settings.” If video podcast delivery is available, follow the steps to generate an API key. If video podcast delivery isn’t available, contact Substack support.
  • Choose “Account,” then select the “Keys” tab.
  • Click the “+” icon next to “Active,” enter a name for the key, and click “Generate.”
  • Download your API key. Note: You can only download an API key once, so save a backup in a secure place before you leave the page.

How do I connect my API key to Substack?

  • Return to your Substack podcast settings.
  • Upload the API key file you downloaded from Apple Podcasts Connect.
  • Add a nickname for the file and click Save credentials. How do I publish a video episode to Apple Podcasts?

Make sure you are publishing to a podcast that’s already connected to Apple Podcasts using the steps above.

  • In the Connected accounts section of the publish flow, toggle on “Publish video to Apple Podcasts.”
  • Click Publish. Your episode will also publish to the Apple Podcasts app. It may take a few minutes for the episode to appear. What will listeners see in the Apple Podcasts app?

Your show appears as a single show page where listeners can watch video or listen to audio-only. Listeners can switch between video and audio at any time during an episode, and playback picks up where they left off.

On iOS 26.4, iPadOS 26.4, visionOS 26.4, and the Apple Podcasts web app, shows with video episodes are marked with a badge. When a listener taps play on a video episode, Apple Podcasts plays the video by default.

How do I receive a verification email from Spotify for my Substack podcast?

If you distribute your Substack podcast on Spotify, they may require you to verify that you own your podcast. They will do this by sending a verification code to the email address associated with your Substack RSS feed.

In order to receive the verification code, you’ll need to follow these steps:

  1. Head to your publication’s Settings page and in the Community section, change the “Who can reply to your emails” to Everyone.

  2. If you’re not receiving the code, you can change your email address for RSS feeds. In the Details section, scroll to “Email address for RSS feeds” and enter the new email address you’d like to use.

How is my Substack podcast encoding quality chosen?

To choose the audio encoding quality that balances both quality and download speed, we take into account the following:

  • Podcast duration and
  • Stereo separation (whether the audio is mostly the same or different between the two channels) With these factors in mind, an MP3 encoding quality is chosen that ranges between 96-192kbps.

Podcast player recommendations on Substack

In this article, we’ll cover podcast player guidelines and recommendations by the Interactive Advertising Bureau (IAB).

Do not implement Auto-play

This will result in a bad user experience for the user with audio they were not expecting to hear.

Preloading

Do not preload unless the intent was clearly to play the podcast.

Using headers

Use header information located at the start of the podcast to prevent a full download when not needed.

File requests

For a full download, ask for the entire file in one go. For a progressive download, ask for the file in slices (byte range). This way a full download can be distinguished from a progressive download.

URLs

Do not modify the enclosure URL when requesting media, don’t add extra parameters.

Caching

Do not cache podcast episodes on your servers. Always download the latest episode from the enclosure URL for every app user wanting to listen.

Using GUID

Use the GUID – as opposed to episode URL, title, publication date, etc. – to identify new episodes in the RSS feed that should be automatically downloaded to a user’s device. The GUID is designed to be persistent through changes to hosting environment, titles, etc.

Automatic downloads

Employ an “automatic download unsubscribe” behavior (e.g. - stop auto downloads after 5 episodes of non-listens).

Do not automatically download all episodes (e.g. back catalog episodes) by default. This creates unnecessary drain on the publishers’ servers and consumes users’ bandwidth.

Understanding your Substack Podcast dashboard

On your Podcast tab, you can see overall data on how your podcast is performing:

  • Average downloads 7/30/90 days after publishing an episode
  • Daily and cumulative downloads
  • Total downloads for each episode
  • Top countries and top players your audience is using Overview

The Overview shows total and average downloads 7/30/90 days after publishing an episode.

Note: Total downloads are how many times a listener has downloaded or streamed an episode on their device via a web post or a podcast player. Any podcasts apps or players that use your Substack RSS feed as the source will be included.

Episodes

Each podcast episode also has a corresponding Substack post which you can view on the Posts tab. Click to expand the post and see view and open rates (if sent as an email) as well as any subscriptions gained after listening to that episode.

What is the difference between views and downloads?

Readers can view a post by going to your Substack publication on the web and never stream the episode. Alternatively, listeners can download or stream an episode via their podcast player and never view the post.

These are two different stats.

Adding tracking prefixes to your Show on Substack

Tracking prefixes help provide accurate download analytics across listening apps. To add a third-party tracking prefix to your Show:

  • Open your Podcast Settings page.
  • Scroll down to Analytics Prefixes.
  • Enable your preferred tracking prefixes using the toggle on the right-hand side.
  • Don’t see your preferred provider? Reach out to support here, and we’d be happy to help.

What artwork appears for my Substack podcast?

When you create a podcast on Substack, you’ll need to upload an image. You can think of this logo like branding for your podcast that’ll appear in podcast players like Apple and Spotify.

The default image is a microphone but this can be changed anytime on your Podcast Settings page. Select “Change” to upload a logo that matches your brand.

Where does my podcast artwork come from?

Your podcast artwork can appear on different parts of your publication like your homepage and the episode page.

However, podcast artwork isn’t limited to just your podcast logo and different images may appear.

Images can be pulled from various places:

  • Podcast artwork: This is the main logo for your podcast set on the Podcast Settings page.
  • Podcast artwork for paid episodes: If you have payments enabled, you can set additional artwork for paid episodes in the Advanced settings section on the Podcast Settings page. This artwork will be shown for both paid podcast episodes and as the artwork in a paid subscriber’s podcast feed. This means that paid feeds will show the artwork you set here instead of your primary podcast art on Substack and in podcast players. You can use this to help listeners differentiate between a free feed and a paid feed.
  • Social preview: The image you set on the Post Settings page.
  • Images from your post: Images that you include in the body of your post can appear as your podcast artwork.
  • Imported podcast artwork: If a podcast episode was imported to Substack, we’ll use that podcast artwork as your Substack artwork.
  • The default Substack microphone: If an image is required, we’ll sometimes use the default Substack podcast microphone. If you upload your own podcast logo, we will never use the default. Note: The order or precedence of the above can vary.

Where can listeners see my podcast artwork?

At the top of an episode page, we show an image which is the same image we use in podcast players that support individual episode images (like Spotify).

The order is:

  • Podcast artwork for paid episodes (if the episode is paid).

  • Podcast artwork.

  • Image from the body of the post.

  • The default Substack microphone. On your publication homepage, we pick the podcast artwork last and will never use the default Substack microphone.

  • Episode art on the “Publish” page of the draft audio post.

  • The social preview image.

  • Image from the body of the post.

  • Podcast artwork for paid episodes.

  • Podcast artwork (unless you have the default Substack microphone as a logo).

  • If none of the above are available and we need to show an image, a gray box will appear. Some external players may show different images for individual podcast episodes. You can customize this by uploading your own artwork on the “Publish” page of your draft audio post.

When you’re ready to publish your audio post, click “Continue”. Scroll to the “Episode metadata” section and click “Upload New”. Here, you can upload an image from your device which will be shown for that specific episode.

Note: For Apple Podcasts, the episode image should be greater than 1400 x 1400 px and less than 3000 x 3000 px.

Things to note:

  • If you import a podcast to Substack, the podcast artwork will not transfer over.
  • If your episode image is greater than 1400 x 1400 px and less than 3000 x 3000 px and Apple Podcasts doesn’t update any artwork changes, we recommend following Apple’s troubleshooting steps here, and reaching out to them using the “Contact us” link in the article if those steps don’t resolve the issue.

Will my Podcast RSS feed show paid-only content?

Each subscriber gets a private Podcast RSS feed that’s unique to them. Depending on whether or not they have a paid subscription, the feed might include public episodes or public and paid episodes.

If your podcast episode is intended for paid subscribers, you can add a customized version of the audio to your post so that free listeners can listen to a preview.

Publications 101

Can I change the price of my subscription after launch on Substack?

Yes, you can change the price at any time on your publication’s Settings page in the Payments section. Keep in mind that the minimum price on Substack is $5/month and $30/year. Only new paid subscribers will pay the new rate once the price change has taken effect. Your existing subscribers will remain on the plan they originally paid for. If you’d like to request a change in price for your existing subscribers, visit the support widget and ask for a human support specialist.

Note: Subscriptions renew at the original rate the subscriber pays as long as they don’t cancel and resubscribe.

Can I customize my subscription benefits?

You can customize the messaging for each subscription plan so your readers can get a better understanding of what they’ll get out of their subscription.

On your publication’s Settings page, click on Payments in the left navigation bar. Enter your message under “Subscription benefits”.

Tip: For a preview of what this will look like, head to your.substack.com/subscribe. Replace “your” with your publication’s subdomain.

Can I embed a signup form for my Substack publication?

To embed a signup form for your Substack publication, head to your publication’s Settings page and click Growth features in the left navigation bar. In this section, you’ll see a unique code for your publication (see below for an example).

You can insert the HTML code onto any website to embed a signup form for your Substack publication.

Note: Our embed signup forms work on any platform that supports iFrames (WordPress, Squarespace, custom websites, etc.) but this excludes Medium.

At this time, the embed signup form is not customizable.

If you’d like to hide your publication name and logo from the signup form, slide the toggle to the lef next to “Show pub logo on embed”. Copy the new code that appears and paste it into your website.

The form will appear without your Substack publication’s name and logo and just a space for new readers to enter their email addresses. After clicking “Subscribe”, readers will then see the subscription plan options if you have payments enabled or the pledges options if you have Pledges enabled. If you’re recommending other publications on Substack, your new subscriber can easily subscribe to these publications in one spot.

Can I make a private Substack publication?

A private Substack publication that you can host alone or readers can request to subscribe to read your posts. As a creator, you can choose to approve or decline each subscription request.

The option to approve or decline a reader’s request to subscribe to your publication is only availalbe for private Substacks. If your Substack is public, any reader can subscribe to your publication.

To make your Substack private, head to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Privacy section.

Click on the toggle to the right and your publication will immediately become private i.e. readers won’t be able to see any posts.

Things to note:

  • Although set to private, your publication’s Welcome page may appear in a web search. The Welcome page is indexed in search engines but no posts will be for a private Substack.
  • Like any post that’s emailed to subscribers from a Substack publication, the recipient may forward this to someone else even if your Substack publication is private. How do I approve or deny a subscription request?

When a reader requests to subscribe to your Substack, you’ll receive an email notification with their details.

To see your requests, head to your Subscribers page and click on approve or deny next to each request.

If the reader does not have a Substack profile, an email address will be displayed instead of their profile name.

If you approve a request, the reader will be automatically subscribed and sent a Welcome email.

I want to stop receiving these email notifications– what can I do?

On your publication’s Settings page, head to the Notifications section. Next to “Subscription requests”, click on the toggle to the left. Moving forward, you won’t receive any subscriber requests emails.

Can I moderate comments on my Substack posts?

On a post, you can either remove comments or moderate comments reported by readers.

If you’d like to moderate comments on a post, you can enable comment reporting on your publication.

  1. Navigate to your publication’s Settings page and click on Community in the left navigation bar.

  2. Click on the toggle next to “Enable reporting”.

  3. Click on “Moderate” in the Community section to review comments and chat messages reported by your community.

How does reader-reported comments work?

Readers can help identify and report unwanted commenter behavior by using the reporting tool on comments.

Reported comments will appear in the “Reported” tab. Review the comment and select “Approve” or “Remove”.

  • If you choose to remove the comment, it’ll appear in the “Removed” tab and not be visible to other readers.
  • If you choose to approve the comment, it’ll appear in the “Approved” tab and remain on your publication. To see why a reader reported a comment, select “See report reasons”.

Will I be notified about reader-reported comments?

Yes! Depending on if there are any new reader-reported comments, you will be notified on a daily cadence via email.

Note: If you do not want your readers to be able to report comments, you can move the toggle to the left next to “Enable reporting” in the Community section of your Settings.

How do I hide a comment on a post?

To hide a comment on the web version of you post, simply click on the vertical line on the left side of the comment. This will collapse the comment from your view.

To hide a comment on the Substack app, tap the three horizontal dots and select “Hide comment”. This will hide the comment from your view.

How to I hide a restack in the comments section on my post?

When someone restacks your post, it can appear in the Home feed of their subscribers and followers. Now, restacks can also appear in the comments section of the web version of your posts. Readers can choose between a Comments tab or Restacks tab.

Just like comments, publishers have the ability to remove specific notes from the discussion section of their post. Click the three dots next to a restack and select “hide note from post” option. Additionally, any users who you have muted or blocked previously will never show up in this section.

Can I pause payments on my publication on Substack?

Yes! When you pause:

  • The billing cycles for your subscribers will be frozen.
  • New readers will not be able to purchase a paid subscription plan.
  • Existing paid subscribers won’t be charged. There is no time limit when you pause your publication; when you’re ready to start publishing again, you can unpause to resume the billing cycles of your paid subscribers.

Head to your Settings and click on Payments in the left navigation bar. Click on “Pause” next to “Pause subscription billing” to pause your publication’s current paid subscriptions.

After you pause payments, you’ll see the option to schedule a date when your publication can resume payments. Click “Choose date to unpause” then select a date from the calendar. Select “Pause until xxxx-xx-xx” and your publication will automatically resume payments on that date so that new readers can purchase a paid subscription and existing paid subscriptions will continue from where they left off.

Note: Your current paid subscribers will not be notified when you pause payments on your publication.

Can I set up another subscription plan for readers who want to pay more?

Founding membership subscribers are, for some creators, a remarkably significant source of revenue. The founding member option allows readers to pay more than the listed price as an extra show of support, similar to a donation. You can set this price at whatever you’d like, as long as it’s higher than your annual plan price.

If you have paid subscriptions enabled, head to your publication’s Settings page and click on Payments in the left navigation bar. Scroll to the Founding member plan and select “Add plan”.

Enter the suggested amount and click “Save”.

Once this plan is enabled, it will be displayed on your subscription plans page along with the monthly and annual plans. To remove the founding members plan, head back to your publication’s Settings page and select “Remove plan” next to the price point.

If you’d like readers to be able to pay less than the founding plan price you’ve set but more than your annual plan, slide the toggle next to “Flexible founding plan pricing”.

What can I send to my founding members?

Custom Welcome email

If you’d like to send a special message just for new founding members, you can customize a welcome email on your publication’s Settings page. Head to the Emails section and select edit next to “Welcome email to founding subscribers”.

This will be the first email founding tier members receive when they subscribe to your Substack. You can embed a file in EPUB, PRC, XLSX, PDF, CBR, CBZ, KFX, or KPF formats.

Exclusive posts

With the founding member plan, you can send posts via email and the Substack app to just this tier of subscribers or they can view posts on the web or the Substack app that are just for them.

Once you’re ready to publish your draft post, select “This post is for founding members only” on the Publish page.

Non-founding members will see a paywall when viewing a post that’s only for founding members.

Note: Founding member posts will have a special founding members symbol instead of the standard lock symbol for paid posts.

What will happen to my Founding subscribers if I remove the Founding subscription from my publication?

When you remove the founding subscription tier, it only prevents new subscribers from subscribing at that level. Existing founding-tier subscriptions will still automatically renew at the founding price. They are not given options, but if you, as the publisher, want to lower their subscription price to your annual subscription price, that needs to be done by Substack support.

Can I use my root domain without “www” or another subdomain on Substack?

When you buy a domain, the name you buy– such as “example.com” or “example.co.uk”– is called the “root domain”. Your domain registrar will also allow you to use subdomains, such as “www.example.com” or “blog.example.co.uk”.

Why is it required that my Substack publication is hosted by a subdomain such as “www”?

Some domain registrars, including GoDaddy and Google Domains, only allow CNAME records to point from a subdomain and not from a root domain (this is sometimes called “CNAME flattening”). For example, on Godaddy, you won’t be able to use example.com for your Substack publication, but you will be able to use www.example.com.

If you’ve registered your domain with one of these registrars, we require a subdomain for your Substack.

Formats that work:

  • www.example.com

  • newsletter.example.com Format that won’t work:

  • example.com Can I set up a root domain redirect on Substack?

Yes! You can set up a root domain redirect on your publication’s Settings page in the Domain section.

If you don’t have a custom domain yet on Substack:

When setting up a custom domain on Substack, check the box next to “Root domain redirects”.

Copy and paste these exact settings and add the DNS record in your DNS provider.

Your DNS record will need an A record with name or host value of @ with target value of: 3.231.35.217

What is an alias record?

An alias record is a special type of record that only a few registrars support that allows you to map a domain to another domain (very similar to CNAME) but crucially allows you to map the “root” domain to something else. Practically this matters because it allows someone to set up their custom domain with us as e.g. example.com as opposed to www.example.com

If you have an existing custom domain on Substack:

Head to the Domain section and click on “Setup redirects” next to “Add root domain redirects”.

Here, you’ll go through the process to get the A record added. Go to your DNS provider and copy and paste the exact settings you see on this page.

After you’ve completed the set up on your DNS provider, select “Setup root domain redirects”.

Note: It can take up to 36 hours for the redirects to start working. If you’ve previously used a forwarding workaround such as redirect.pizza, please remove all other A DNS records for your root domain before setting up the root domain redirect on Substack.

Can my Substack publication have multiple authors or contributors?

Yes, you can add multiple contributors to your publication.

  • Head to your publication’s Settings column.
  • Click on Team in the navigation bar.
  • Click “Invite” and type the email address of the person you’d like to invite. Click “Send invite”.
  • If someone is listed as public, they’ll be publicly listed as a contributor to your publication such as on the About page.
  • If you give someone Admin privileges, they will have full access to your publication and Settings. They can also start a new thread on Chat.
  • If you give someone contributor privileges, they can see your Posts column to view, edit, and publish drafts. On an individual post, they will not have access to the Growth tab or the recipients table on the Reach tab. They also won’t have access to new subscriptions driven by a post nor the estimated annualized revenue that an individual post has generated. Contributors will not have access to your publication’s Settings or Stats.
  • If you give someone byline privileges, they can be listed as an author but cannot access Posts or Settings. If you get a “Cannot invite user to publication” message when trying to invite someone to your team, this may mean that this user has previously subscribed to your publication and then unsubscribed.

If the person you’ve invited to your Substack team isn’t getting the invitation emails, they can go to their Substack account Settings and accept your invite in the Publications section.

If you’re the owner of a Substack and your publication isn’t appearing on your profile, ensure that your visibility in the Team section is set to “Public”.

Tip: To remove someone from your team, select the three dots and click “Remove”. To change the role of a team member, click on the role and select either Byline, Contributor, or Admin.

Prefer to have a one-time or occasional guest writer on your publication? Try adding the collaborator directly to the byline on a draft post and they’ll appear as a guest writer. They will only be able to view the draft and not make any edits.

Are there any statistics for contributors or authors on my Substack?

If you have multiple contributors on your Substack, you can see who drives the most engagement across all posts, without digging through individual posts. Head to your Stats tab and click on Posts in the navigation bar. Click on “Display” and in the Aggregation column, select “Author”.

Can readers see my Substack publication in a different language?

Localization support for certain languages is available on all Substack publications on both the web and the Substack app.

Here’s what to expect with the localization feature:

  • For subscribers and visitors: Reader-facing surfaces will appear in the language that you select on your publication’s Settings page. These surfaces may include but are not limited to post emails that you send out, buttons on your posts and Substack publication, subscriber plans and benefits, paywalls on paid posts, and many more.
  • For you: Anything you see on your dashboard will be translated. This includes anything you see on your publication’s Settings page to what appears on the Subscribers dashboard. Note: If you’ve changed any customizable fields on your publication such as your subscriber benefits, welcome emails for free or paid subscribers, or your welcome page, then these will not be translated. Any custom content that you’ve written such as a custom subscribe button, a poll, or text in a post will not be touched. We will not try to translate something that’s written in your voice.

A reader’s point of view

When readers visit the web version of your publication, it’s as if the whole publication (besides the content you’ve written in English or in a different language) will be in the translated language. From the “Subscribe” buttons to the navigation bar, new subscribers can easily subscribe in the primary language of your publication, providing a better reader experience for non-English readers.

Tip: Readers will not be able to select what language they’d like to view your publication in. Their Substack account will not be translated and aspects such as their Account settings will remain in English.

Emails that come from Substack such as password reset links will not be translated. However, transactional emails from your publication such as a payment confirmation email or a pledge thank you email will be translated.

How do I enable translations of my publication?

To enable localization of your Substack publication, head to your publication’s Settings page. In the Basics section, scroll to “Language”. From the drop-down menu, select from the following languages: Catalan, Danish, German, English, English (UK), Spanish, French, Italian, Dutch, Norwegian, Polish, Portuguese, Brazilian Portuguese, Swedish, Turkish, Arabic, Japanese.

If you’d like your publication to appear in English, head back to the Basics section on your publication’s Settings page and select “English” from the drop-down menu.

Does Substack have a referral program?

Note: Interested in a feature where free and paid subscribers can refer their friends to your Substack and get rewarded? Check out the subscriber referrals program here.

Yes! With our gift subscription referral program, paid subscribers can bring new readers to your publication by gifting them access to your paid content for a limited time.

How do I turn on the gift subscription referral program?

Navigate to your publication’s Settings and scroll to the Growth features section. Slide the toggle to the right next to “Enable paid subscriber gifts”.

In the paid subscriber gifts section, you can customize the email sent to paid subscribers about the gift subscription feature and enable or disable re-up referral emails to those who’ve shared all their gift subscriptions.

How can my subscribers refer their friends to my publication?

New and top paid subscribers will receive an email with a link to a Gift Rewards page on their Substack account.

On the Gift Rewards page, referrers can send up to five gift subscriptions (one free month of paid subscriber benefits) with a personal message and view who has accepted a gift subscription. If they send out the initial five offerings and they’re redeemed, the referrers may receive an additional five gift rewards to give away.

If you’d like to disable referrers from receiving a re-up of gift subscriptions once their initial gift rewards have been shared, slide the toggle next to “Enable re-up referral emails” to the left.

What do gift subscription recipients need to do?

No credit card is required in order for the recipient to claim their gift and cancelling a subscription isn’t necessary.

When a gift subscription is ending and the recipient hasn’t added a payment method and converted to a paid subscription, they’ll automatically become a free subscriber.

Tip: We’ll send reminder emails to recipients before their subscription is expiring and nudge them to add a payment method to keep their paid access. A link to continue their subscription will be in these emails.

Where can I see who is sending gift referrals?

Head to your Stats dashboard and select on Gift Referrals. Click on each category to see least to greatest or vice versa.

You can also view on your Subscribers dashboard who the recipients in the referral program are. Set a filter as “Subscription type is Gift”. These subscribers will appear as “Gift”. Other paid subscribers will appear as well under this filter as “Monthly Gift” and “Yearly Gift”.

Note: “Monthly Gift” and “Yearly Gift” subscribers are different from the referral program as these subscribers have been gifted paid subscriptions and count towards your ARR whereas recipients in the referral program have been gifted trials to your publication.

To opt of the Referral program, follow these steps here.

Guide to your Substack Home

With the Substack Home page, you can gain valuable insights into your publication’s health.

This launchpad offers a high-level overview of your Substack publication including subscriber growth and engagement, gross annualized revenue, recent post performance, and strategic guidance.

On your Home tab, you may see the following sections:

  • Announcements
  • Overview
  • Latest Post
  • Drafts
  • Recent Posts
  • Subscriber Notes
  • Resources In this guide, we’ll focus on the Overview and Recent Posts.

Overview

This section will show your gross annualized revenue, paid subscribers, and total subscribers.

Total subscribers: The total number of free and paying subscribers to your Substack.

Gross annualized revenue: How much money you make per year, based on annualizing your current paid subscriptions.

Paid subscribers: The total number of paying subscribers to your Substack.

If you’d prefer not to see the Overview section on Home, head to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Privacy section. Turn the toggle on next to “Hide stats”.

Learn more here about your Substack Home.

Recent Posts

This section will show you view counts, recipients, and open rates for recently published posts– both paid and free.

  • Views: The total number of times the post was viewed as an email, on the web, or in the Substack app.

  • Opened: The percentage of subscribers who viewed this post after receiving it in their email or the Substack app. If one person views your post five times, they are only counted once.

  • New subs: The number of people who subscribed to your publication after reading this post. Things to note:

  • Drafts can be accessed on both the Home and Posts tab. Drafts on the Home tab

Drafts on the Posts tab

  • Want to see more details? Click on any data point or post on the Home page and you’ll be directed to the corresponding metrics page or health summary of the post.
  • Once you publish a post, you can view how it’s performing in the Latest post section. Any new subscriptions that the post has generated, paid or free, will be displayed here.

Guide to your Substack Posts page

Stats update: We’ve updated how Delivery Rate and Open Rate are calculated to better reflect how posts are received and viewed across email and the Substack app. In some cases, subscribers who view posts in the app were not fully reflected in delivery and open metrics; these updates ensure that app inbox receipt and views are accurately counted. As a result, publishers may notice these rates increase on new and historical posts.

The Posts page streamlines your ability to create, edit, and publish posts. With features such as filtering and sorting, this page allows you to view multiple posts and high-level post stats in one view.

Overview Post Statistics

The Posts page is designed into three sections for your posts: Published, Drafts, and Scheduled.

In the Published section, you’ll see every post that you’ve published on your Substack– posts that you’ve deleted will not appear here.

In the Drafts section, you can access any posts that you’ve started but haven’t yet published. Click on the hamburger menu next to each draft post to edit, duplicate, or delete the draft. Filter the drafts by recently edited, newest, or oldest.

Tip: Drafts can be accessed both in the Home and the Posts tab.

Posts that appear in Scheduled are drafts that you’ve scheduled to be published on the web version of your Substack or scheduled to be published to the web and sent via email and the Substack app at a future date and time.

To edit or remove the scheduled date and time of the future post, select the hamburger menu and select “Edit post”. On the draft, click on the date and time above the editor toolbar to see the option to un-schedule the post.

What can I do on the Posts page?

For each section, you can filter and sort how you want to view your published and draft posts.

Click on “Filter” and select from an array of options to quickly filter your posts list: audience, post type and date, author, or tag. If you don’t see one of these options, it likely means you don’t have any posts with that criteria. For example, if you don’t see “Tag”, perhaps you haven’t added any tags to your posts yet.

If any posts match your filters, they’ll appear on the Posts page. To remove a filter(s), select “Filter” and click on “Clear all”.

If you’d like to see your posts in a different order, select the down arrow next to “Filter”. Select whether you’d like to view your posts from newest to oldest, oldest to newest, or recently edited. If you use the search function, you can sort the posts that appear by relevance.

Note: What you select in the sort order will determine how you view posts on the Posts page. This does not change how readers view posts on your publication.

On the posts page, you can type any search term into the search bar to see posts that match that search term populate below. You can search for posts on your Published, Drafts, and Scheduled sections.

Post statistics give you insight into the reach, engagement, and growth performance of each post you publish. Click on a post and you’ll see a detailed summary of metrics such as total views, comparison charts of post-performance compared to past posts, and time series charts to help you understand how the post is performing during the first few days after publishing.

Overview

The Overview tab shows a high-level view of how your post is performing and how readers came to the post.

  • Total views: The total number of times this post has been viewed across web, email, and the Substack App. If one person views this post five times, that counts as five views.
  • Free subscription: The number of people who subscribed to your publication from that post.
  • Paid subscription: The number of people who signed up for a paid subscription from that post.
  • Estimated revenue increase:The increase in Gross Annualized Revenue from new paid subscriptions driven by this post. Coupons, subscription changes, and payment failures after retries are not factored into this estimation.
  • Recipients: The number of subscribers who were sent an email or mobile app notification for this post. Subscribers who receive both an email and a mobile app notification are only counted once.
  • Open rate:The percentage of subscribers who viewed this post after receiving it in their email or the Substack app. If one person views your post five times, they are only counted once.
  • Link clicks: The percentage of openers who clicked a given link in your post and the total click count.
  • Traffic sources: The top traffic sources that readers found a post from. Reach

The Reach tab summarizes how your post reached your subscribers. You can see chart views of recipients, delivery rate, and view count, plus a list of how readers found your post and its traffic sources.

  • Recipients: The number of unique people who received an email or Substack app notification about this post. Subscribers who receive both an email and a mobile app notification are only counted once.
  • Delivery rate: The percentage of subscribers who received this post in their email or the Substack app. Subscribers who receive it in both places are only counted once.
  • Views: The total number of times this post has been viewed across web, email, and the Substack App. If one person views this post five times, that counts as five views. Engagement

The Engagement tab gives you a sense of how your subscribers interacted with your post via an engagement rate, open rate, click-through rate,

  • Engagement rate: The percentage of subscribers who engaged with this post after opening an email or mobile app notification for the post. Engagements include: liking a post, clicking a link, sharing or restacking a post, commenting on a post, and reading a post to the bottom. Subscribers who engage multiple times (e.g. like and comment) are only counted once. Users who engage with your post but are not subscribed are not included in the engagement rate.
  • Open rate: The percentage of subscribers who viewed this post after receiving it in their email or the Substack app. If one person views your post five times, they are only counted once.
  • Click-through rate: The percentage of subscribers who clicked a link in this post after opening an email or mobile app notification for the post. Users who click links but are not subscribed are not included in the engagement click-through rate. The Engagement tab also shows which subscribers who opened the email post clicked on a link and which readers liked and commented on the post.

Growth

The Growth tab helps you understand how the post affected your publication. If you have payments enabled, this tab will show how much your gross annualized revenue changed and how many new paid and free subscribers the post generated.

  • Estimated revenue increase: The estimated increase in Gross Annualized Revenue from new paid subscriptions driven by this post. Coupons, subscription changes, and payment failures after retries are not factored into this estimation.
  • Paid subscribers: The number of readers who became paid subscribers after reading this post. Readers may have been free subscribers or could be new subscribers.
  • Free subscribers: The number of readers who became free subscribers after reading this post.

How can I block AI from using my Substack publication to train their models?

To disable AI tools and AI assistants from using your published Substack content as part of a training model, first head to your publication’s Settings page.

Scroll to the Privacy section and slide the toggle to the right next to “Tell AI tools not to train their models on your content.”

This setting indicates to AI tools like ChatGPT, Claude, and Gemini that their models should not be trained on your published content. This will only apply to AI tools that respect this setting, and blocking training may limit your publication’s discoverability in tools and search engines that return AI-generated results.

How can I choose a leaderboard category for my Substack publication?

Categorizing your publication helps others discover your writing through search and discovery surfaces on Substack. Country leaderboards are separate from category leaderboards. Your country is determined by your Stripe account location—not your category selection.

To add categories to your publication:

  • Sign in to your Substack account
  • Navigate to your publication’s Settings page.
  • In the Basics section, select from the drop-down menu what you think your publication is about. For example, Culture or Travel can be two categories you can select. Note: Category selections are requests. Substack takes these preferences into account in creating and displaying our leaderboards and reserves the right to adjust inappropriate assignments. If your publication appears on a leaderboard, it will only be listed under the primary category you select. Category selection does not affect country leaderboard eligibility.

How can I create multiple newsletters or podcasts under one publication?

You can create and manage multiple newsletters or podcasts associated with a single publication.

On Substack, additional newsletters are called “Sections.” A section lets you send posts to a subset of your audience, and subscribers can control their email settings for this content.

You can also add a podcast to each of your newsletters. This lets you publish podcast episodes that will be emailed to the subset of your audience subscribed to the associated newsletter and lets you publish this podcast in iTunes, Spotify, and other podcast platforms.

Access your Sections and their settings directly at this link: your.substack.com

To create a new newsletter

  1. Head to your publication’s Settings page and click on Sections in the left navigation bar.

  2. In this section, select “Add section”.

  3. Fill out the details of your new newsletter.

Select whether you’d like to:

  • You can hide posts from the publication’s homepage: Choose whether you’d like section posts to appear on your homepage or in the archives.
  • Add new subscribers by default: Any new subscriber who signs up for your publication will be automatically subscribed to your new newsletter. If this box is unchecked, they must subscribe to this newsletter separately.
  • Copy your email list: All your current subscribers, including subscribers who are subscribed to to other sections, will automatically be subscribed to this newsletter if this is checked. Subscribers will not receive a notification when added to a new section.
  1. Your new newsletter will be given a URL that defaults to the newsletter’s title:

your.substack.com/s/newsletter-title

Tip: To show or hide your newsletter as a tab on your publication, click on “Show” or “Hide” next to the title in the Website section of your Settings page.

To create a new podcast:

Head to your Settings page and click Podcasts in the left navigation bar.

  1. You can nest the podcast in your publication or create a new section.

  2. Enter the details of your new podcast.

  3. You can now view your new podcast in the Podcast section on your Settings page. To make changes to your podcast, click “Edit” next to your podcast.

What happens if I have a new subscriber or would like to add current subscribers?

If you’ve checked the box next to “Add new subscribers by default” when creating a new section, all new subscribers will receive the new section by default.

If you didn’t check the box next to “Copy your email list” when initially creating a section, you can still add your existing subscribers after you’ve made a section.

Head to the section’s settings and scroll to “Subscriber settings”. Select the “Add subscribers” button, and emails will be turned on for this section. It should be completed in a few minutes.

How can readers unsubscribe from a section?

They can edit this by doing one of the following:

  • Click “unsubscribe” at the bottom of any email from you, which takes them to a page where they can choose what sections in your publication to subscribe to.
  • Log into their Substack account, head to their account Settings, select their subscription, and choose which newsletters to receive. How do I display sections on my publication’s main homepage?

In the Website editor, select “Posts & Modules”.

Select which section(s) you want to surface on your homepage. Below the fold, the specific section(s) will appear alongside recent posts.

Can I change how my section homepage appears to readers on the web?

You can customize what your readers see when they visit a section. Head to the Website editor and in the Posts section, you can customize the header and body of the section’s homepage.

Some things to keep in mind when using Sections:

  • We recommend using sections for publishing new content rather than displaying static resources or information, for which recommended links are a better feature.
  • Sections are not just a way to organize your posts– this feature lets you target a subset of your primary audience. Sections are recommended if you want to be able to send emails to subsets of your subscribers.
  • To hide the section’s posts from appearing on your publication’s homepage or archives, head to the section’s settings and check the box next to “Hide posts from homepage”.
  • If you want to change your section’s URL, just head to your section’s settings and enter the new URL.
  • To see which readers are subscribed to a particular section, you can follow these steps here.
  • If you want to move an existing post to a section, navigate to the post you’d like to move and click “Edit”. Select the desired section from the dropdown and then update the post.

How can I customize renewal reminder emails?

We automatically send renewal emails to annual paid subscribers one week before their current subscriptions expire.

You can customize this email by following these steps:

  1. Navigate to your publication’s Settings page and click on Emails in the left navigation bar.

  2. Select “Edit” next to “Subscription renewal email”.

  3. Change the default template by deleting and entering your own text and click “Save”.

How can I get a QR code for my Substack?

You can access your Substack’s custom QR code by following these steps:

  • Sign into Substack and head to your publication’s Dashboard.
  • Click on Settings and on the settings page, scroll to the Growth section.
  • A unique QR code for your Substack will be shown. Click “Download” and you’ll have the option to save the QR code to your device as a PNG or SVG format.

How can I optimize my Substack publication for SEO?

At Substack, we do a number of things behind the scenes to ensure that all publications are indexed and ranked well in search engines. While ranking is determined by Google’s concept of creator credibility and the number of links that direct to your website, we’re continually evolving our approach and improving the SEO benefits for publications.

There are a number of things you can do to strengthen your SEO presence and help grow your Substack’s prominence on the internet.

Use your name as your Substack publication URL

When creating your publication, we recommend using your name in your Substack URL as this is most likely what readers will search for.

Note: If you have an existing site or publication name that readers are already familiar with, we recommend using that as your Substack subdomain.

Readers are more likely to search for your name on Google rather than the title of a publication that they may not be familiar with.

“Your name Substack” is a better search term than “Two minute tea time”.

If you already have a Substack publication, you can change the subdomain by heading to the Danger Zone section on your publication’s Settings page and selecting “Change subdomain”.

Link your Substack on your personal site and social media

Have a personal site? Here’s a couple things you can do:

  • Share your Substack publication link on your site by asking visitors to check out your publication e.g. “Subscribe to [your name or name of your Substack] on Substack.”
  • Embed your newsletter’s email signup form directly onto your site. This HTML code can be found in your Settings. Add your Substack URL on social media like X and Instagram as a clickable URL in the bio or website field. This can drive traffic to your Substack and your reputation on Google.

The more links, the better

The best way to improve your SEO ranking is to get more inbound links to your Substack publication. An inbound link is a link on another site that directs readers to your Substack.

How can you increase links to your Substack? Our Community team recommends talking about your Substack everywhere. Not only does this increase the visibility of your publication but inbound links indicates to search engines the trustworthiness of your Substack.

Tip: Know a fellow Substack creator? Ask them if you can be a guest author or if they can share your Substack on their publication. Both are considered as inbound links.

How can I recommend other publications on Substack?

Recommendations enable creators to promote and support one another. You can promote the Substack you recommend to your audience, and the creators of those Substacks are prompted to recommend you back.

How do I recommend Substacks?

Head to your Substack’s navigation bar and click on Recommendations. Click on “Add Recommendation”.

Start by searching for your favorite publication and add a blurb as to why you recommend your readers to check out this Substack.

On the Recommendations page, you can:

  • Add or remove recommendations
  • Write a blurb explaining why you recommend that Substack
  • Manage settings such as whether to display it on your publication’s homepage or include it in recommendation digests Where do recommendations appear?

Recommended Substacks are shown to your new readers in three places:

  1. In the subscribe flow, your recommendations are presented to readers after subscribing to your Substack.

Readers are presented with the option to follow the creators behind all the Substacks you recommend, as well as subscribe to a handful of publications.

  1. On your publication’s homepage.

  2. In Recommendations digest emails, we send your subscribers when you make a new recommendation.

Readers can unsubscribe anytime from this digest email, which won’t affect their subscription to your publication.

Learn more here if you’d like to opt out of being included in this digest.

How are recommended creators notified?

Recommended creators will receive an email notification letting them know that you are recommending their Substack.

If you recommend a creator who subscribes to your Substack, they’ll receive the email within an hour.

If the creator isn’t subscribed to you, they’ll receive the notification later when the recommendation has resulted in some new subscribers.

Tip: If you write a “Why I recommend” blurb, it’ll appear in this email.

Where can I see the impact of my recommendations?

The Recommendations tab shows who is recommending you, who you’re recommending, and the number of free subscriptions each recommendation has generated.

Tip: If you see an “Inactive” status on your Recommendations stats page, this means that this publication stopped recommending your publication or you’ve stopped recommending that publication. Any traffic will be displayed here regardless.

How do I add a cover photo to my welcome page?

A cover photo is an optional image used to introduce your publication on your Welcome page.

You can add a cover photo by heading to your publication’s Dashboard and clicking on Website editor in the left navigation bar. Head to the Welcome page section on the website editor and slide the toggle next to “Show navigation buttons”. clicking on Welcome page in the left navigation bar. Next to “Image”, upload an image from your device. Once you upload a welcome page photo, the options to display or hide your publication name and show your publication’s short description on your welcome page will be available.

Tip: We recommend this photo be at least 600x600px.

How do I add an endorsement to my Substack publication?

If you’ve set up Recommendations on your Substack, you can add endorsements to your Welcome page so that new readers can see why other creators on Substack recommend your publication.

Head to your Recommendations settings: your.substack.com

In the Settings section, click “Manage” next to “Recommendation blurbs on welcome page”.

Here, you’ll be able to view creators who have recommended you to their readers and a blurb about why they recommend your publication.

Select “Add Blurb” next to the endorsement that you’d like to display on your Welcome page. You can add up to 3 blurbs.

The blurbs you’ve selected will now appear on your Welcome page. New visitors are shown this page if they haven’t been to your publication before.

Note: Only Substack publications that have Recommendations enabled and have recommended your publication can be added as an endorsement to your Welcome page. Blurbs will not appear on the mobile version of Substack.

How do I add tags to a subscriber on Substack?

Note: Subscriber tags and post tags are different features. Post tags organize your posts and help readers navigate topics on your Substack.

Tags and segments lets you organize your subscriber list. With tags, you can label subscribers with information that lives outside of Substack and send emails to just the people you want to reach. This can be attendees from a live event or meetup, customers from a course or workshop, or VIPs you want to flag.

Tags are private labels you attach to individual subscribers. Only you can see them — subscribers never know they’ve been tagged. You can add unlimited tags per subscriber and edit or remove them at any time.

How to create tags

Create a tag when importing subscribers or directly on your Subscribers dashboard. On your Subscribers dashboard, click the three dots and select “Manage tags”.

Enter a tag name, then click the “+” to add it. Click “Done” and the tag saves automatically. To edit a tag, click the pencil icon; to delete one, click the trash can icon.

How to add tags

Tags can be added three ways: during import, by bulk-tagging a list, or on an individual subscriber.

During import

When you upload a CSV of subscribers, you can apply a tag to the whole batch as it comes in.

  • Go to Subscribers and click Add subscribers.

  • Enter emails manually or upload a CSV file.

  • Check the box next to Apply tags to imported subscribers. By bulk-tagging a filtered list

  • Go to Subscribers and apply a filter (for example: location, signup date, or paid status).

  • Select the filtered results and click on the three dots in the “X selected” box. Select Tag.

  • Apply or remove a tag from all selected subscribers at once. On an individual subscriber

  • Open a subscriber’s profile from your Subscribers list.

  • Click the three dots next to the subscriber’s email address. Select Tag.

  • Assign or remove a tag. How to see tagged subscribers

  • Go to your Subscribers dashboard and click Filter.

  • Select Tags from the type dropdown menu.

  • Select one or more tags to see the subscribers who have them. Alternatively, you can add a new tags column to your Subscribers dashboard. Click Display and select Tags from the drop down menu. Each tag you’ve applied to a subscriber will be shown in that individual’s row.

How do I change my subdomain?

To change your subdomain, head to your publication’s Settings page.

Scroll to the Danger Zone section and click on “Change subdomain” and enter the name of your new subdomain.

Note: Changing your subdomain will break all of your links. The easiest time to change your subdomain is before you’ve published anything.

How do I change my subdomain without breaking links?

To change your subdomain and redirect all links to your new URL, click on “Change subdomain without breaking links”. Changing your subdomain without breaking links can only be done once.

How do I change my Substack publication name?

Just created a publication or have an existing publication and want to change the name?

You can do that anytime by following these steps:

  1. Head to your publication’s Settings page.

  2. In the Basics section, delete the current text in “Publication name”.

Note: If you don’t see a “Publication name” option on your publication’s Settings page, your publication theme is likely set to “Profile”. To see the option to change your publication name, first change your publication theme to “Custom” and the setting will appear. Learn more here about publishing on Substack.

  1. Enter the new name and click “Save”.

  2. Your publication name will change immediately.

Tip: If you’d like to change your publication’s subdomain, follow the steps here.

How do I change the currency on subscription plans?

On Substack, you have the flexibility to set the default currency on how you’d like subscribers to pay.

To change the currency of paid subscribers on your publication, follow these steps:

  1. Head to your publication’s Settings page and click on Payments in the left navigation bar. Scroll to your subscription plans.

  2. Click on the currency drop-down menu next to a plan and select your preferred currency.

  3. Once you’ve selected your desired currency, select “Save”.

Moving forward, your new subscribers will see the plan pricing in your selected currency.

Tip: If you’d like readers to see subscription plan prices in their local currency, follow the steps here to enable localized pricing.

How do I configure a CNAME with my registrar?

Check with your domain registrar to configure your CNAME. Below are a few helpful docs for common registrars to get you started.

- Google domains: [Create a CNAME record](https://support.google.com/domains/answer/3290350?hl=en)
- GoDaddy: [Add a CNAME record](http://godaddy.com/help/add-a-cname-record-19236)
- Namecheap: [How to create a CNAME record](https://www.namecheap.com/support/knowledgebase/article.aspx/9646/2237/how-to-create-a-cname-record-for-your-domain/)
- Domain.com: [How to update a CNAME](https://www.domain.com/help/article/dns-management-how-to-update-cname-aliases)
- DreamHost: [Adding a CNAME](https://help.dreamhost.com/hc/en-us/articles/360035516812-Adding-custom-DNS-records)
- Bluehost: [What is a CNAME?](https://my.bluehost.com/hosting/help/resource/714)
- Hover: [Managing DNS records](https://help.hover.com/hc/en-us/articles/217282457-Managing-DNS-records-#h_5eab4aa7-b044-4cc6-a3c0-5869f583edc8)
- Dynadot: [How do I create a CNAME?](https://www.dynadot.com/community/help/question/create-CNAME)
- Cloudflare: If you have registered your domain through Cloudflare, make sure that the “Proxy status” on your CNAME record is set to “DNS only”. The orange cloud icon in the CNAME record row should be grey, not orange.

Need to link your custom domain to your Substack publication? Find a step by step guide here.

How do I connect Google Analytics 4 to my Substack publication?

Connecting Google Analytics to Substack allows you to track page views, sign ups, and paid subscriptions.

How do I get started?

  1. To start with Google Analytics, you’ll need to create an account and a property with a data stream.

You can find the complete steps including an instructional video on Google: [GA4] Set up Analytics for a website and/or app

  1. After you’ve finished the setup or if you already have an account with a property, locate the Measurement ID (also known as a Google tag) associated with your property’s data stream.

Step by step instructions with screenshots can be found on Google: [GA4] Find your Google tag ID

  1. Navigate to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Analytics section. Enter the measurement ID and click “Save”.

Tip: The Google tag will usually start with “G-”.

What happens next?

Events for page views, sign ups, and paid subscriptions should begin to show up in your Google Analytics account after 24 hours.

How do I create a publication on Substack?

New to Substack? Follow these steps to create your first publication.

  1. Create an account by clicking on Create account on Substack.com.

  2. Set up your profile with a photo, your first name, and a short bio.

  3. Once you create an account, you’ll land on Substack Home.

  4. Click on the “+” sign and select “Post”. You’ll be taken to your Substack where you can create content and manage your Substack.

If you’d like to begin with a simpler publishing experience, you can post directly from your Substack profile on the Substack app. Learn more here: How can I publish on Substack?

Note: When you create a publication for the first time, it will be default public. To make your publication private, click on Settings and slide the toggle to the right next to “Privacy mode”.

Can I have multiple publications attached to the same Stripe account?

At the moment, it’s not possible to link two publications to the same Stripe account; you must have a separate Stripe account for each publication.

How do I delete my Substack publication?

Need help on deleting your Substack?

  1. Log into your account on Substack’s homepage under Sign In or directly at this link.

  2. Go to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Danger Zone.

  3. Select Delete your Publication and your content and email list will be permanently deleted.

Find more help on how to export your posts and how to export your email or subscriber list before deleting your publication.

Note: If you are unable to find the ‘Danger Zone’ section in your publication’s settings to delete your publication, it may be due to the following reasons:

  • You need to turn off paid subscriptions and disconnect your Stripe account if you have a paid publication.
  • You may not be in the correct section of the Settings page. Please ensure that you are on your Writers Dashboard Settings page. Which can be found by clicking “Dashboard” on the top right corner of the page.

How do I edit email headers and footers on Substack?

Any publication can add a banner, header, or footer to their publication emails. Banners only appear in emails, not on posts.

To make edits:

  1. Navigate to your publication’s Settings page and click on Emails in the left navigation bar.

  2. Select “Edit” next to “Email header & footer”.

  3. On this page, you can set up header and footer content for free subscribers and paid subscribers.

  4. Uploading a banner image is optional and we recommend 1100x220 PNG for the best results.

Tip: To see the various email layouts, send yourself a test email by entering your email address under “Send test emails”.

Tip: A mailing address is shown at the bottom of all emails. It can be a PO Box. You can edit that in the Details section of your settings page.

How do I edit my About page for my Substack publication?

Your Substack’s About page is a place to tell new readers who you are and what you write about in greater length than your publication’s one-line description. The About page’s goal is to establish credibility with your readers while showing off your writing voice and style.

Head to the Website section on your publication’s Settings page and select “Edit” next to “About page”.

On your About page you can add:

About: What your publication is about, such as topic areas and post types. This description should also imply what your publication does for readers and your intended audience.

A brief personal bio: Who you are and why you’re qualified.

Publication logistics: How often you publish and for how much.

Subscriber benefits: What free or paid subscribers get.

You can polish a simple About page with any of the following additions:

Images of yourself and/or your subject matter: Images help define your publication’s brand, attach a face to your writing, and break up large blocks of text.

Testimonials from subscribers: Collect and publish compliments from your subscribers about why they love your publication.

Highlighted posts or unlocked paid posts: Links to your favorite posts you’ve written. For paid creators, it may be useful to make an old paid post free again and link it to tease your paid work.

Special offers: A link to special offer campaigns or student discounts.

A personal video: Welcome new readers by sharing a personal message directly on your Substack account.

Click on the video icon to upload a video file or record a video. Once your video is ready, select Insert. You can move and place the video embed into any part of the About page draft.

How do I export my email list on Substack?

To export your email list on Substack, head to your Subscribers page and scroll down to “All subscribers”.

Click on the three dots above your subscribers list and select “Export”.

Select whether to download a .csv of your subscriber list with all columns or visible columns only.

  • All columns: These columns can vary from when the subscriber last opened an email to their individual post views.
  • Visible columns only: These columns are the default columns (such as the date someone subscribed and their type of plan) and the options you’ve selected using the Filter feature. If you’d like to export details for a specific subscriber or a select group of subscribers, first click on the checkbox next to their subscriber email on your Subscribers page.

After clicking on the checkbox next to the the subscriber’s email, a new box will appear. Select the three dots and click on “Export”. Choose whether to export all columns or export visible columns only.

Is it possible to export a file with the full names of subscribers?

It’s not currently possible to export a file with subscriber names.

How do I export my posts?

Anything you publish on Substack is yours to own.

To download your posts, subscriber list, and related statistics, head to your publication’s Settings page and click on Exports in the left navigation bar.

Select “Create new export” in the Exports section.

A zip file containing your publication’s data will be generated which you can download in this section. Once the report is ready, you’ll be notified by email.

How do I import my mailing list from another platform, such as Mailchimp, Ghost, or Beehiiv?

Have a list of emails you’d like to import to your Substack account? Follow these steps to get started.

If don’t have any subscribers to your Substack yet:

  1. On your account Home, click on the Dashboard icon (graph icon right above your profile picture).

  2. Click on Subscribers in the left sidebar and select “Add subscribers”.

  3. Scroll to “Add subscribers by upload” and import a mailing list by dragging and dropping or selecting “Choose file” to upload a .csv, .xlsx, or .ods file containing the email addresses.

Not sure how to get a CSV? Find instructions for Mailchimp, Ghost, Medium, Patreon, Beehiiv, ConvertKit, Flodesk, Tinyletter, and Mailerlite.

  1. Once you upload a file, confirm that these subscribers have opted in to receiving emails and select their subscription type if you have payments enabled.

If you’re importing paid subscribers and would like to ensure they don’t lose access after your transition to Substack, you can offer these readers a trial.

Note: After the trial ends, they’ll be prompted to enter their payment information into Substack to keep receiving your newsletter.

  1. Select “Import” and your mailing list will begin importing immediately to your account. Depending on the size of your list, it may take a few minutes.

  2. Refresh the page and you’ll be able to see the count for emails that were successfully added and those that were skipped. Duplicate emails will not be imported.

Tip: Emails may be skipped and not imported if they have an invalid email domain such as gnail.com instead of gmail.com.

If you already have subscribers to your Substack but want to add more:

  1. Head over to the Subscribers tab and scroll down to All Subscribers.

  2. Select “Add subscribers” and click on “Add subscribers by upload” to import more emails in one step.

How do I delete an imported list?

To delete an imported list visit the support widget and ask for a human support specialist to assist you with your request.

How do I import my posts from another platform, such as Mailchimp, WordPress, Medium, or Ghost?

Substack enables seamless content imports from the following services: Medium, Ghost, Wordpress, Mailchimp, Beehiiv, SeekingAlpha, Tumblr, and Blogspot. Just paste in your blog or newsletter URL to get started.

If you use a different platform from the ones listed, check if they have an RSS feed. Our post importer also takes in RSS feeds from sites like Squarespace and ConvertKit.

Things to note:

  • Some platforms will only include the most recent posts, such as the latest 20 posts, in their RSS.
  • Here are detailed guides on moving from Ghost to Substack or Mailchimp to Substack or Beehiv to Substack.

  1. Log into your account and navigate to your publication’s Settings page. Click on Import/Export in the left navigation bar.

  2. Click on “Import posts”.

  3. Paste the URL of the website you’d like to import, such as tinyletter.com or medium.com. If you’re importing your posts from Mailchimp, use your email campaign archive as the URL.

Tip: If you’re importing content from Seeking Alpha, use the following URL: http://seekingalpha.com/author/<author_id>. Replace “author_id” with your Seeking Alpha author name. For example: http://seekingalpha.com/author/hamish-best

  1. Once the Substack importer has found your archive, select “Import”. If you have payments enabled on your Substack publication, you’ll see the option to import the posts as paid posts. This means that the posts on Substack will be automatically paywalled to free subscribers. If you re-import posts to Substack, select the “Update existing posts” checkbox to update the current posts with the newly imported version.

Note: Importing posts from Mailchimp, Webflow, or Tumblr requires you to upload an export of your posts from those platforms. When importing from Mailchimp, please be sure to upload the Campaigns zip file–don’t unzip it first.

  1. Confirm that you own the publication that you’re importing and agree to Substack’s Terms of Use and Privacy Policy. Select “Next” and your posts will appear in your Home and Archive on your Substack publication.

Imported posts may appear with a platform’s subscribe options or a banner at the bottom of a post. To remove the unnecessary text, head to your Posts page and select the three dots next to your imported post.

Delete the selected text or image and choose “Continue” to update your changes. Select your audience and if you’d like to send the imported post as an email to your Substack subscribers and the Substack app.

How do I import my posts manually?

If you’re running into issues with exporting a file or importing it to your Substack publication, you can copy-and-paste each post into the Substack editor.

Once each post has been published, you can go into that post’s settings and change the publication date so that it matches the date of original publication.

How do I manually add a new subscriber to my mailing list?

If you’d like to add a new subscriber to your mailing list, you can do that by manually entering their email address or importing a list of email addresses.

  1. Sign into your Substack and head to your publication’s Subscribers. Scroll down to the “Add Subscriber(s)” button.

  2. If you have a free Substack, add an email address and select “Add emails”.

  3. If you have payments enabled, select from the drop-dox menu whether the new subscriber is on a free subscription or a trial.

  4. Check the box next to “Send a welcome email” if you’d like to your new reader to receive a customized welcome message.

Tip: Editing the free subscriber welcome message will change the welcome email that all new free readers receive when they subscribe to your publication.

If you’re trying to manually add a subscriber via their email and see this message, “No valid emails found. This could be because an email previously unsubscribed”, it likely means that that they used to be a subscriber to your publication and unsubscribed themselves. If you see this message, they’ll need to re-subscribe to your publication by visiting your publication and clicking on “Subscribe”.

How do I move from Beehiiv to Substack?

If you’re ready to move from Beehiiv to Substack and experience growth with Recommendations, engage with other creators and readers in Notes, or start a paid podcast, this guide will show you what you need to do to transition your subscribers and posts to your new publication seamlessly.

In this guide, we’ll explain:

  • What you need from your Beehiiv account
  • How to get started on Substack

What do I need to do on Beehiiv?

To get started, you’ll need to export your Beehiiv subscribers and find your Beehiiv URL.

Exporting all subscribers

On your Beehiiv dashboard, select Settings and then Export Data. Click on the “Export Subscribers (Full Version).
