Once the export is complete, you can download a .csv file of your Beehiiv subscribers in the Historical Exports section on the same page.

Finding your Beehiiv URL

On your Beehiiv dashboard, select Settings and then Domains. Copy the address under “Web URL”.

If you’re using a custom domain on Beehiiv, make sure to grab the website URL that has your Beehiiv root domain (subdomain.beehiv.com).

How do I get started on Substack?

Start your move to Substack by first creating an account. Head to the Import/Export section on your publication’s Settings page: your.substack.com

How to import your posts

Click “Import posts” and you’ll be prompted to enter your Beehiiv URL and also upload a .csv of your posts. Beehiiv will only export your free posts and previews of your paid posts. If you have payments enabled on your Substack publication, you can choose to make the imported posts only for paying subscribers.

To export your posts from Beehiiv, head to your Settings and click Export Data. Then click “Export all Posts.”

Once your Beehiiv archive has been imported to your Substack publication, you can view the posts in the Home or Archive tabs.

Can I import premium articles from Beehiiv to Substack?

Option 1: Manually copy and paste your Beehiiv premium articles as a new post on your Substack publication. Before you publish the post on Substack, set the audience to “Paid subscribers only” and uncheck the box next to “Delivery- Send via email and the Substack app”. This will only publish the post to the web version of your publication.

If you’d like to change the date of a post, click “Edit post” and select “Settings”. Scroll to the date picker under Displayed publication date and change it to your preferred date.

Option 2: Make your Beehiiv premium articles available to free subscribers and start the import posts process on Substack. Once your posts have been imported, you can automatically paywall your archive. If you paywall your archive on Substack but would like to offer some free posts, you can just head to the post’s settings and remove the paywall.

How to import your free Beehiiv subscribers

On your publication’s Settings page, scroll to “Import email addresses” and click on “Import emails”. Upload the .csv file of subscribers you downloaded from your Beehiiv account. Subscribers will have a “free” status on your Substack Subscribers dashboard.

Tip: After you’ve added your Beehiiv subscribers, you can view them on your Substack Subscribers page.

How do I offer a complimentary subscription to a reader on Substack?

You can offer complimentary paid subscriptions to readers from your publication’s Subscribers page.

  1. On your Subscribers page, select which readers you’d like give a paid subscription to and click on the three dots in the banner that appears. Select “Comp”.

  2. From the drop-down menu, choose how long you’d like the reader’s complimentary paid subscription to last for. You’ll have a choice of 7 days, 30 days, 90 days, 6 months, 1 year, or forever.

To grant a subscriber Founding Member status, check the box next to “Set to Founding”.

Tip: To extend all of your paying subscribers’ benefits, first apply a filter where type is “Paid” to locate all your paid readers. Then follow the steps above to extend their paid status.

What happens at the end of a complimentary subscription period?

The subscriber will receive reminder emails from your publication one week before and the day before the complimentary subscription ends with the option to continue as a paid subscriber. This email is not customizable.

Once the complimentary subscription ends, the subscriber will receive an email letting them know that their paid subscription has ended. However, you can customize this email by following these steps.

  1. Head to your publication’s Settings page and click on Payments in the left navigation bar.

  2. Next to “Paid subscription expired email”, select “Edit”.

  3. What you see on this page will be the default message subscribers receive when their paid subscriptions end. Change the copy as you’d like and when ready, click “Save”.

How do I offer a discount to my Substack?

Interested in offering a discount on your Substack publication? Follow these steps to create a unique link that you can share with your readers.

Note: Discounts can only be applied to monthly and annual subscription plans.

  1. Sign into your Substack account and navigate to your publication’s Settings page. Click on Payments in the left navigation bar.

  2. Next to “Special offers” click on “Manage special offers”.

  3. Select “New offer”.

  4. Enter the name of the special offer and fill out details such as the discount amount, duration, and also advanced options. These advanced options include customizing the end date of the offer to limiting the special subscription discount to only email addresses from education institutions.

  5. When you’re ready, click “Create” and a unique link will be generated.

Note: If you enable “Automatically apply to all new subscribers”, every new subscriber moving forward will see the subscriptions plans offered at the discounted rate.

What will readers see when they click on the discount link?

When readers click on your link, they’ll see the length of the special offer remaining (if you’ve entered an offer expiration date) and see your publication’s subscription plans.

How can I see if an offer has been redeemed?

Head to your Special Offers dashboard at: your.substack.com

Here, you’ll be able to see the terms of your special offer, when it expires, and how many times it’s been redeemed.

How do I offer a free trial to my publication?

Interested in offering a free trial to your publication? Follow these steps to to create a unique link that you can share with your readers.

  1. Log in to your Substack account and navigate to your Settings page. Click on Payments in the left navigation bar.

  2. Next to “Special offers” click on “Manage special offers”.

  3. Select “New special offer”.

  4. Under “Terms”, choose “Free trial” and fill out details such as the special offer name and description.

  5. When you’re ready, click “Create offer” and a unique link will be generated.

  • Copy link will automatically copy the URL to your clipboard.
  • Send by email will create a new draft post which you can publish and send via email to your readers. What will readers see when they click on the free trial link?

When readers click on your link, they’ll see the length of the free trial and see your publication’s subscription plans.

Note: To redeem and start their free trial, readers must enter their card details. If they’d like to cancel their subscription, they can do that by following these steps.

To enable a 7-day free trial on all your paid posts, check out this article here.

How do I offer group subscriptions?

If you think that organizations, companies, or other institutions might want to buy multiple subscriptions for their members, you can provide group subscriptions.

In this guide, we’ll show you how to:

  • Manage group subscriptions
  • Create a group subscription discount
  • Share group subscriptions with your readers

Managing group subscriptions

Head to your Subscribers page and click on the three dots. Select “Manage group subscriptions”. Alternatively, head directly to this link: your.substack.com

Here, you’ll be able to see your current group subscriptions and the number of seats filled per group subscription.

Click on each individual Admin to see more details about the group subscription the payment history, status of invoices, and links to the invoices on Stripe.

Creating a group discount

For a target audience

On your group subscriptions page, click on “Create new group offer”. On the group offers page, select whether you’d like to offer an unlimited number of seats for a fixed price or a group discount per seat.

Note: The set minimum number of seats for a discount per seat offer is 2 seats. You can enter any number higher than the set minimum. If you select Unlimited offer type, you will not see the seat or percentage off options.

After clicking on “Send offer”, the group admin will automatically receive an email about the offer.

Clicking on the offer will send the group administrator to a subscription page. Here, they’ll be able to enter a credit card or see the option to pay by ACH.

You can keep track of your pending group subscription offers at this link: your.substack.com

For any group

If you’d like to offer a default discount that can be used by anyone who is purchasing a group subscription, head to your publication’s Settings and click on Payments in the left navigation bar. Scroll to the Group Subscriptions section and select “Add a group discount”.

When a new reader subscribes to your publication and sees your plan offerings, they’ll see the discount automatically applied when clicking on the Group Subscription option.

Letting your readers know about group subscriptions

For regular annual subscriptions

Group subscriptions for annual plans are available via your.substack.com (replace “your” with the name of your Substack publication) and an administrator can subscribe with a single card.

You can also let readers know about group subscriptions by adding a button in a post. On the post editor, select Button and select Get a group subscription. Readers can then click on the button and sign up a group with the default discount.

For discounted annual subscriptions

Head to your Group Subscriptions page and create a new offer outlined in the steps above. A group administrator will automatically be emailed the offer and can subscribe their group right away.

What happens next?

Once the group subscription admin subscribes to the publication, they can enter the group’s emails on the Manage subscription page of your publication. Learn more here on the next steps of group subscriptions.

Note: Group subscription administrators can add a new seat or purchase more seats to the group subscription anytime. However, unlimited group subscriptions will not see the option to purchase additional seats.

Can individuals who already have a subscription be added to a group subscription?

It’s not possible to convert an individual subscription to a group subscription. They’ll need to cancel their subscription in order to be added to a group.

Once the individual has canceled their paid subscription, the group administrator can do the following:

  • For a new group: Subscribe a group, keeping the number of seats in mind, and in the Group Subscription section add the individuals using the same email addresses that the individuals use for their current subscriptions.
  • For an existing group: In their Group Subscription section, add the individuals as new members using the same email addresses that the individuals use for their current subscriptions.

How do I opt out of the gift subscription referral program?

If you’re not interested in participating in the gift subscription referral program, please follow these steps:

  1. Log into your Substack account and click on your publication’s Settings column.

  2. Navigate to the Growth column on your publication Settings page and slide the toggle to the left next to “Enable paid subscriber gifts”.

What happens after I opt out of the gift subscription referral program?

Once you disable the referral program on your publication, future paid subscribers will not be able to refer new readers and current referrers won’t be able to access the Gift Rewards page that allows them to send gift subscriptions.

How do I organize the navigation bar on my Substack publication?

You can add links to the top navigation bar that lead readers to pages on and off Substack.

The navigation bar displays and links to the following by default:

  • Home: Your publication’s homepage
  • Archive: All your publication’s posts
  • About: Your publication’s About page Tip: If you have a Substack podcast, chat, or reader referrals enabled, these can also be displayed in the navigation bar.

Follow these steps to customize your navigation bar:

  1. Head to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Website section.

  2. In the Navigation bar section, add a link by selecting “Add item”. To show or hide any default navigation bar links, slide the toggle to the left or right in the “Visible” column.

  3. Under Title, enter what you’d like the header in your bar to be displayed as. Enter the link URL and select “Add”.

  4. To edit or delete a link you’ve added, select the three dots on the right and make your selection.

  5. To add a drop-down menu to your publication’s navigation bar, drag and drop an item from the navigation bar section and add a group title.

Tips for the navigation bar:

  • Custom pages and tags you create in your Settings can also be added to your navigation bar. These can be removed from your navigation bar by selecting the three dots and clicking “Delete”. This will remove the page from the navigation bar– if you’d like to fully delete a page, you can do so in the “Custom pages” section.
  • If your Substack publication has sections, these headers will appear in the navigation bar.
  • While you can drag and drop navigation bar items you create to change their order, select built-in pages like Podcast and About can’t be reordered.

How do I pin a post to the top of my publication’s homepage?

On your publication’s home page, navigate to the pencil icon in the hero (top part of the homepage) and click the pencil icon in the top-right corner.

This will open the website editor, where you can choose which section of your homepage you’d like to pin a post to.

Click on a post in the layout to select it. The right-hand panel will then appear, allowing you to choose a different post to pin.

Select a new post from the right-hand panel. It will appear in the layout with an orange pin icon in the top-right corner, indicating it’s pinned to your homepage.

When you’re finished, click “Save” to apply your changes.

Alternatively, navigate to the post you’d like to pin to the top of your homepage and click the three dots next to the title. Select “Pin to home page” and this post will now be what readers see when they view your publication’s homepage.

How do I see a list of contributors to my publication?

If you have multiple public contributors or authors on your Substack, you can see your publication’s People section at your.substack.com (replace your with the name of your publication) or by following the steps below:

  • Navigate to your publication’s About page (https://your.substack.com/about).
  • Scroll down to People. To hide who appears in your People section, head to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Team section.

Under visibility next to their name, select “Private. Their profile card will not be hidden from the People section on your About page.

How do I set a custom theme for my Substack?

Christene of A Tiny Apt. uses the website editor to customize her Substack theme

Using the website theme editor, you can customize your branding and layouts to make your Substack unique. The Website editor can be accessed directly at this link: your.substack.com

When signed in to your publication, select Website editor in the left dashboard. Alternatively, you can access the website theme editor by heading to your Substack’s Settings page and clicking on Website. Ensure that “Custom theme” is selected in the “Publication theme” drop-down menu.

Home Page

Here, customize your Substack’s home page:

  • Colors: Add color to your site’s background or select a color accent for items like the “Subscribe” button.
  • Typography: Pick a font for post titles that matches your writing’s personality.

Header

Logos: In the website editor, establish your brand with your logo and a wordmark featuring your publication name. Logos should be at least 256 x 256 pixels and wordmarks should be a horizontal image at least 1344 x 256 pixels.

Appearance: Select how you’d like your publication name or wordmark (if you uploaded one) to appear on your homepage header.

  • Standard: This is the default header layout where the publication name or wordmark appears above the navigation bar.

  • Wide: The publication name or wordmark will appear in the center of the homepage and the navigation bar will appear on the left side of the homepage.

  • Hamburger: The navigation bar will appear in a three line drop-down menu on the left side of the homepage.

  • Inline: The publication name or wordmark and the navigation bar will appear on the left side of the homepage. Navigation bar: Select how you’d like the navigation bar to appear to subscribers and viewers. The following options are available if you select Standard, Wide, or Inline as your header layout.

  • Text: The navigation bar will appear as text in the header.

  • Button: The navigation bar will appear as buttons. Tip: If you’d like to re-size your wordmark and make it smaller or larger, click and drag the corners to your desired size.

Hero

The Hero section determines the post or posts that appear the top of your homepage

  • Feature: Here, your single most recent or pinned post will be at the top.
  • Newspaper: Here, three pinned posts and five top posts will appear at the top.
  • Magazine: Here, five of your most recent posts or pinned posts will appear at the top.
  • Media feature: The most recent post will headline the top of your homepage.
  • Highlight: Here, your single most recent or pinned post will be at the top. The three most recent posts will appear on the side.
  • Podcast options: If you publish podcast episodes on Substack, showcase your podcast above the fold. You can select whether to show your top posts and podcast links.

Body

The body of the homepage lives beneath the hero section. Here, you can select between three preset layouts: list, grid, or groups (tags or sections). However, you can access more advanced customization by adding content blocks.

While on the Home page tab, scroll to Layout and check the box next to “Use custom body layout”. Once the box is checked, the Body tab name will change to Posts & Modules.

Posts & Modules

You can get started by adding a content block below your Hero section.

Posts: Your posts can appear as a list or grid.

  • Source: Select whether to display recent posts, tagged posts, or individual sections.
  • Rows: Select whether you’d like your content block to display 1-4 rows of posts.
  • Title: If you’ve selected Tags or Sections as a source, you can change the title by checking the box next to “Use override” and enter a new title in the text box. Note that you can add multiple content blocks to your homepage (up to 10), and mix-and-match as best serves your publication. For example, you could add a content of the latest news, a Feature of a specific series, and a List of recent posts.

Modules: Add a module to the left or right side of the content block by clicking on “+ Add module”.

  • Contributors: the latest posts from contributors to your publication listed as “public” in your publication’s settings.
  • Links: creators use this to list all their published books, social media accounts, resources, and posts’ greatest hits.
  • Podcasts: if you publish a podcast on your Substack.
  • Recommendations: a place to showcase the Substack publications you love and would like to share with your audience.
  • Subscribe: a customizable banner prompt to encourage visitors to subscribe.

Posts

Select new fonts for how you want titles and the bodies of your posts to appear. You can also select whether you want links to appear as underlined or as colored links.

Footer

Customize the footer of your homepage by displaying your publication name, social media links, or choosing a background color.

Podcasts

If you have a publication podcast, you’ll see the option to customize the layout for your podcast homepage. If you have a podcast section on your Substack, the Podcast tab won’t appear on the website editor.

You can choose between multiple hero options:

  • Default left: Your title and description will be aligned to the left.
  • Feature media: Your latest podcast episodes will be headlined. In the Body section, select if you want your podcast episodes to appear in a list or a grid (Latest, Top, Discussions) layout.

Sections

Here, you can select to show your section logo and description or spotlight your latest posts.

Select in Body whether you’d like to display the section posts in a list or grid (Latest, Top, Discussions) layout.

How do I set up a Creator Kit on Substack?

Creator Kits let you build a shareable media kit to attract brand partners through Substack’s native sponsorships program. Creator Kits are currently available to all Bestsellers publications, or those with at least 100 paid subscribers. If your publication qualifies, you’ll see a Sponsorships tab in your publication dashboard.

Eligible publishers can turn on their Creator Kit now to express interest in working with brands. If there’s a potential match, the Substack team may contact you by email. Enabling your Creator Kit also lets us know you’re interested in joining our upcoming partnership platform, where you’ll soon be able to connect directly with brands investing in the Substack creator ecosystem.

If you haven’t built your paid subscription base yet, here are some resources to help you grow your audience.

Setting up your Creator Kit is entirely optional.

How to create your Creator Kit

  • Sign in to your Substack account and go to your publication’s Home.
  • If you are a Substack Bestseller, you can click “Go to Sponsorships” in the Announcements banner — or click Sponsorships in the left sidebar.
  • Click “Build creator kit”.

What to fill out in your Creator Kit

Your Creator Kit has multiple sections to help brands understand what your Substack is about and how it’s performing. The publication description, stats, formats, and about sections are required. All other sections are optional and will automatically hide if left blank. You can update your Creator Kit at any time.

Header

The header displays your publication name automatically. You can edit:

  • Your name.
  • A short description of your publication.
  • Your Substack type and publishing cadence (tap the pills to select the options that apply). About

The About section lets you control what metrics and content brands see.

  • Click “Edit” to choose which stats to display, including subscriber count, open rate, and number of active readers.
  • Write a longer description of your publication’s focus and audience.
  • Select which posts to feature. You can also edit the section title. Audience

The Audience section shows brands where your subscribers are located and what they’re interested in.

  • Write a short description to help brands identify their customers in your audience.
  • Substack automatically displays your subscribers’ top countries, top US states, and reader interest categories (such as Food & Drink and Culture) based on subscriber data. Brand partnerships

If you already work with brands, list them here.

  • Click “Add brand”.
  • Enter the brand’s logo, name, website link, and a short description of the partnership. Testimonials

The Testimonials section lets you include quotes about your publication from readers, friends, or family.

  • Copy and paste testimonials manually from any source like other Substacks, creators, or notable people.
  • You can select up to four subscriber notes from the list Substack provides. When a subscriber signs up for a paid subscription plan, they have the option to write a message that only you can see. Not interested

Use this section to describe the types of brands or sponsorships you do not want to work with.

  • This section is private and is not included in the Creator Kit that brands see.
  • Only Substack can view your Not interested notes and uses them to improve brand matching. Sponsorship formats

Select from ten available sponsorship formats to show brands how you prefer to collaborate.

  • Next to “Minimum spend”, enter the minimum budget you’d consider for a sponsorship opportunity.
  • Your selections indicate what types of partnerships you are open to.
  • Example: a paywalled post that a sponsor can make free for all subscribers.

What happens next

Once your Creator Kit is complete, you may be contacted via email by the Substack team if there is a brand that could be a fit. Creator Kits are not public and your subscribers cannot view them. Only brands that are part of the sponsorship program and who you’ve been connected with by the Substack team can view your Creator Kits.

Over time, Creator Kits will become part of a broader partnership platform designed to help connect publishers with brands more directly. For now, all matchmaking is handled by the Substack team as we continue to learn and refine the program

You can always edit your Creator Kit by clicking on the Sponsorships tab on your publication’s Dashboard and selecting “Edit creator kit”.

Tip: Once you’ve published your Creator Kit, you can send a demographics survey to your subscribers to learn more about them. You can add a summary of the survey in your own words to your creator kit in the free text box in the Audience section.

How do I set up gift subscriptions on Substack?

Readers can gift paid subscriptions to other readers by navigating to your.substack.com (Replace “your” with your publication subdomain).

What happens next?

Once they purchase a gift subscription, the gift recipient will receive an email and be able to access paid content immediately. If they don’t have a Substack account, one will be created for them automatically, and they can sign into your Substack publication to start reading.

You’ll receive a new paid subscriber email if you have the “New paid subscriber” notification setting toggled on in your publication’s Settings page. The source will appear as “gift” in the email and the new paid subscriber’s status will appear as “gift” on your Subscribers dashboard.

What happens when the gift subscription ends?

Before the gift subscription ends, we’ll send reminder emails to the recipient on your behalf a week before and a day before it ends with the opportunity to purchase a paid subscription or subscribe as a free reader. If no action is taken, the recipient will be automatically unsubscribed from your publication on the expiration date.

Learn more on how your Substack can upsell gift subscriptions: How can I promote gift subscriptions during the holidays?

How do I set up my custom domain on Substack?

Do you have a custom domain you’d like to use for your Substack publication? Get started by linking your domain to Substack by following these steps.

Note: Substack charges a one-time $50 USD fee to use a custom domain for your publication. You’ll need to be the publication’s owner or group administrator to connect the custom domain. Sales tax or VAT is not charged for the Substack custom domain fee.

  1. Navigate to your publication’s Settings page and click on Domain in the left navigation bar. Select the “Add custom domain” button.

Tip: You must complete this step and set up your domain on Substack before adding the CNAME alias in your DNS provider.

  1. Enter your domain, and you’ll be provided with the correct CNAME alias to add to your DNS provider.

Below are links to popular provider support pages on CNAME records.

  • Google domains: Create a CNAME record
  • GoDaddy: Add a CNAME record
  • Namecheap: How to create a CNAME record
  • Domain.com: How to update a CNAME
  • DreamHost: Adding a CNAME
  • Bluehost: What is a CNAME?
  • Hover: Managing DNS records
  • Dynadot: How do I create a CNAME?
  • Cloudflare: If you have registered your domain through Cloudflare, ensure the “Proxy status” on your CNAME record is set to “DNS only”. The orange cloud icon in the CNAME record row should be grey, not orange. Tip: What Substack calls “Name” and “Target” may have different labels on your registrar’s setup page. What we call “Name” can be called “Host” or “Subdomain,” and sometimes “Target” is labeled “Points to.”

Here’s a quick video on how to add a CNAME on GoDaddy:

  1. To manually check whether or not the configuration is ready, use the “check status” button in the Custom Domain section on your Settings page.

How long does it take for the configuration to work?

Once your custom domain is linked to Substack, it can take up to 36 hours to fully configure. If it’s been over 36 hours, there may be an issue in the configuration.

First, check in your configuration settings that you’ve started off your host name with a subdomain such as “www.”

You may need to delete the configuration and start over (you won’t be charged again).

What should I enter in the TTL section?

Your provider may ask you to configure a TTL, which you can leave alone.

Can I use my root domain without “www” or another subdomain?

We require that your Substack publication be hosted by a subdomain such as “www.” You can use a 301 redirect to forward the root domain version of your custom domain to your newsletter. You can read more about custom domains here.

Will having a custom domain send my newsletters from the domain?

No, even if you change your custom domain, you will not get your own Substack inbox. The newsletters will always be sent [publicationdomain]@substack.com.

Can I access my publication while setting up a custom domain?

Even if you feel you have temporarily lost access to your Substack dashboard due to your custom domain, you can access your publication settings by logging in and going directly to [yoursubdomain].substack.com/publish/settings

Can I view my receipt for my custom domain?

You can view your custom domain receipt by going to your publication dashboard and navigating to Settings -> Domain -> Custom domain payment receipt. Then select View receipt.

How do I share posts or publications from other creators on my Substack?

Have a creator you’d like to share with your Substack audience?

By embedding a link into your post, your readers will be able to see a card-style preview which links to other Substack posts and publications.

How do I embed a link to share a publication?

  1. Copy the creator’s Substack URL and paste it directly into a new post.

  2. Choose how you’d like the card to appear by selecting a layout icon on top of the embed.

  • Horizontal icon: Clicking on the card will send readers directly to the creator’s Substack main page.
  • Vertical icon: A subscribe button will appear.

How do I embed a link to share a post?

To share a post, grab a post’s URL and paste it directly into your draft post. This will embed a linked preview of the post’s first few lines.

Can I embed my own posts in a new post?

Yes! When creating a new text post, you can insert links to past free and/or paid posts. By doing this, you can quote and share past content and also lets readers get a sneak peek of a paid post.

Grab a post’s URL and paste it directly into your draft post. Click on the hamburger menu and select how you’d like your embed to appear.

A small display will display the social preview image.

A medium display will show the first few lines of the post and the social preview image.

A large display will display the social preview image and a snippet of the post. To edit the caption that appears in the large display embed, click on “Edit caption” and once the edit is ready, select “Save”.

If you’d prefer the original card-style preview, select “Convert to default”.

Tip: If you’d prefer to embed a direct link/URL with no preview or image, select “Convert to link”. If you select the “Convert to link” or “Convert to default” options, the re-sizing menu will disappear and you’ll need to delete the embedded post and re-enter it into the draft post again to see the size options.

How do I switch my publication’s homepage to a different layout?

Does your publication’s homepage need a lift? Try upgrading your homepage with the different layouts in just a few clicks.

When signed in to your publication, select Website editor in the left dashboard. Alternatively, you can access the website theme editor by heading to your Substack’s Settings page and clicking on Website. Ensure that “Custom theme” is selected in the “Publication theme” drop-down menu.

Home Page

Here, customize your Substack’s home page:

  • Colors: Add color to your site’s background or select a color accent for items like the “Subscribe” button.
  • Typography: Pick a font for post titles that matches your writing’s personality. Header

Logos: Establish your brand with your logo and a wordmark featuring your publication name. Logos should be at least 256 x 256 pixels and wordmarks should be a horizontal image at least 1344 x 256 pixels.

Appearance: Select how you’d like your publication name or wordmark to appear on your homepage header.

  • Stacked: This is the original header layout where the publication name or wordmark appears above the navigation bar

  • Wide: The publication name or wordmark will appear in the center of the homepage and the navigation bar will appear on the left side of the homepage.

  • Hamburger: The navigation bar will appear in a three line drop-down menu on the left side of the homepage.

  • Inline: The publication name or wordmark and the navigation bar will appear on the left side of the homepage. Navigation bar: Select how you’d like the navigation bar to appear to subscribers and viewers. The following options are available if you select Stacked, Wide, or Inline as your header layout.

  • Text: The navigation bar will appear as text in the header

  • Button: The navigation bar will appear as buttons Tip: If you’d like to re-size your wordmark and make it smaller or larger, click and drag the corners to your desired size.

Hero

The Hero section determines the post or posts that appear the top of your homepage

  • Feature: Here, your single most recent or pinned post will be at the top.
  • Newspaper: Here, three pinned posts and five top posts will appear at the top.
  • Magazine: Here, five of your most recent posts or pinned posts will appear at the top.
  • Media feature: The most recent post will headline the top of your homepage.
  • Hilight: Here, your single most recent or pinned post will be at the top. The three most recent posts will appear on the side.
  • Podcast options: If you publish podcast episodes on Substack, showcase your podcast above the fold. You can select whether to show your top posts and podcast links.

Body

The body of the homepage lives beneath the hero section. Here, you can select between three preset layouts: list, grid, or groups (tags or sections). However, you can access more advanced customization by adding content blocks.

While on the Home page tab, scroll to Layout and check the box next to “Use custom body layout”. Once the box is checked, the Body tab name will change to Posts & Modules.

Posts & Modules

You can get started by adding a content block below your Hero section.

Posts: Your posts can appear as a list or grid.

  • Source: Select whether to display recent posts, tagged posts, or individual sections.
  • Rows: Select whether you’d like your content block to display 1-4 rows of posts.
  • Title: If you’ve selected Tags or Sections as a source, you can change the title by checking the box next to “Use override” and enter a new title in the text box. Note that you can add multiple content blocks to your homepage (up to 10), and mix-and-match as best serves your publication. For example, you could add a content block of the latest news, a Feature of a specific series, and a List of recent posts.

Modules: Add a module to the left or right side of the content block by clicking on “+ Add module”.

  • Contributors: the latest posts from contributors to your publication listed as “public” in your publication’s settings.
  • Links: creators use this to list all their published books, social media accounts, resources, and posts’ greatest hits.
  • Podcasts: if you publish a podcast on your Substack.
  • Recommendations: a place to showcase the Substack publications you love and would like to share with your audience.
  • Subscribe: a customizable banner prompt to encourage visitors to subscribe.

Footer

Customize the footer of your homepage by displaying your publication name, social media links, or choosing a background color.

How do I pin a post?

On your publication’s home page, navigate to the pencil icon in the hero (top part of the homepage) and click the pencil icon in the top-right corner.

This will open the website editor, where you can choose which section of your homepage you’d like to pin a post to.

Click on a post in the layout to select it. The right-hand panel will then appear, allowing you to choose a different post to pin.

Select a new post from the right-hand panel. It will appear in the layout with an orange pin icon in the top-right corner, indicating it’s pinned to your homepage.

When you’re finished, click “Save” to apply your changes.

Alternatively, navigate to the post you’d like to pin to the top of your homepage and click the three dots next to the title.

Select “Pin to homepage” and this post will now be what readers see when they view your publication’s homepage.

Most popular posts

These popular posts are generated based on views, engagement, and recency.

If selecting views such as Media feature or Magazine, check the box next to “Show top posts row”. Your top posts will appear in a highlighted bar on your homepage.

Tip: To exclude posts from the Most Popular section, select “Hide from top posts” in the three-dot menu of an individual post.

How do I transfer ownership of my publication to a new email address?

Start the ownership transfer

  • Go to your Publication Settings page.
  • Scroll down to the Danger Zone section.
  • Click Transfer ownership.
  • Enter the email address of the person you want to transfer the publication to. Click Send transfer request.

Confirm the transfer

Once you click Send transfer request, you’ll receive an email confirming the request. The transfer status will change to Pending. Both you and the recipient must confirm the request before the ownership transfer is completed.

After you confirm transfer from the email received, a message will appear in the bottom-right corner of your screen confirming that the recipient has been notified.

Recipient confirmation

The recipient will receive an email asking them to Accept Transfer. Once they accept, the publication ownership will be transferred to their account and a confirmation message will appear on their screen.

Note: The confirmation email sent to the recipient expires after 6 hours if the request expires, a new transfer request will need to be sent.

After the transfer is completed, the new owner will have full administrative control over the publication, including access to settings, subscribers, and billing.

Myself or my Recipient did not receive the email, what can I do?

  • Double-check the email address you entered when sending the transfer request to make sure it’s correct.
  • Check your spam or junk folder—sometimes emails are filtered there.
  • Test your email by sending a message to yourself or the recipient to confirm it’s working.
  • If the email still doesn’t arrive, see Substack’s guide on email delivery issues: I’m not receiving my transfer email. What can I do?

How do I turn off paid subscriptions on Substack?

To turn off paid subscriptions, navigate to your publication’s Settings page and click on Danger Zone in the left navigation bar.

In the Danger Zone section, click on “Disconnect Stripe”:

This option will:

  • Refund and notify all your paid subscribers by email
  • Cancel all paid subscriptions to your newsletter
  • Revoke Substack’s access to your Stripe account The balance you’ll need in your Stripe account to successfully refund paying subscribers for subscription time they paid for but won’t receive will be automatically calculated for you.

If you’re moving to a new platform and need help disconnecting Stripe, visit the support widget and ask for a human support specialist.

How do readers subscribe to my Substack publication?

From your Substack publication:

Readers can directly subscribe to your Substack on their first visit to your publication. They’ll be prompted to subscribe or have the option to read it first.

Tip: To add a cover photo about your publication, head to your publication’s Website editor and scroll to the Welcome page section. Once you upload a cover photo, the option to display or hide your publication’s description will appear.

From a post or email:

You can also encourage readers to subscribe by adding a Subscribe now button in your posts.

When creating a new post, select “Buttons” and select “Subscribe now” or “Subscribe with caption” from the drop-down menu.

What will readers see when they enter their email and subscribe?

If you have payments enabled, when readers enter their email address and click “Subscribe”, they’ll be presented with your publication’s subscription plans.

On Substack, there are four types of plans:

  • Free: Free subscribers will receive free posts via email and the Substack app.
  • Monthly: Subscribers will automatically be charged each month and can access paid posts.
  • Annual: Subscribers will make a one-time payment and this rate will automatically renew. Readers or listeners can access paid posts like monthly subscribers.
  • Founding Member: Subscribers can access founding member only posts. Learn more here on how to set up a Founding Member plan. Tip: To customize each plan’s benefits, follow the steps here.

Is there a limit to the mailing list size I can import?

As long as your list is legitimate, and everyone who is on it has explicitly opted in to receive emails from you, there is no limit to the mailing list size that you import.

What if I have a large list?

If you try to import a really big list, you will be asked to undergo a verification review with the Substack team.

Tip: For faster uploading times for importing extra large lists, try dividing them into multiple .csv files and importing them onto your account. We recommend only one column of emails in your email import list since names will not be imported.

Is there an RSS feed for my publication?

You can find the RSS feed for your publication at your.substack.com.

Replace “your” with the name of your Substack publication.

What are recommendations digests?

When a creator makes a new recommendation for a publication or podcast they like, we’ll include this in a recommendations digest email that we send to their subscribers.

Readers can unsubscribe anytime from this digest email and this won’t affect their subscription to your publication.

Can I opt out of being included in this email?

Yes! Opting out will exclude from the digest:

  • Recommendations you make and
  • Recommendations your publication has received from other creators. Note: Readers will still receive emails if other creators that they subscribe to make new recommendations.

Head to your Manage recommendations page and slide the toggle next to “Include in recommendation digests.”

Learn more about Recommendations here.

What are Subscriber Perks on Substack?

The Subscriber Perks feature gives exclusive benefits to paying readers including promotional codes, digital downloads, or access to live videos on Substack. Subscriber Perks have a dedicated Perks tab on your publication’s navigation bar, encouraging free subscribers to upgrade. At this time, Subscriber Perks are only available on your web publication and not the Substack app.

Caroline Chambers of What to Cook offers a variety of perks for founding members

Before you start

You must have payments enabled on your Substack to see the Subscriber Perks feature in your publication’s Settings.

How to create a perk

  1. Go to your publication’s Settings page. Scroll to the Payments section and click Manage next to “Subscriber Perks.” You can also go directly to your perks settings page.

  2. Click Create Perk.

  3. Select a perk type:

  • Discount or promo code: a code you’ve created for an external site
  • Digital download: a file up to 700 MB that subscribers can download (PDF, ebook)
  • Community invite: a link to a private space, such as Discord
  • Link: a link to an online resource such as a Zoom session
  • Substack Live video: a link to a future live video for paying subscribers

  1. Enter a title, description, and image for the perk. The image must be at least 1200 x 800 pixels.

  2. Select who can unlock the perk: founding members, annual and founding members, or all paid subscribers (including monthly).

Note: To limit the perk to new paying subscribers only, turn on the New subscribers only toggle and set the date and time when they can access it.

  1. Click Publish and your perks will appear in a dedicated tab in your site’s navigation bar.

To customize the Perks tab

To hide the Perks tab: Go to the Website section of your publication’s Settings, then slide the toggle next to “Perks” to the left. The tab will be hidden from your navigation bar.

To rename the Perks tab: Go to the Website section of your publication’s Settings, click “Edit” next to “Perks,” and enter a new title.

How subscribers unlock a perk

Subscribers can unlock a perk directly on the Perks page or you can copy the perk’s URL from your browser’s address bar and share it directly with paying subscribers.

Paying subscribers who qualify can click Unlock to reveal the code, link, or download.

Free subscribers who try to access the perk see a prompt to upgrade and are sent to the subscription flow.

Visitors who try to access the perk are prompted to subscribe and become a paying reader.

Refund policy for perks

Substack does not honor refund requests from subscribers who cancel after redeeming a perk. Before unlocking a perk, subscribers must review and acknowledge that their subscription becomes non-refundable. You may issue a refund at your own discretion by following the steps here.

What are subscriber referrals on Substack?

Turn growth on autopilot with subscriber referrals — this tool will help you reward your paid and free subscribers for inviting their friends to sign up for your Substack. Rewards are based on the number of new free subscriptions to your publication so the more free subscriptions a reader brings to your Substack, the closer they are to reaching the next rewards tier.

With subscriber referrals, you can:

  • Set your own reward tiers
  • Automate fulfillment with reward emails
  • Invite your subscribers to participate
  • Celebrate your top referrers
  • Keep growing We’ll automatically handle the award distribution and all you’ll need to do is customize your tiers and rewards.

We’ve made it simple to start: paid publications can begin with default tiers of complimentary paid subscriptions and free publications can add custom rewards to kick off the subscriber referrals program.

Once a reader reaches a tier, regardless of if they’re paying or free, they’ll receive the tier reward.

For Creators

How do I enable subscriber referrals?

Head to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Growth section.

Slide the toggle next to “Enable subscriber referral program”. Your next step is to set up your referral reward tiers.

Note: Subscriber referrals are not available on private publications.

How do I set up my referral rewards tiers?

Click “Edit” next to “Customize rewards” in the Growth section. This page will be your subscriber referral dashboard. Here, you’ll see how many referrals have been made so far, the number of referrers, and your reward tier levels.

For paid publications

For paid publications, the default rewards are complimentary paid subscriptions and the default number of required referrals are two, ten, and twenty five for each respective tier.

To change the comp time period or to add your own custom reward, select the drop-down box under each tier.

For free publications

Free publications do not have paid subscriptions turned on so the default reward will be “Custom” and up to the creator on what they’d like to offer as a reward. This can range from sending your readers:

  • Special content like a video or ebook or a special shoutout in a future post
  • Discounts for paid subscriptions
  • A Zoom chat or access to community like an exclusive Discord or Slack group Tip: Custom rewards are available for both paid and free publications. For more suggestions on what rewards to give to readers, view our guide here.

How are rewards sent?

When a reader reaches a tier milestone, we’ll automatically send them a reward email. These emails are customizable by selecting “Edit” next to Reward email on your subscriber referral dashboard. Just like any Substack post, you can embed video, audio, PDFs, and more, opening up a wide range of options for custom rewards.

How do I keep track of my referrers and their progress?

  • When a referrer reaches a tier milestone, we’ll send you an email notification about the referrer, the tier they’ve reached, and the reward they received
  • You can also access the subscriber referrals Leaderboard by clicking the three dot hamburger menu on your subscriber referrals dashboard or at this link: your.substack.com
  • On the subscriber referrals dashboard, you’ll see the total amount of referrals made (new subscribers to your publication via a share link) and total number of referrers (who’ve introduced new subscriptions via a share link). Note: Admin and Contributor referrals are not included in the referrals count.

How do I tell my subscribers about the subscriber referrals feature? What do they see?

When you set up subscriber referrals on your publication, you’ll see the option to edit an announcement template. This template will live in your Drafts section on your Posts page until you publish or delete it. The subscriber referrals announcement post can be sent immediately to your subscribers or you can schedule to send and publish to your Substack at a future time.

Referral links may be automatically included in email posts.

To remove the prompt for subscribers to invite their friends and earn rewards in emails, slide the toggle to the left next to “Show referral link in emails” in the Growth features section.

Once you’ve set up your custom tier rewards and edited the reward milestone emails, head to the Referral program section on your publication’s Settings page. Slide the toggle to the right next to “Show referral leaderboard”.

A new Leaderboard tab will appear on your publication’s navigation bar and signed in subscribers can track how close they are to unlocking their next reward and access their unique referral link. This leaderboard is public and can be viewed by any visitor.

Things to note:

  • If you change the reward, the reader gets whatever the reward was when they achieved that tier
  • If you lower the referrals needed to reach a certain tier, any reader that has that many referrals will receive the reward immediately
  • At the moment, the ability to reset the referral program where all readers start back at 0 referrals is not available

For Readers

With the subscriber referrals feature, your favorite Substack’s community becomes even better when shared with friends. For every person you refer to a publication and signs up as a subscriber using your unique referral link or post you’ve shared with them, you’ll be closer to reaching exclusive rewards that’s been hand-picked by a creator.

There are no limits - the more readers you refer, the more rewards you can collect!

Sign into the publication that’s participating in this program and head to the Leaderboard.

your.substack.com (replace “your” with the name of the Substack)

On the leaderboard page, you’ll see your unique referral link, your progress i.e. how many people you’ve referred and have subscribed to the publication, and your place on the leaderboard.

One you’ve reached a referral milestone, you’ll receive an email with the reward. If you have any questions about the reward, contact the creator directly at their Substack email address by replying to the milestone email.

Note: If you’re an existing paying subscriber to a Substack and a reward is a complimentary paid subscription, then the complimentary subscription will be applied at the end of your current billing period.

What are subscriber segments on Substack?

A segment is a saved filter of your subscribers, built from one or more criteria such as start date, activity level, paid status, or subscriber tags. Segments are dynamic and update automatically — any subscriber who matches the filter criteria is included or any subscriber who doesn’t match the filter criteria is excluded. For example, a segment for “free subscribers who signed up after March 1, 2026” includes every free subscriber past that date, including ones who joined today.

How to create a segment

  1. Go to Subscribers in your dashboard and click on the Segments tab.

  2. Select a segment template (everyone who is a free subscriber, everyone on a paid plan, longtime free subscribers) or create your own.

  3. Create a new filter by clicking “Start from scratch”.

  4. Choose your filter criteria (tag, country, activity, paid status, signup date, etc.). Select Save segment.

Tip: You can combine multiple filters in one segment.

How to update a segment

Change the filtering logic

  1. Head to the Segments tab and click on the three dots next to the segment you want to update. Select “Edit segment”.

  2. Change any element — for example, change “Type is Free” to “Type is Founding.”

  3. Click Save segment. Select whether to save the segment as a new segment or just update the existing segment.

Change the segment details

  1. On the Segments tab, select the three dots next to the segment you want to update. Click “Edit segment”.

  2. You can edit the segment name or change the description. Select Update segment.

How to send to a specific segment audience

Once you build a segment, you can customize what to send to these subset of subscribers.

Send a direct email to a specific segment

  1. Navigate to the Segments tab and select a saved segment.

  2. Select the checkboxes next to the email addresses of the subscribers you want to contact.

  3. Click Send email and the post editor will open a new draft (this is only the email draft and not a post draft).

Publish a post and notify only a segment

  1. Finish your draft post and click Continue.

  2. On the Publish settings page, expand the Advanced section.

  3. Check the box next to “Only send to a specific audience segment”. Select from the drop-down list who you’d like to receive the email newsletter or app push notification.

  4. Click Publish.

Note: A post where you’ve selected to send to a specific audience segment is still visible to anyone who visits your Substack on the web on on the app. Only the segment you’ve selected receives the email or app push notification.

What are Substack funded gifts?

Substack-funded gifts are complimentary 1-month gift subscriptions to your publication that are redeemable in the app and are fully paid for by Substack. Publications can manage their participation in Substack-funded gift campaigns via their publication Settings.

Note: This feature is only available to certain publications at this time. Subscribe to our company blog for future updates.

How does it work?

During select campaign periods, Substack will offer eligible free subscribers a 1-month gift subscription to your publication, redeemable in the app and paid for by Substack.

Eligible free subscribers will be notified of the limited-time offer via a targeted email sent from Substack.

Clicking the “Claim gift” button in the campaign email opens a new browser window, directing users to the app store so they can install the Substack app and claim their gift subscription. Once they install the Substack app, they will be prompted to verify their phone number to unlock the gift. If users are not logged in to Substack when they click “Claim gift”, they will be prompted to enter their email address before being directed to the app store.

Note: Free subscribers who qualify for a Substack-funded gift subscription have shown high engagement with your publication, are not already paid subscribers, and do not have the Substack app installed on their device. Only eligible free subscribers will receive a campaign email from Substack or a mobile web banner when they visit your publication.

Once the gift subscription is claimed in the Substack app, users will have free access to your paid subscription benefits for 30 days (starting from when the gift was claimed) – fully funded by Substack.

When the gift subscription is ending, the gift recipient will receive a reminder email with the opportunity to purchase a paid subscription plan to your publication.

FAQs

Is the email sent from my email or Substack?

The email is sent from Substack.

What happens if a user unsubscribes from this email?

Because the email is sent from Substack, anyone who unsubscribes from the email will be unsubscribing from Substack marketing emails. They will not be unsubscribing from your publication.

When will I receive money from Substack?

Substack will directly pay for each trial successfully claimed through the app, with payouts sent via Stripe within a few weeks after the campaign ends. Reimbursement for gift subscriptions will depend on the monthly subscription fee for your publication, with Substack’s 10% fee subtracted from the total.

Note: Substack reserves the right to terminate the campaign or deny access to the promotion if we detect abuse.

What are the countries supported by this program?

  • AU - Australia
  • CA - Canada
  • US - United States How do I turn off Substack-funded gifts?

Substack-funded gifts will be enabled by default on for all publications. To disable this feature, head to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Growth features section. Slide the toggle next to “Enable gifts from Substack”. A gray toggle indicates that the feature has been disabled.

What are Substack Pledges?

If you haven’t turned on paid subscriptions for your Substack, Pledges are a tool for your audience to show you they believe your work is valuable. This feature allows your subscribers to pledge to become a paid subscriber if and when you turn on paid subscriptions.

Note: This feature is only available if you have not set up payments on your Substack.

Here’s how it works:

Managing Pledges

Pledges will be on for all free Substacks with suggested pricing tiers. To make changes, head to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Payments section.

  • To turn off or turn on Pledges, click on the toggle next to “Allow readers to pledge subscriptions”. You can change the suggested monthly, yearly, and founding subscription pricing and enter your minimum. The pledge amount must be higher than the standard Substack minimum of $5/month and $50/year.

What do readers see?

When readers subscribe to your Substack, they’ll see an opportunity to pledge a yearly, monthly, or founding plan. If they’d like to continue without pledging, they can select “No pledge”.

Subscribers must enter their payment information to complete their pledge, but there will be no immediate charge. If you turn on paid subscriptions, their pledges will turn into recurring paid subscriptions. If you never turn on paid, nothing will happen.

The subscriber can also write a message about why they’d like to support your publication.

Tip: If a subscriber is logged into their Substack account and has a paid subscription to at least one other publication, their card details will be automatically pre-filled.

Pledges work on auto-pilot. Free subscribers logged into their Substack account will see prompts to “Pledge your support” instead of the “Subscribed” buttons on your publication and in your posts. If you created a Substack from your profile, you will not have a publication.

How do I know when I get a Pledge?

When a subscriber pledges to pay for a subscription, you’ll be notified by email.

You’ll see the options to turn on payments or manage the Pledges feature (found on your publication’s Settings page).

To turn off email notifications for pledges, head to the Notifications section on your Settings page and toggle off next to “New pledged subscription”.

To see a complete list of your Pledges, head to your Stats page and click on Pledges. Here, you’ll see the number of pledges, potential annualized revenue from pledges, and any notes a subscriber has sent.

What happens when I enable payments on my Substack?

When you set up payments and connect to Stripe, your pledges automatically convert to paid subscriptions. Only enabling payments are needed on your end. The new paid subscriptions can take up to one hour to appear on your Subscribers dashboard.

Readers who’ve pledged will get an email notifying them that their pledge has converted to a paid subscription. These notifications are automatic.

What happens if I enable payments but my subscription plans are different than the pledge amounts?

If you set up your subscription plan prices at a price lower than the initial pledge plan amounts, the pledges will be converted to paid subscriptions at the lower price.

If you set up your subscription plan prices at a price higher than the pledge plan amounts, we’ll display the number of mismatched pledges and the pledge amount. You’ll have the option to accept pledges at any pledge tiers you’ve previously set up, meaning that all pledges will automatically convert to paid subscriptions at the price point pledged.

If the “Accept pledges at any price” is toggled off, we’ll notify the reader via email about the price change. The reader can become a paid subscriber at a higher rate by clicking on “Subscribe” in the notification email. Then, they’ll immediately become a paid subscriber.

For example, if you set the pledge pricing tiers to be $8.00/month but you set your subscription monthly price to $10.00/month during the payment setup, the reader’s pledge won’t automatically turn into a $10.00/month subscription.

What are the optimal image dimensions for my Substack publication?

We’ve found that these are the best image dimensions for your publication’s design elements:

  • Logo: At least 256 x 256 px with a transparent background.

  • Email banners: Recommended 1100 x 220 px with a transparent background, but could be taller.

  • Cover image: At least 600 x 600 px.

  • Social / post preview image: We recommend at least 1200 x 630 and 14:10 is the aspect ratio for the preview images.

  • Full-width images: The maximum aspect ratio is 1:1 for height — taller images will be automatically cropped to 1:1. If some images with faces are being cut off in your post thumbnails (social preview images), try enabling smart cropping on your publication. On your publication’s Dashboard, click on Website editor in the left navigation bar. Head to the Home page section on the website editor and next to “Cropping” select center or smart.

  • Center: focuses on the center of the thumbnail image

  • Smart: focuses on the most interesting part of the thumbnail image.

What are the stars on my Subscribers dashboard?

The stars next to your subscriber’s email on your Subscribers dashboard represent how actively the subscriber has used your publication in the last month; this includes email opens and web views.

Note: These stars are not given by the publication’s author i.e., these are not subscriber ratings, and you cannot give stars to your readers.

What does it mean when there are no stars?

A subscriber with zero stars has yet to open your email newsletters.

This subscriber could be a new sign-up since your most recent post. Since they have not received a newsletter post since subscribing to your publication, their star status would be zero.

Another reason for zero stars could be the subscriber’s mail app. Privacy features such as in Apple Mail prevent senders from knowing when a recipient opens an email.

What does it mean when a subscriber has five stars?

A subscriber with five stars is in the highest percentile (80th-100th) for days active on your publication relative to your other subscribers. More than likely, they have either read all or most of your posts.

Can I filter on my dashboard to see who has the least and most amount of stars?

Yes! To see a list of subscribers with 0-5 stars, click the “Filter” button, select the subscriber type, and then “Add a filter.” Select “Activity” from the drop-down box and choose between 0-5 stars.

What does email disabled mean?

If you’ve selected to receive an email notification when a free subscriber unsubscribes, we’ll send you an email confirmation that their email has been disabled and removed from your list.

This notification is optional and can be turned off by going to your publication’s Settings page and sliding the toggle next to “Canceled free subscription”.

What is a Substack Bestseller badge?

Substack Bestseller badges are for creators and are based on the number of paid subscribers a publication has at that time. Unlike a verification badge seen on other platforms, the only way to earn one of these bestseller badges is to start and grow a paid publication to hundreds of subscribers. Free trials and comped subscriptions are not included in the count for a bestseller badge.

You may notice different colored badges next to a creator’s name:

  • Purple: tens of thousands of paid subscribers
  • Orange: thousands of paid subscribers
  • White: hundreds of paid subscribers A badge can appear on multiple surfaces such as a publication’s welcome page, post byline, a Substack profile, or in the comments section.

How do I hide a bestseller badge from my name and Substack profile?

If you don’t want a Substack Bestseller badge to appear next to your name, you can hide this in your Profile settings. Alternatively, head to your publication’s Settings page and in the Team section, there is the option to hide or show your Bestseller badge.

  1. Head to your Substack profile: substack.com

  2. Scroll to the Activity privacy section and slide the toggle to the left next to “Bestseller badge”.

Tip: You cannot show the badge without the corresponding number of subscriptions attached to the badge.

What is a Welcome page on Substack?

When new readers who haven’t visited your publication before visit your Substack, they’ll be automatically shown a Welcome page. To view your publication’s welcome page, add “/welcome” to the end of your Substack URL: subdomain.substack.com

The Welcome page will show:

  • A cover photo (image or GIF) to introduce your publication.
  • The author of the publication.
  • A description i.e. what you write about.
  • Publication launch date or the total number of your subscribers.
  • Any endorsements (up to 3).

How do I edit my Welcome page?

To change your Welcome page, head to your publication’s Website editor:

Cover photo: In the Welcome page section, add or change the image for your Welcome page. Once you upload a photo, you can hide your publication’s name or one-line description. If you upload an image, it should be at least 600 x 600px. GIFs can be used as a cover photo by uploading any .gif file.

Author: To change the name that displays on your Welcome page, head to your Profile and edit your name. The name will be removed automatically if (1) the name on your profile is the same as the name of your publication or (2) your publication has more than one public Admin.

Short description: This appears at the top of the Welcome page. If you’d like to change your description after creating your publication, head to the Basics section on your Settings page and make any edits here.

Subscriber count: If your publication has more than 1,000 total subscribers, an approximate subscriber count will be shown on your Welcome page. You can hide your subscriber number in your publication’s Settings. In the Privacy section, click the toggle next to “Show approximate subscriber count” to remove the number.

Launch date: The launch date reflects the date you created your Substack publication. It may be displayed like “Launched 15 days ago” or “Launched 1 year ago”. If the date appears on your welcome page, it can’t be reset or edited.

Recommendation blurbs: You can add or remove any endorsements from other Substack creators in the Recommendations section: subdomain.substack.com. Only Substack creators who have recommended you to their readers can be added to your Welcome page. Blurbs will not appear on the mobile version of Substack.

Note: If you haven’t uploaded a welcome page image in on your Website editor, the options to display or hide your publication name and short description will not be available.

Can readers subscribe to my publication on the Welcome page?

Yes! Readers can enter their email address to subscribe, or they can select to browse your publication first. The text underneath the subscribe box is customizable. If you’d like to change it from the default “No thanks”, head to the Website editor. Go to the Posts section on the website editor and next to “Skip button” enter up to 25 characters that readers will see and click when they choose not to subscribe at that moment.

Can I create a new Welcome page?

When you create a Substack, a Welcome page is created automatically for you. While the design of the Welcome page can’t be modified, you can change what appears on your page, such as a different cover photo and description.

What is an alias record?

An alias record is a special type of record that only a few registrars support that allows you to map a domain to another domain (very similar to CNAME) but crucially allows you to map the “root” domain to something else. Practically this matters because it allows someone to set up their custom domain with us as e.g. example.com as opposed to www.example.com

What is Substack Boost?

Growth on autopilot

Put the Substack team’s data and expertise to work for your publication. Substack Boost will automatically show discounts and special offers at the right moment to maximize your revenue.

Growing a Substack publication isn’t all on your shoulders. The Substack team is building tools that will grow your Substack on auto-pilot so you can focus on the hard part: the writing itself.

Boost is our most comprehensive investment to date in helping creators accelerate their businesses. For creators who opt into Boost, we’ll work in the background to optimize your revenue by determining the most opportune times to offer a particular subscriber a special offer, including free trials, discounts on paid subscriptions, gift subscriptions, or the likes. For example, we may use Boost to optimize free to paid conversion on promotional surfaces and to help prevent subscribers from churning. These upsell efforts require no time from the creator to manage or implement – and you can opt out at any time.

What can I do with Boost?

In addition to Substack working in the background to optimize your revenue with Boost, you can choose to surface several features for your publication.

Select or unselect the following options in the Substack Boost section of your publication’s Settings page:

  • Upsell email to engaged subscribers Convert free readers to paid subscribers with an automated customizable email. We use a “score” to predict a free subscription’s likelihood to convert to paid, based on a variety of engagement metrics. The email is currently designed to go to a very small segment of the email list (~0.1% of the email list each day).

This email has a default 20% discount for one year but you can change the text and add/remove your own offers by following these steps.

  • Multiple-subscription discount When a reader finishes becoming a paid subscriber to a different Substack, they’re shown an upsell offer to subscribe to other Boost-enabled publications at a discount (up to 20% off).

With Boost enabled, your publication can appear as a recommended subscription and conversely, other publications can be recommended to your new subscribers after they sign up for a paid plan.

  • Pre-cancel offer When eligible paid subscribers are in the process of canceling a paid subscription, offer them a free month as an incentive to not cancel.

Can I turn off Boost on my publication?

When you turn on payments on your publication, Boost will appear in the Growth section on your publication’s Settings page.

To turn Boost off, head to the Growth section on your publication’s Settings page and click the toggle to the left next to “Enable Substack Boost”. A gray toggle indicates that the feature has been disabled. To re-enable it on your publication, click the toggle to move it to the right.

Publishing a post

Can I add a cashtag to a Substack post?

Yes! With the cashtag feature, readers can see realtime changes in US stock prices in your posts. Other markets are not supported.

In a draft post, type a cashtag, followed by a space, to generate a ticker with daily price change.

When readers click on a cashtag in a post, a new tab will open and they’ll be able to discover other posts that mention the same stock.

This also means that if your post contains a cashtag, the post will appear in the Discover search results if a reader is searching for that same stock.

Note: At this time, the current price of a stock cannot be embedded using the cashtag feature.

Can I add a free trial offer to a paywall on my post?

In addition to directly giving free readers a free trial to your publication, you can now add a free trial to a paywall on your Substack post.

Readers will be able to read a free preview of your post and see a 7-day free trial offer to your publication once they reach the paywall.

Note: At this time, the paywall message cannot be customized.

To enable a free trial offer on your paywalls, head to your publication’s Settings page and click on Payments in the left navigation bar. In this section, slide the toggle next to “Offer 7-day free trials in paywall”.

What steps does a reader need to do to get the free trial? Do they need to enter their payment information?

In order to redeem the free trial, free readers will need to select a subscription plan and enter their card payment details.

Tip: Once a free trial begins, you may receive a “New Paid Subscription” confirmation email with a coupon the new subscriber used. This coupon is the free trial.

How do readers cancel a free trial?

To cancel a free trial and avoid being automatically charged on the subscription plan they’ve selected, readers will need to go to their Substack account and cancel their plan.

What can readers on a free trial access?

Free readers will be like paid subscribers and can access your paid posts and archives.

Note: At this time, free readers who accept the free trial will be counted as paying subscribers but will not be counted towards your GAR until their trial ends and they start paying.

Where can I see how many readers are on a free trial?

Head to your Subscribers dashboard and click on “Paid subscribers”. Readers who are on a free trial will appear as a new category labeled “Free trials” and you’ll be able to see the exact number of subscribers who are in that category.

Can free subscribers comment on posts with paywalled content?

No. When a post has a paywall, comments are limited to paid subscribers only. Free subscribers can read the preview, but won’t be able to join the discussion.

Can I add multiple contributors to a byline?

On a post, you can have multiple writers sharing the byline. You can either add a collaborator or have a guest writer on your post.

Ensure that two things happen before adding a collaborator to a byline:

  • Bylines and images on bylines on posts are enabled on your publication’s Settings page.
  • Collaborators have set up their Substack profile. How do I add a collaborator?

To add a collaborator, head to your publication’s Settings page and click on Team in the left navigation bar. In the Team section, click “Invite”. This will send an email invitation to the collaborator and in this section be automatically set to “Private” and “Byline”.

The invited collaborator will be listed as an author in a byline, but they cannot access your posts or settings.

Tip: If you’d like their profile to be shown on your publications’ People page, change the setting from “Private” to “Public”.

When you draft a new post, add a title and then a “+” sign will appear. Select the collaborator you’d like to add to the byline.

Can an invited collaborator access and edit the draft post?

If the invited collaborator is assigned the role of “byline”, they will be listed as an author but do not have access to your posts.

To be able to access and edit a draft post, you’ll need to change and assign them the role of “contributor”. Learn more here on how to change the roles on your publication’s Team.

Can I edit the CSS or HTML on Substack?

We do not currently support custom CSS or HTML in the post editor. This includes custom iframe embeds — you can’t paste raw <iframe> code into the editor. Supported embeds like YouTube and Spotify playlists work through the embed feature.

Can I see how a post will look like to readers before publishing?

Before you’re ready to publish a post, you can see how it’ll appear to free and paid subscribers using the Preview tool.

With the Preview tool, you can see how a post will appear in these formats:

  • Mobile view
  • Desktop view
  • In an email
  • On the web
  1. Click Preview on the top right corner of the page and the Preview Post page will appear.

Note: If this post is intended for paid subscribers, select the “paid subscribers only” audience in your Post Settings before previewing the post.

  1. Select from the drop-down menus at the top how you’d like to view your post.

  2. If you’d like to grab a secret draft link which you can share before publishing or send a test email, click on “Test”.

  3. Click “Done” to exit the Preview Post page.

Free subscriber mobile view of web post that’s for paid subscribers

Paid subscriber view of web post on laptop that’s for paid subscribers

Mobile view of web post that’s for Everyone

Paid subscriber mobile view of email that’s for paid subscribers

Can I tag a creator or Substack publication in a post?

With the Mentions feature, you can tag a creator or Substack publication in your post. When a reader hovers over a mention, the creator’s profile card or a publication card will appear.

How do I insert a mention in a post?

On a draft post, enter “@” and start typing out the creator’s name or publication name. Click on one of the choices presented.

Note: If a creator’s name doesn’t appear when you @- mention them in a post, it’s possible that either you’ve spelled their name incorrectly or they’ve opted-out of having their Substack profile appear in a post.

If you’re mentioned by another creator, you’ll see a notification in your Activity section.

How do I disable getting notifications whenever I’m tagged in a post, comment, or note?

To opt-out of receiving email or push notifications whenever your Substack handle or publication is mentioned in a post, comment or note, head to your account Settings.
