Scroll to the Notifications section and click on the down arrow next to Engagement. Uncheck the boxes for “Email” and “Push” next to Mentions.

Can I use custom fonts on Substack?

When signed in to your publication, select Website editor in the left dashboard. Alternatively, you can access the website theme editor by heading to your Substack’s Settings page and clicking on Website. Ensure that “Custom theme” is selected in the “Publication theme” drop-down menu.

Select new fonts for how you want titles and the bodies of your posts to appear. You can also select whether you want links to appear as underlined or as colored links.

Can I use shortcuts in the Substack editor?

The Substack editor supports the following shortcuts:

Type * or - followed by space to create a bulleted list.

Type 1. followed by space to create a numbered list.

Type # followed byspace to create an H1 heading.

Type ## followed by spaceto create an H2 sub-heading.

Type ### followed by space to create an H3 sub-heading.

Type > followed by space to create a quote block.

Press enter to insert a new paragraph.

Press shift+ enterto create a line break within a paragraph.

Type ---(three hyphens in a row) to create a divider.

With text selected, press cmd/ctrl+B to bold text.

With text selected, press cmd/ctrl +I to italicize text.

With text selected, press cmd/ctrl + shift+ xfor strikethrough.

With text selected, press press cmd + K to add a link.

Do you support Markdown?

We do not currently support Markdown in the post editor.

How can I add a guest author to a post?

Have an author you’ve been collaborating with and now you’re ready to spotlight their writing with your subscribers? The guest post tool lets you feature a guest creator by easily creating their own byline and also prompts readers to subscribe to their Substack.

Here’s how to get started:

  1. From your Dashboard, click on “Create” and then choose any type of post.

  2. In the byline section, select the plus sign.

Tip: To have bylines appear, check that you have “Show bylines on posts” enabled which can be found in your publication’s Settings.

  1. Enter the Substack profile of the guest creator you’d like to invite to preview the draft post. If the guest does not have a Substack profile, enter their email address.

  2. The guest creator will get an email invitation to join your upcoming post. They can accept or decline your invitation in the email. Guest creators must have or create a Substack account in order to appear in the byline– this can be done via the email invitation. They do not necessarily need to download the Substack app in order to accept the invite.

  • If they haven’t accepted your invitation: their email address will appear as “pending” in the byline.
  • If they have accepted your invitation: their name will appear in the byline and you’ll also receive a confirmation email.
  1. When a guest creator accepts your invitation, they’ll have the opportunity to set up a profile and create their own Substack publication (if they don’t already have one). The guest creator will be notified by email once the post is live.

Note: Guest authors will be unable to make any edits or publish the draft as you’ll be posting on their behalf. If you’d like the guest creator to be able to directly edit the draft post, you can add them as a contributor to your publication.

How can I add a template into my Substack post?

If you’d like to pre-load reusable text or images into your post without having to copy and paste every time, follow these steps:

  1. Sign into your Substack account and click on “Create”. Select an article, video, or podcast post.

  2. Click “Template” in the post editor toolbar and “+ New template”.

  3. In the post template composer, enter a title and text. Once you’re finished drafting the template, select “Save”.

  4. The post template will be automatically saved and can be accessed by clicking on the “Template” button in the editor toolbar.

Note: Templates are only available on posts on the web and not available on Notes or the Substack app.

How can I organize my post templates?

Post templates can be organized by titles. When creating a new post template, add a slash after the group name and then the name of the individual template. For example, “Welcome/free subscribers”.

After saving your new template, click on “Template” and select from the group which template you’d like to insert into your post.

How do I edit or delete a post template?

To edit or delete a post template, click on “Template” and select the three dots next to the the individual template.

What can I use a post template for?

You can add reusable templates into your article, video, or podcast posts for content you’d like to repeat often like:

  • Standard disclaimers or disclosures
  • Calls to action (subscribe, share, etc.)
  • Custom dividers or recurring sections
  • Post templates or boilerplate

How can I add recipes to a post on Substack?

With the recipe embeds feature, you can easily add a “how-to make” card into into a post so that your subscribers can read your recipes on the web or on the Substack app.

Recipe embeds are only available on the web version of the post editor and currently not available on the Substack app article composer.

  1. Sign into your Substack on the web and start a new draft post.

  2. From the editor toolbar, select “More” and then click on “Recipe”.

  3. A recipe card template will appear in the draft post. Here, you can enter more details about the recipe such as the title, description about what you’re making, prep time, cook time, servings, and equipment needed. If you’d like to rearrange the ingredients list or the directions, select the six dots next to the line item you want to move and drag it to its desired place.

  4. The Metadata section is optional and what you enter here can help with SEO.

Category: Only the first category you select will be used for SEO.

Keywords: For better SEO, we recommend that you do not use words that overlap with your category or cuisine.

Once you’ve published your post, the recipe will appear as a recipe card within the body of post. If you’d like to change the placement of the recipe card, head back to the post editor and drag and drop the recipe into the desired placement.

Note: At this time, recipes cannot be downloaded as a PDF nor partially paywalled. If you’d like to paywall the recipe, add a paywall divider into the draft post and place the recipe under the paywall line.

How can I add space and align certain text in a draft post on Substack?

On a draft post, you can add white space or align certain blocks of text by using the poetry block feature.

Select “More” and then “Poetry block” from the editor toolbar.

Enter the text you’d like to customize into the block that appears, and make your alignments using the space bar. When ready, select “Continue”. The custom-aligned text will appear in your new text post.

Note: Any text outside the block you customize will correct itself into the standard spacing once you publish the post.

How can I attach a file to my Substack post?

With file embeds, you can share a document such as a PDF, comic file, Excel spreadsheet, or ebook with your readers in posts.

File embeds can also be added to automated emails such as welcome emails and referral reward emails. Head to your publication’s Settings page to edit these emails then drag and drop file types listed in the article into the email drafts.

How do I get started?

  1. In the post editor, drag and drop these types of files directly into your draft post:
  • EPUB, PRC, XLSX, PDF, CBR, CBZ, KFX, KPF, ODS

  1. The embed will display the title, format, and size of the file.

  2. Click on the hamburger icon where you’ll be able to edit, replace, download, or delete the file.

  3. Select “Edit” and you’ll have the options to change the title of the file, add a description, or upload a thumbnail image to the file.

Note: Thumbnail images will be cropped to a 3:2 aspect ratio.

  1. When you’re ready to send your post as an email or have it appear on your website publication, click “Publish”.

What does the file look like for my readers?

For comic files

For CBZ/CBR files, we’ve partnered with Panels, an IOS comic reader app, to provide a higher-quality reading experience for comics.

When readers click on “Read now” in the post, they’ll have the options to open the file in Panels or download the file.

Tip: Panels is free for readers– if readers don’t have the app already installed, they’ll be prompted to download the app for their iPhone or iPad devices.

For PDFs and spreadsheets

The embed appears like a file attachment that readers can download. A scrollable full-screen preview within the Substack post is not available at this time.

Note: The file size limit for a PDF is 700 MB.

How can I detect AI on Substack?

Scan for AI text estimates whether text was written by a human or AI-assisted. It works on posts and notes published on or after July 21, 2026, and uses Pangram to assign a percentage of how much of the text is human-written or AI-assisted.

Note: Neither Substack nor Pangram uses publisher content to train generative AI models. Substack offers a Block AI training toggle in your publication’s Settings that signals to external AI crawlers not to use your content for training models.

The Scan for AI text tool can be used in the following surfaces and content:

  • The Substack Reader (Web): Click on a post from your Subscriptions to open the reader.
  • Substack iOS App (Android coming soon): Check for AI on notes and posts on the app.
  • Notes: Scan notes on your feed without leaving Home.
  • Comments & Replies: Scan individual comments and replies on notes. Note: The AI transparency tool is unavailable for video or audio posts, or for posts viewed on standalone Substack sites (such as on.substack.com or custom domains) or emails.

How do I scan a post for AI text on the web?

  • Open a post from Subscriptions on the web. This opens the Substack Reader.

  • Click the three dots in the top right corner.

  • Select Scan for AI text. How do I scan a note for AI text?

  • Click the three dots on the note.

  • Select Scan for AI text. Notes with too little text will show a “not enough text” message instead of an analysis.

How do I scan for AI on the Substack app?

  • Tap on any note or post and select the three dots in the upper right corner.
  • Tap Scan for AI text. How can creators manage AI detection on their content?

Add a transparency note

  • Go to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Details section.
  • Click “Edit” next to “How I make this.”
  • Enter your statement which will appear if readers scan one of your posts, notes, or replies for AI text. Select “Save statement”. Tip: You can always edit your “How I make this” statement by clicking on the three dots on a Pangram analysis of your post or note.

Analyze and disable detection on a post

On the Publish page of a draft post, click Disable AI detection.

When you disable AI detection on a draft post, readers who view the post on the Substack Reader will see an “AI detection unavailable” message when using the Scan for AI text tool. The AI detection tool will need to be disabled manually for each individual post published on or after July 26, 2026.

Note: If you’re writing an article on the Substack app, tap “Next” once you’re finished with the draft. On the Publish page, click the three dots and select “Scan for AI text”. On the analysis report, tap the three dots and select “Disable detection.

Disable detection on a note

  1. Click the three dots in the notes composer.

  2. Select Scan for AI text.

  3. Once the Pangram analysis appears, click the three dots in the upper right corner.

  4. Select Disable detection.

Once detection is disabled, readers will no longer see a Pangram analysis on that post or note. Instead, they’ll see an “AI detection unavailable” message.

Tip: You can disable detection on the web and on the Substack app by clicking on the three dots on a Pangram analysis.

Report a detection error

  • On the AI detection report, select Report detection error.
  • Enter your feedback which will reviewed to improve detection quality.

How can I edit images on a Substack post?

With the Substack post editor, there’s a variety of things you can do with an image that’s been added to a post. Note that many of these editing features are only available on the web post editor and not available on the app mobile editor.

Need to change the size of an image?

Once you add an image, click the black bars on the side of the image. This will size the image making it larger or smaller– whatever your preference.

To make your image full width, click on on the three dots in the top right corner and select “Set full width”.

Need to add alt text to an image?

Click on the three dots in the top right corner of the image and select “Edit alt text”.

Enter your text and click “OK”.

Need to add a caption to an image?

Click on the three dots in the top right corner of the image and select “Edit caption”.

The caption will appear at the bottom of the image for you to edit.

Need to add a link to an image?

Once you add an image and see a blue border around it, click on the Link icon in the toolbar or use the hot-key combo (command+K on Apple devices).

Enter the URL in the pop-up box and select “Link”.

Need to add a watermark?

Click on the three dots in the top right corner of the image and select “Add watermark”.

A preview of the image will appear with the options to change the opacity and size of your publication’s logo on the image. Drag the logo to the desired position in the image and select “Save”.

Need more advanced editing tools?

If you’d like more editing options, click on the three dots on the image and select from the following:

  • Crop: Click on Crop to rotate, flip, scale, and resize the shape of the image.
  • Filter: Select Filter to change your image to different colors such as Sepia and Mono.
  • Finetune: Click on Finetune to change the brightness, exposure, clarity, and contrast of the image.
  • Annotate: Select this option to add text, add shapes, and write on the image.
  • Redact: To blur parts of the image, select Redact.

How can I edit the Substack Boost upsell email?

The Substack Boost upsell email is a targeted message which we’ll send to your most engaged free subscribers to help convert them into paid subscribers. This email is automated and we’ll send it out on your publication’s behalf at the optimal time. However, you can edit and customize the upsell email anytime.

We use a “score” to predict a free subscription’s likelihood to convert to paid, based on a variety of engagement metrics. The email is currently designed to go to a very small segment of the email list (~0.1% of the email list each day). There are filters in place to make sure an individual subscriber doesn’t receive this email more than two times per year.

Note: The default upsell email template includes a special offer of 20% off for one year for the recipient. All of this is subject to change as we test and try to find the most optimal approach to the email.

Follow these steps to make changes to the upsell email:

  1. Head to Boost on your publication’s Settings page.

  2. Click on “Edit” next to “Customize automated upsell email”.

  3. Change the subject and body of the text as you’d like using the editor (while this may look like a draft post, it will be sent out as an email).

Can I change the default 20% off special offer?

Yes! If you’d like to offer a discount that’s more or less than 20%, you can delete the “Redeem special offer” button in the draft email template and add your own button. Alternatively, this upsell email is 100% customizable and it isn’t required to include a discount in order for it to be sent to your free subscribers.

In the editor toolbar, select Button> More> Special offer.

Once you select “Special offer” you can add an existing special offer or create a new offer.

Your most engaged free readers can then click on the special offer button when they receive the upsell email and easily become a paid subscriber.

Note: If you remove the default 20% off one year button, you’ll also want to edit the body of the email template that mentions the 20% off.

Can I turn off Boost on my publication?

When you turn on payments on your publication, Boost will automatically be enabled and a new section will appear on your publication’s Settings page.

To turn Substack Boost off, head to the Substack Boost section on your publication’s Settings page and click on the toggle to move it to the left. A gray toggle indicates that the feature has been disabled. To re-enable it on your publication, click the toggle to move it to the right.

How can I share another publication’s post with my subscribers?

If you’re reading a post or listening to a podcast episode on Substack and would like to share it with your subscribers, you can share the entire post with the cross-posting feature instead of embedding a post link and publishing a text post.

Note: You can only use this feature for free posts.

From a web post

  1. When signed into your Substack account, click on the three dots of the free post you’d like to share and select “Cross post”.

  2. In the pop-up, type a blurb for your subscribers about why you’re sharing that post and select “Continue”.

  3. You can send a post to your founding members, paid subscribers, or everyone. You also have the option to schedule a time to publish the cross-post.

Note: If you’d like the post to appear on your Substack, check the box next to “Publish cross-post to web.” If you’re cross-posting from a publication, you must be an Admin in order to cross-post.

  1. Your subscribers will receive an email from your Substack with your blurb and the entire post.

  2. If you’ve selected the option to publish the post to your Substack publication, the cross-post will appear as a normal post on your publication.

Are there stats for posts I share?

On your Posts page, posts that you’ve shared with your subscribers will have the cross-post icon.

We’ll display stats such as total views, recipients, and the open rate. Just like any other post, you can click on the Reach, Engagement, and Growth tabs to see granular details.

I don’t want other publications to share my free posts– can I opt out?

Head to your publication’s Settings page click on Privacy in the navigation bar. Slide the toggle to the left next to “Allow cross-posting” to opt out of being cross-posted.

Will I be notified when publications cross-post my post?

On your publication’s Settings page, scroll to the Notifications section. If you’d like to receive a notification when another publication cross-posts your post, slide the toggle to the right next to “Cross-posts of your posts”. To opt out of receiving notifications, move the toggle to the left.

How can readers flip to a new post?

Readers can go forward and back between posts by tapping on “Previous” and “Next” buttons found at the bottom of your posts.

On your publication’s Dashboard, click on Website editor in the left navigation bar. Head to the Posts section on the website editor and slide the toggle next to “Show navigation buttons”.

Once enabled, a reader will see these buttons and like with a book, move forward or back. This can help readers navigate your publication.

How do I add a callout block on a Substack post?

Add callout blocks to your posts: visually distinct, formatted containers that let a piece of text stand apart from the rest of your writing. Use them to highlight a key idea, surface a quote from another writer, flag a note to readers, or draw attention to something you want to make sure doesn’t get skipped. It’s a clean way to add visual rhythm or reference something you want your audience to see.

  1. Create a new article, video, or podcast post.

  2. In the editor toolbar, select the quotes icon.

  3. Click “Callout block” and enter text within that block.

  4. Once you add a callout block, you can’t move the position of the block in the draft. However, you can delete a block and add a new block anywhere within the draft post.

How do I add a drop cap to my Substack post?

To add a large capital letter at the beginning of your post, you’ll need to enable drop caps in the Website editor. Drop caps are available only for the first letter of your post and not available for each new paragraph or section in a post.

  • Head to your Dashboard and select Website editor in the left navigation bar.
  • In the Website editor, click on the Posts tab. Next to “Drop caps”, click the toggle to the right and select “Save”.
  1. Any posts that you publish moving forward and any past posts will display a large capital letter at the beginning of the post text.

Matt Taibbi of Racket News uses the drop cap feature on their posts

Note: Drop caps is not a per post setting but applied to all your Substack posts on web. At this time, drop caps will not appear on the Substack app or in email newsletters.

How do I add a poll to a Substack post?

Follow these steps to add a poll to your post:

  1. From the post editor toolbar, click on “More” and select “Poll” from the drop-down menu.

  2. Once the poll template appears in the draft post, click on the pencil icon in the top right corner to enter the requested details such as how long the poll will last and who can vote. There’s also an option to hide the total vote count from readers.

Who can vote on a poll?

  • By default, only your subscribers can vote- this includes both paid and free subscribers.
  • If you’d like to restrict a poll to only paying readers, select from the drop-down menu, “Only paid subscribers”.
  • As a publication author, contributor, or admin, you can also vote on a poll by selecting, “Show options”. This link is only visible to you.
  1. Your subscribers will be able to vote and see the results of the poll immediately. Results are also visible after a poll closes.

Poll results are visible to you, contributors, and admins by clicking on “Show results” on the poll. However, if you, a contributor, or an admin has individually voted on a poll or if the poll is closed, the “Show results” option will not appear for that individual.

Note: If a non-subscriber tries to vote on a poll, they’ll be prompted to enter their email address and subscribe to your publication.

At this time, there can only be one vote for one option on a poll and votes cannot be changed. If you’d like to edit a poll after a post has been published, click on the pencil icon on the poll in your draft post. Editing a poll will remove all the existing votes.

How do I add a voiceover to my Substack post?

With voiceover, your subscribers can read and listen to you read out loud the post in one spot.

How do I get started?

  1. When signed in to your Substack account, click on “Create” then select “Article”.

  2. In the editor toolbar, select the headphones icon and then select Voiceover.

  3. A voiceover panel will appear and you can either upload an audio file or narrate your post by using our recording feature directly on your device. To record audio, check your browser settings to ensure that Substack has access to your microphone. Or select between different input sources on your device.

  4. Once you’ve finished uploading an audio file or recording, select “Done” and the voiceover will appear at the top of your post. To replace the audio, download the file you’ve uploaded or recorded, or delete the audio, select the hamburger menu.

Tips for voiceover:

  • The position of the voiceover cannot be moved from the top at this time.
  • Each post can have one voiceover. Your readers will be able to listen to the audio post on the web post or on the Substack app.

On the web:

If you send the post as a newsletter, a voiceover player will appear in the email and subscribers can click the player and listen to the post in a new internet browser.

On the Substack app:

When the post appears in a subscriber’s Inbox, they’ll be able to tap on the voiceover and directly play it in the app. Just like with podcast episodes or text-to-speech, the voiceover will play in the background as long as the subscriber doesn’t fully exit/fully close the app i.e. they remove the Substack app from their device’s menu of open apps.

Note: If text-to-speech is available on your publication, a voiceover in the post will override the TTS.

To have your voiceover posts appear in podcast players, follow these steps here.

Can I paywall a voiceover?

If you’d like to keep your text free for any reader but paywall your voiceover, follow these steps:

  1. After adding a voiceover audio file to your post, click on “More” in the draft editor toolbar and select “Paywall”. Move the paywall to the end of the draft if you’d like the entire text to be free to readers or insert the paywall in the draft where only paying subscribers can access the content.

  2. Click “Continue” and ensure that your Publish settings are like the below:

  • This post is for “Paid subscribers only”
  • Send free preview is selected (if you uncheck this section the paywall will be removed)
  1. Once you publish the post, free subscribers will receive a “preview” of the post but the voiceover will only be available for paying subscribers.

Free preview email newsletters with voiceover

Free preview post with voiceover on the Substack app

If you’d like your entire post (text and voiceover) only for paying subscribers, do not add a paywall and select “Paid subscribers only” on the Post settings page before you publish your post.

How do I add alt text to an image on a Substack post?

Alternative text is a written description of an image. To add a alt text to an image on your post, follow these steps:

  1. Create a new article, video, or podcast post.

  2. Upload an image from your device into the post draft.

  3. Click on the three dots in the upper right corner of the inserted image. Select “Edit alt text”.

  4. Type a clear, concise description of the image.

  5. Select “Ok”.

How do I add anchor links in my Substack post?

If you’d like to share certain parts of a post or allow readers to jump to a specific section of a long post, try adding anchor links.

When on a draft post, click on “Style” in the toolbar and select either Heading 1, Heading 2, or Heading 3, or Heading 4.

After publishing your post, hover over the H1, H2, H3, H4 header and click on the link icon that appears. This anchor link will automatically be copied to your clipboard.

How do I add audience-specific content to a post on Substack?

Audience-specific content lets you add personalized content blocks within a single post that are only visible to specific reader groups—such as free subscribers, paid subscribers, or founding subscribers. You can add audience sections to article, video, and podcast posts from the draft editor.

How to add audience-specific content to my post?

  • Open the draft editor for a new article, video, or podcast post.
  • In the editor toolbar, select More, then Audience-specific content
  • Choose a pre-made template for free or paid readers, or select Custom to define your own audience.
  • In the Audience drop-down menu, select who should see this section:
    • Not subscribed
    • Free subscribers
    • Paid subscribers
    • Founding subscribers
  • Write your content inside the content block.
  • Select Preview to see exactly what each tier will see before you publish. Tip: Add a For everyone else block to show a fallback message to readers outside the audience group. This block is optional and fully customizable.

What can I put in an audience-specific content?

Audience-specific content supports the same content types as the rest of the post editor—text, images, embeds, and more. You can create and save templates to reuse audience sections across future posts.

Who can see the audience sections?

Audience-specific content is visible wherever subscribers can log in—on your web posts and in the Substack app. Each reader (email newsletter recipient, Substack app, web version of your Substack) sees only the content that matches their subscription status. Free subscribers see free content; paid subscribers see paid content.

Note: RSS feeds and search engine crawlers see the Not subscribed version of any audience section.

How do I add equations to my Substack post?

You can easily add equations in LaTeX into your post by following these steps.

  1. On your publication’s dashboard, select “Create” then “Article”. In the editor toolbar, select “More”. From the drop-down menu, click on “LaTeX.”

  2. Click on the block to access the LaTeX editor and enter your equation. When you’re ready, select “Save”.

  3. The equation will appear in the body of your draft post. You can edit it anytime by clicking on the pencil icon in the top-right corner of the block.

  4. To see how the equation will appear on your post, click on “Preview” in the post editor.

How do I add free images or pictures to my Substack post?

Using the post editor, you can search for free images from Unsplash or generate an image using AI and add them directly to your post.

  1. Click on the image icon in the editor toolbar and select “Stock photo” or “Generate”.

For stock photos: Use the search bar to find an image. Click on an image you’d like to use and select “Insert”. This will automatically add what you selected into your post.

For AI images: Enter a prompt and select “Generate”. Select “Insert” for the image you’d like to add to your draft post.

  1. To delete the image in the body of your draft, select the three dots in the top right corner of the image. To re-size the image, click on the black bars on the sides of the image.

How do I add links to my Substack homepage?

On your publication’s homepage, you can use links as a blogroll, to direct readers to other places to find you on the web, or to highlight popular posts, among other uses.

To add a link to your Substack’s homepage, go to your publication’s Settings page and click on Website in the dashboard. Next to Homepage links, select “Edit”.

You can add any link URL, title and subtitle, and select which group the link will appear under on your homepage. When assigning a group, think of this as a category or genre by which to organize your homepage links.

To make any edits to a homepage link, select the pencil icon. To remove a link from your homepage, click on the trash can icon. You can re-arrange the sort order of each link by clicking on the six dots next to the link in the Hompage links settings page and dragging it to it’s desired place.

How do I add tags to Substack posts?

Tags allow you to organize your posts and make it easier for readers to navigate through your publication for topics that interest them.

With the tags feature, you can add a label to any post and create a page for a specific tag. This page can be added to your publication’s navigation bar and/or shared with your readers.

How to create a tag

You can create a tag in your publication’s Settings page or in your draft post’s settings.

Publication’s Settings: In the Website section, scroll to Custom Tags and select “Add”. After saving your new tag, you’ll be able to easily select it from a list of choices when you’re adding a tag to a new draft post.

Draft Settings: On a draft post, select either the Settings icon (Post settings) or when finished writing, select Continue (Publish settings). In the “Add tags” section, enter a keyword and select enter. A tag will automatically be generated.

Tip: To add tags to an already published post, click on the three dots on a post and select “Edit”. On the draft, select “Continue”. Scroll to the “Add tags” section and enter your desired tags. Select “Update now” and your post will be updated with the new tags.

Tags can be managed on your publication’s Settings page in the Website section.

  • To edit the name of a tag or slug URL, select “Edit”.
  • To delete a tag, select “Delete”
  • To display a tag page on your publication, select “Add to navigation bar”.
  • To share a tag page with readers, select the underlined URL and you’ll be directed to a new page that has a URL like this: subdomain.substack.com Note: If you delete a tag, it will automatically be removed from every post that you’ve added that tag to. Additionally, if you’ve added that tag page to your navigation bar, that will be removed also.

How to display tagged content

In the Tags section on your publication’s Settings page, click on the three dot menu next to a tag and select “Add to navigation bar”. You can manage the navigation bar in the Navigation section. Click on the three dots menu next to the page link to edit the tag title/URL or remove it from the navigation bar.

How do I display tagged posts on my publication’s homepage?

Click on Website editor and add a new content block to the homepage. Select “Posts & Modules” or “Feature block”. For “Posts & Modules”, select “Tag” as the source and then select from the list any tags you’ve created.

How do I center or right-align text?

Currently, you cannot change the alignment of text, headings, or subheadings in the Substack post editor.

If you’d like to add space and align certain text in your draft, you can use the poetry block feature.

How do I change a post from paid to free?

If you’ve published a post for paid subscribers and would like to open it to free readers, you can unlock the post by following these steps.

  1. When logged into your Substack account, head to your Posts column.

  2. On the post you want to change, click on the hamburger menu and select “Edit post”.

  3. On the post editor page, select Settings.

  4. Change your audience under “This post is for…” from “Paid subscribers only” to “Everyone” and select Save.

  5. Your post will now be unlocked so that free readers and paid subscribers can view your post.

Interested in scheduling a paid post to unlock automatically and be sent as a newsletter to free subscribers?

Once you’re finished with the draft, click “Continue”. On the Publish page, check the box next to “Schedule time to unlock post and email free subscribers”. Select a date and time and your post will unlock for free readers on the web and be sent as an email to your free subscribers at the scheduled time.

How do I delete a comment on a Substack post?

  • Navigate to the comment you’d like to delete.
  • Click the three dots underneath.
  • Click “Remove”. More information can be found about Banning or Suspending a user here: How do I ban or un-ban someone from commenting on my publication?

How do I delete a post that I’ve published?

You can delete any post from your publication and archive on the web version of your Substack. At this time, you’re unable to edit or delete a post on the Substack app.

To delete a post on web:

  1. Navigate to the post you’d like to delete.

  2. Click the three dots under its title.

  3. Click “Edit.”

  4. In the post editor, click “Settings”, then scroll to the bottom and click “Delete Post.”

Once a post is deleted, we will stop delivering email replies from your readers to your inbox.

How do I edit a post that I’ve published on Substack?

You can edit any post from your publication and archive on the web version of your Substack, or directly in the Substack iOS app.

To edit a post on the Substack iOS app:

  • Open the Substack iOS app and navigate to the post you’d like to edit.

  • Tap the three dots in the upper right corner.

  • From the menu, tap “Edit”.

  • Make the edits and select “Next” and then “Update now”.

  • Your edited post will be reflected on the Substack apps and on the web. To edit a post on the web:

  • On the web version of your Substack, navigate to the post you’d like to edit.

  • Click the three dots under its title.

  • Click “Edit”. Posts only ever go out by email once. If you edit a post that you’ve previously emailed out to subscribers, it won’t be emailed out again. Your changes will only be reflected on the web post and on the Substack app.

How do I edit my post’s URL?

On a draft post, select Settings at the bottom of the post.

Scroll to the “SEO Options” and select the drop down arrow look for “Post Url”. Enter your desired URL.

Note: A URL slug can only contain lower-case letters, numbers, and dashes and must be between 2 and 48 characters long. If you have multiple words, make sure to include a dash between each word; no spaces are allowed.

How do I edit what my post looks like on social media?

Interested in seeing what your post will look like on social media?

  1. From the Substack post editor, click on Settings at the bottom of the draft post.

  2. The social preview section displays what your post will look like on public places.

  • To change the text, click the “edit” link, or click on the box itself.
  • To select a different image from your post for the preview, hover over the thumbnail or click “Upload New”. If you edit the social preview when on the Publish page of a draft, you will not see a “Save” button. Any change will be saved automatically.

Tip: For photos to appear in the social preview, we recommend the dimensions to be at least 1200 x 630 (or larger).

How do I embed a code block in a Substack post?

With code blocks on the Substack post editor, you can access new features such as syntax highlighting, automatic language detection, and publish snippets that render with consistent formatting, spacing, and line numbers.

To embed a code block on a Substack post, follow these steps.

  • Head to the web version of your Substack publication and start drafting a new post. This can be a new article or a video or podcast post.
  • From the post editor toolbar, click on “More” and select “Code block” from the drop-down list.

  1. You can select programming languages such as Python, CSS, or JavaScript or our editor will automatically detect the language if you paste directly into the code block.

  2. Once you’re ready to publish your post, the code block will appear in email newsletters, on the web, or on the Substack app.

Tip: Subscribers can easily copy the code snippet on a web post by clicking on the Copy icon on the snippet.

How do I embed an audio file in my Substack post?

Looking to create an audio version of a post that your subscribers can listen to? That’s a Voiceover! Learn more here on how to add one.

With our audio embed tool, you can directly embed an audio file anywhere into a post.

  1. Sign in to your Substack account and select “Create” on your Dashboard. From the drop-down menu, select any type of post.

  2. From the post editor toolbar, select the headphones icon and click on “Audio embed”.

  3. You can either upload an audio file or record audio directly into the post. To use our recording feature, check your browser settings to ensure that Substack has access to your microphone. Or select between different input sources with your device.

  4. Slide the toggle to the right next to “Include download button on web” if you’d like to offer the option to download the embedded audio.

  5. Select “Insert” and move the audio file wherever you’d like in the post: at the top, in-between text, or as a closing line. To move the file, click and drag the file.

Can I embed more than one audio file in a single post?

Yes! You can insert as many audio files as you’d like and the location is up to you. Just like other media files that can be embedded in a post, you can also place an audio embed below a paywall.

Your subscribers or new readers will be able to listen to the audio embeds on a web post or in the Substack app as they read through the text. They can play and pause the audio files anytime.

Note: Audio embeds will appear in an email newsletter but will play in a new web browser. Listeners can download embedded audio on your web posts.

How do I embed Datawrapper charts in a Substack post?

With the Datawrapper embed feature, you can integrate interactive charts, maps, and tables into your posts, enriching them with visualized data.

How do I embed a Datawrapper chart?

  1. Sign in to your Substack account and select “+New post” on your Posts page.

  2. On a separate browsing tab, open your Datawrapper chart, map, or table that you’d like to embed. Copy the shareable link provided by Datawrapper and switch back to your Substack post editor tab.

A shareable link can look like this: datawrapper.de

  1. In your draft, paste the Datawrapper link where you want the embed to appear in your post. The Datawrapper embed will automatically load and appear in the draft body.

If you’d like to change the placement of the chart, select and drag the image to the desired spot.

Tip: You can include as many Datawrapper embeds as you like in a single post.

Your subscribers or new readers will be able to interact with the Datawrapper embeds on a web post or in the Substack app as they read through the text.

Note: Datawrapper embeds will appear in an email newsletter, but interactions with the data (like zooming in on a map or scrolling through a table) will open in a new web browser.

How do I embed media in my post (e.g. images, video, GIFs)?

Here’s our tips on how to use the Substack editor to add media to your posts.

How to add images

Drag and drop them into your draft post, or upload an image by clicking on the Image icon:

Tip: Image files that are supported are avif, gif, jpg, jpeg, png, webp. Undecided on what image to use on your post? Click “Add stock photo” and you’ll be able search and insert free photos from Unsplash directly from the Substack editor.

How to add a GIF

Drag and drop them into your draft post, or upload a new file by clicking on the Image icon, just as you would any other image.

How to embed a video

Click on the video icon in the editor toolbar and upload a video file or record directly from your device. Follow these steps here on how to embed videos hosted on Substack into your post.

How to embed rich media

Copy-and-paste the URL directly into the draft post and the embeds will automatically appear.

  • Embedding TikToks: videos will play within a web post on Chrome and in the Substack app. On Safari, videos will open in a new tab. Email posts contain a static image that link to the TikTok.
  • Embedding Spotify: songs or podcasts will play within a web post and on the Substack app. Email posts contain a static image that will open the song or podcast episode on the Spotify app.
  • Embedding Youtube: videos will play within a web post and in the Substack app. Email posts contain a static image that link to Youtube video (readers can choose whether to open the video on the Youtube app or youtube.com on their browser).

How to add a banner image

Above your post title on a draft, select “Edit email header/footer”. You can upload a banner image in this Banner image section.

Note: Image must be in PNG format.

How to embed other media

We do not currently support custom CSS or HTML in the post editor. However, we support Datawrapper charts, Polymarket odds, LaTeX equations, and financial charts.

Writers can easily create a stock, crypto, or commodity price chart (including text, line, and emoji annotations) and insert it into a draft post as a static image. On the post editor, select “More” and from the drop-down menu, click on “Financial chart”.

Looking for more tips on how to make your posts stand out? Check out our Editor guide.

How do I embed Polymarket odds on Substack?

With the Polymarket embed feature, you can integrate market prediction data into your notes and posts that allows your subscribers to follow news on some of the most hotly debated topics across current events, sports, politics, crypto, and more.

Polymarket events can be directly embedded on Substack using the Polymarket search feature or by adding a shareable link to a post or note. A shareable link can look like this:

polymarket.com

How do I embed Polymarket odds into my post?

  1. On the web version of your Substack, first sign into your Substack account and click Dashboard. Select “Create” and select an article, video, or podcast post.

  2. Click “More” in the editor toolbar and select “Prediction market”.

  3. In the pop-up, search for an event using keywords or a phrase.

  4. Select whether you’d like to embed a snapshot view (current odds) or a trend view (line graph over time).

  5. The Polymarket embed will automatically load and appear as a widget in your post. It will automatically refresh with the latest odds. If you’d like to change the placement of the widget, select and drag the image to the desired spot.

How do I embed Polymarket odds into a note?

On web

  1. Open the notes composer on a computer or on mobile web.

  2. Click on the three dots and select Prediction market. In the pop-up, search for an event using keywords or a phrase. A snapshot view of the Polymarket event will appear with the option to switch to a trend view.

  3. Once you’re ready to publish, click on Post.

On the Substack app

Embedding a Polymarket event into a note is available on the Substack iOS and Android apps. Follow the steps below on how to embed on the Substack iOS app.

  1. Tap on the plus sign and the notes composer will open up. Click on the Prediction market icon (looks like a crystal ball) and search for an event.

  2. A snapshot view of the Polymarket event will appear in your note. When you’re ready to share your note, select Post.

Once published via the web or the Substack app, a note with the Polymarket event will appear in the Substack Notes feed. Notes will also appear in the Activity tab of your Substack profile.

What will my subscribers see?

Your subscribers will be able to view real-time changes of the Polymarket widget on the web version of your Substack and on the Substack app.

For email newsletters, Polymarket embeds will appear as an image. Readers can view real-time changes by clicking on “View polymarket” in the post and a new browsing tab will appear on their device.

Note: At this time, the Prediction market icon cannot be hidden or disabled from your Notes composer.

How do I embed Substack posts or notes on a website?

From a post on the web

To get an embed code for a Substack post, click Share at the top of the post and select “More”. Select “Embed” from the menu options.

A preview of the embed will appear. Select the copy icon and paste the code snippet into the HTML section of an external website.

From a note on the web

To get an embed code for a Substack note, click on the share icon (up arrow) and select “Embed note”.

A preview of the note embed will appear. Select the copy icon and paste the code snippet into the HTML section of an external website.

How do I enable or disable bylines (name and photo) on my posts?

If you’d like to add multiple authors to a byline on your post or add a different author other than yourself, you’ll need to first enable bylines for your publication.

Head to your publication’s Dashboard and click on Website editor in the left navigation bar. Head to the Posts section on the website editor and slide the toggles next to “Show bylines” and “Show byline photos”.

To disable bylines on posts, slide the toggles to the left in the Posts section on the Website editor.

How do I enable or disable comments on my posts?

Start by enabling comments for your publication:

  • Navigate to your publication’s Settings and click on Community in the left navigation bar.
  • Slide the toggle to the right next to “Enable comments, likes, and restacks”. After enabling comments:

Choose whether to allow comments on a per-post basis. From the post editor, click Settings.

  • Everyone- If the post audience is set to Everyone, you’ll see the option for everyone to comment. This means that any reader, subscriber or non-subscriber, will be able to leave a comment on the post. If they do not have a Substack profile, they’ll be prompted to create one.
  • Free or paid subscribers- If the post audience is set to Everyone, you’ll see the option to gate comments to just free or paid subcribers. A non-subscriber will see a message that the comment section is for subscribers only and it’ll prompt them to subscribe.
  • Paid subscribers only- Only paid subscribers to your publication can leave a comment on the post.
  • Founding members- If the post audience is set to founding members only, then only founding members can comment on the post
  • No one- Comments will be disabled for the post. Free subscribers cannot comment on a paid post. Only paid subscribers can comment on paid posts.

Tip: If you’d like to lock your post to prevent readers from leaving new comments, head back to your post settings and select “Allow comments from no one (disable comments).”

How do I pin a comment?

If there’s a comment that you’d like to pin to the top of the comment thread, click on the three dots next to the desired comment. From the menu, select “Pin”. That comment will now appear with a “Pinned” label and appear above the rest of the comments on a post. You can pin more than one comment on a post.

What are restacks in the comments section?

When someone restacks your post, it can appear in the Home feed of their subscribers and followers. Now, restacks can also appear in the comments section of the web version of your posts. Readers can choose between a Comments tab or Restacks tab.

Learn more about restacks on your posts or how to disable them from appearing here.

How do I enable or disable likes on my posts?

  1. Navigate to your publication’s Settings column and click on Community in the left navigation bar.

  2. Next to “Enable comments, likes, and restacks”, slide the toggle to the right.

  3. With community features enabled, you’ll be able to see the number of likes and click to see a list of readers who liked that post.

Note: Only readers who have enabled likes to be visible on their Substack profile will appear on the Likes list. Readers who have this disabled won’t appear on the list but will count towards the likes.

Where can I see who liked my post?

You can see who liked your post in several places.

At the bottom of the post

Click on the number of likes and see the list of reader profiles who hearted your post. Just note that some profiles may not be visible if they have disabled likes from being visible on their Substack profile.

Email notifications

Head to your publication’s Settings and scroll to the Notifications section. Slide the toggle to the right next to “Likes on your posts” and you’ll receive an email every time someone likes your post. With this setting on, you’ll also be able to view likes in your Activity section on your Substack account.

Can I make likes private so that only I can see who liked my post?

  • For free posts, any reader who visits your post can see the profiles of other readers/subscribers who’ve liked that post.
  • For paid posts, only paying subscribers will be able to view other paying subscribers’ profiles who’ve liked that post.
  • Likes made on invite-only publications will not be visible on a reader’s profile. Note: Historical likes made prior to the launch of the new likes feature (late May 2022) will not be visible. Only the number of likes and/or any new likes with a reader’s profile picture since the feature’s launch will appear on an older post.

How do I paywall my archive of free posts on Substack?

Add a paywall to all of your old posts in just a few steps.

  1. Go to your publication’s Settings and click Payments in the left menu.

  2. Under Paywalling, you’ll see “Auto-paywall after.”

  3. Enter how long you want posts to stay free (for example, “7 days”), then click Save.

Your archive of free posts will automatically be locked and free subscribers or new readers will see a paywall.

To remove the paywalls, head back to the “Paywall your archives” section and uncheck the box next to the time period picker and select “Remove paywalls”.

Note: Posts that were individually paywalled before the use of this setting i.e. you inserted a paywall yourself will remain paywalled.

To exclude a post from the paywalled archive and make it available to free subscribers and new readers, follow these steps:

  1. On a paywalled post, click on the three dots next to a post and select “Edit Post”.

  2. In the post editor page, select Settings at the bottom.

  3. Click on “Remove paywall” and the post audience will automatically change to “Everyone”.

How do I publish a free preview of a paid post on Substack?

Tip: To publish a free preview of a paid post, you must have payments enabled for your Substack. Learn how enable payments on Substack here.

On Substack, you can publish a free preview of a paid post for your free readers. This feature is flexible meaning that the placement of the paywall within the post is 100% up to you.

How do I get started?

  1. Within the post editor, you can add a paywall directly from the toolbar. Click More then select Paywall from the drop-down menu.

  2. Once the paywall is automatically embedded in your post, you can drag and move it between paragraphs.

  3. To send a free preview of the paid post as an email to your free subscribers, make sure to do the following on the Publish page:

  • Post is set to “Paid subscribers only” or “Founding members only”.
  • In the Delivery section, check the box next to “Send via email and Substack app”.
  • In the section that says “Send a free preview” check the box next to “Free subscribers”. This section will not appear on the page unless you’ve selected to send via email and the Substack app inbox. Note: If you switch the post from paid subscribers to “Everyone” or “Free subscribers only”, the paywall will be removed.

Can I customize the paywall?

Yes! You can customize the header for free preview emails.

  1. On the post editor, click on “Edit email header and footer”.

  2. Click on “Edit” in the headers for the free previews posts section.

Note: Customization is limited to only free preview email headers and won’t appear on the web post.

What does the paywall look for free readers?

Free readers can view a preview of a paid post in their email, on the Substack app, or on the web version of the post.

  • Emails will have an Upgrade button, your customized free preview header (if you’ve set that up), and a list of subscription benefits.
  • Web posts will display “This post is for paid subscribers” and a Subscribe button. Tip: To see what the paywall looks like via email, click on the Preview button which can be found on the post editor page and enter your email address in the “Send test email” section or use the Preview tool.

How do I publish a new post on Substack?

This article shows how to publish a desktop or mobile web post. If you have the Substack iOS app, follow the steps here to post from the app.

  1. Go to your publication’s Dashboard, click on “Create” and select “Article”.

  2. Start writing using our Substack editor.

  3. When you’re ready, select Continue and you’ll have the option to choose:

  • The intended audience (everyone, paid subscribers, free subscribers, or founding members)
  • Who can comment on your post
  • Add a free preview for a paid post
  • Add tags to make categorization easier
  • If you’d like to send the post via email and Substack app inbox
  • A future date and time to publish your post How do I save a draft?

Posts are automatically saved as drafts. Click on the clock icon in the Substack editor to see previous drafts of your post.

You can view all your unpublished posts in the Posts tab and the Drafts section.

Tip: Have a draft you’d like to share? On the Substack editor, click on Settings at the bottom of the draft. On the post Settings page, you’ll find a secret draft link that you can copy and share.

How do I only publish a post to my publication?

Your posts are set to publish to the web and be sent via email and to the Substack app by default. If you’d like to only publish a post to your publication, uncheck the box next to “Send via email and Substack app inbox” after clicking on Publish.

Can I resend an edited post to my subscribers?

Once a post is emailed, it can’t be resent- sorry about that! Any edits you make to a published post will appear on the web version of the post and the Substack apps.

Can I add a reading time to my posts?

An estimated reading time for posts will only appear on the Substack app. However, you can manually estimate the reading time and add a line to the top of your draft and then publish it.

How do I publish a Substack post in a different language?

To publish a post in multiple languages, turn on “Additional post languages” in your publication’s Settings, then use the “Languages” option in the post editor to add a custom translation for each language you want to offer. Readers will see the version that matches their device’s language setting.

Before you start

Your publication’s automatic translation and additional-language settings must be turned on.

  1. Go to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Basics section.

  2. Turn on the toggles next to Additional post languages and Allow automatic translation.

  3. Click Create and select an article, video, or podcast post.

  4. Write your draft in the language you want as the default for email newsletters and the Substack app.

  5. The editor automatically detects the draft’s language. Once the language is determined, select the Languages menu above the post title.

  6. Select one or more of the 18 supported languages to create versions in.

Tip: The languages offered are: Arabic, Catalan, Danish, Dutch, English, English (British), Finnish, French, German, Icelandic, Italian, Japanese, Norwegian, Polish, Portuguese (Brazilian), Portuguese (European), Spanish, Swedish, and Turkish.

  1. Click each language above the title and write a custom translation for it.

  2. Click Continue, then confirm the languages listed under Language editions are the ones you want available to readers. The language marked Email is the default version sent in email newsletters and shown on the Substack app.

What happens next

Readers see notes and posts automatically translated into their device’s preferred language, on both web and the Substack app, if automatic translation is enabled. If a post has custom language versions published, readers see the version that matches their device’s language instead of the automatic translation.

On the web, custom translated posts are only available in the Substack web reader. For example, when you click a post in your Subscriptions inbox or click a post shared in a note, you’ll be able to read it on the Substack web reader. The translation option won’t appear on posts viewed on your standalone Substack site.

How do I restore a post draft?

Have you written a post but need to step away and publish at a later time?

As you write and edit your posts, the Substack post editor automatically saves your drafts. The draft history feature shows your version history of a post and also lets you revert your draft to a previously saved version. Draft versions are only available on the web version of your Substack and not on the Substack app.

How do I restore a draft?

  1. Click on a draft and select the clock icon at the bottom left-hand side of the page.

  2. Here, you’ll see versions of your post, including your current version, i.e., working draft, which is labeled as “Latest.”

  3. Select a version from your history you’d like to restore and select Restore draft.

  4. This version will now become your new working draft.

Note: Restoring a draft never directly affects a published version unless you click the Publish button after restoring.

How do I schedule an article to publish for a future date on Substack?

  1. On the web, create a draft article. Once you’re finished with the draft, click “Continue”. You’ll be given a set of options before publishing, including the ability to schedule a post.

  2. Check the box next to “Schedule time to email and publish” and select a time and date. Posts cannot be scheduled to publish more than three months from the current date.

  3. If your post audience is selected for paying subscribers only, you can schedule a time to unlock the post and email free subscribers.

Note: When scheduling a post, the time zone it will publish in is the time zone you and your device’s clock is in. Scheduling an article, podcast, or video post is only available on the web and is not available on the Substack app.

How do I see replies to an email or post that I’ve sent out on Substack?

Your Substack creator email is an alias to the address on your Substack account, which means when a subscriber replies to an email or newsletter, you’ll receive it in your inbox, just like a regular email.

All Substack posts are sent from an email address with the format [subdomain]@substack.com. At this time, there currently isn’t an option to change or otherwise customize the send email address. However, you can customize the email address where you receive replies to your emails and newsletter posts.

  • Head to your publication’s Settings page and scroll to the Community section.

  • In the “Forward reader emails to” section, enter an email where you’d like to receive subscriber replies.

  • Click “Save” and moving forward, any replies will be sent to the email you’ve added. If you aren’t receiving any replies from readers, we recommend these troubleshooting tips:

  • Check Your Email Settings: Ensure you can receive emails at your Substack account by checking your publication’s Settings page. Under Emails, ensure the “Who can reply to your emails” option is set to anything but “No one”. This will allow you to receive emails forwarded from your Substack alias.

  • Email Forwarding: Emails sent to your Substack alias are automatically forwarded to the email address listed as the owner of your Substack publication or to the email address in the “Forward reader emails to” section. Check the Inbox and Spam of the email addresses associated with your Substack publication. Tip: Only want to receive replies from your subscribers? View steps on how to change those settings here.

How do I send a test email of a post on Substack?

Before sending an email to your subscribers, you can send a test email to yourself or to someone else. This will let you preview what the post will look like when it’s sent as an email to your readers.

If you’re sending a test email to someone else, ensure that that person and their email address is part of your publication team. You can send them an invite on your publication’s Settings in the Team section. Once they’ve accepted your invitation to join your Substack team, you can change their role to an Admin or Contributor and then you’ll be able to send them a test email.

  1. On your draft post, select Preview (found on the top right corner of the page).

  2. Once you’re on the Preview Post page, select Share and enter an email address under “Send test email”.

Tip: To send a test email to more than one email address, separate each email using a comma. You can also send a test email of the draft post by selecting “Settings” on the draft and scrolling to the “Send test email” section of the page.

How do I send an email to one or a select group of subscribers on Substack?

You can use the subscriber dashboard to send emails to a subset of your subscribers. For example, you may want to send a thank you email to your earliest subscribers, a special offer to the free subscribers who open emails most frequently, or find and email subscribers by country.

When you send out a targeted email with the subscriber dashboard, we won’t create a post on your Substack publication or archives– these are direct emails.

  1. On your Subscribers tab, select “Filter” to sort out your email list to find your audience.

  2. Select all the recipients (make sure the box next to their email address is checked). Click on “Send email” to start a draft.

  3. From here, you can write an email as you normally would on Substack, then click “Send email” when you’re ready.

  4. This email is available to view on your Posts dashboard in the Published tab. An email icon will appear next to the title indicating that it was sent as just an email to certain subscribers.

  5. Once the email is sent, it can take up to 10 minutes for data to populate. To see a list of recipients, click on the individual post. In the Overview tab, select “Details” in the Recipients box.

How can I email subscribers after I send out an email post?

After you publish a post, head to the Posts tab and click on the specific email newsletter post. On the post page, select “Recipients”. You’ll see subscriber engagement such as opens and clicks for each person. Select the checkbox next to the email addresses of the subscribers you’d like to email.

Write a message and when you’re ready, select “Send email.” This email will appear in the Posts tab with an envelope icon next to the title.

How do I set up welcome emails on Substack?

Welcome emails are a great way to engage new subscribers by thanking them for joining and providing valuable information about your publication.

Here’s how you can set them up:

  1. Sign in to your Substack account and head to your publications’s Settings page: your.substack.com

  2. In the Emails section, you may see four types of welcome emails: welcome email to paid subscribers, welcome email to free subscribers, welcome email to imported subscribers, and welcome email to founding subscribers.

  3. Click “Edit” next to each type of email you’d like to customize:

  • Welcome email to free subscribers: When readers subscribe to your publication, they’ll receive this email. If you enable payments and readers subscribe to your free plan, they’ll receive this email.
  • Welcome email to paid subscribers: If you have payments enabled and a reader subscribes to a paid plan, they’ll receive this email.
  • Welcome email to imported subscribers: If you’ve imported a mailing list, these new subscribers will receive this email.
  • Welcome email to founding subscribers: If you have payments enabled and a reader subscribes as a founding member, they’ll receive this email.
  1. Edit the email template with your own content and click “Save”.

Tip: The New Reader survey will be included by default in your welcome emails to free and paid subscribers if you’ve never edited the default welcome email templates.

Tips on how to welcome your new readers

By the time your welcome email hits your reader’s inbox, you may think your job to sell your publication is done: they’ve subscribed and are on the list! What else is there to do?

But this overlooked space can be the difference between instant churn (unsubscribe or inactive reading) and upsell from free to paying subscribers. The welcome email can bring your reader deeper into your publication from the get-go and deliver on the promise of your welcome page and your About page—both instrumental in converting browsing readers to signups.

While the boilerplate copy created by Substack for your welcome email is a good guide for length and content, some simple and personal tweaks to your welcome email can make all the difference.

Greet your new subscribers

Imagine your welcome email is how your reader might like to be greeted on entering a party you’ve put on for subscribers only. What’s the atmosphere and feeling in your reader you want to exclusively create?

Invite free subscribers to upgrade their subscription

There is never not a good time to invite subscribers to upgrade. From day one, you can start the messages you want free subscribers to hear.

Our team advises including an upgrade button “above the fold” of the email (before the reader needs to scroll), along with details of the paid benefits laying out the differences for free and paid subscribers.

Include a call to action

The welcome email is also an opportunity to get your new reader into the swing of reading your posts every time they see an email from you.

Some creators like to include links to foundational posts, to archives, or to previous posts in a series they are currently running. Including a list of great paid posts acts as another invitation to upgrade so readers don’t miss out.

Get to know your readers

Now is a perfect chance to get to know the person behind an unknown email address.

You can ask your readers to reply to the welcome email with why they signed up, or invite them to take a survey to share more about where they are in the world and why they subscribed.

How do I share a preview of my post with others?

Before publishing and sending an email to your subscribers, you can share a draft to a friend, editor, or contributor using a secret draft link.

  1. On your draft post, select Preview (found on the top right corner of the page).

  2. Once you’re on the Preview Post page, select Test and copy the URL in the”Secret draft link” section.

  3. Anyone who has the secret draft link will be able to preview your post, even if it is not published. However, if your publication is in private mode, the recipient of the draft link will need to be subscribed in order to view the draft. To remove access, click “Reset the link”.

Note: Paywalls in the secret draft link will not be visible to viewers who have access to this draft preview. You can also send a test email of the draft post by selecting “Settings” on the draft and scrolling to the “Send test email” section of the page.

How do I test different titles for email newsletters on Substack?

As a way to boost to open rates and increase engagement, you can test and try out different subject lines for emails that are sent to a random portion of your subscribers with the top performer being sent automatically to the rest of your recipient list.

Note: Title testing is currently available only to publishers with at least 200 subscribers to ensure a large enough sample size for meaningful results.

How do I get started?

  1. Once your draft is ready to publish, click on “Continue” and on the “Publish” page, scroll down to “Run a title test”.

In this section, you’ll be able to enter some alternate titles, choose the size of your test, and how long you’d like the test to run for.

Tip: The recommended test size is 50% of your recipients for a test duration of one hour. This means that once your post is published, 50% of recipients will receive an email with one of the experimental titles. After one hour has passed, the title with the highest open rate will win and be automatically sent to the remaining 50% of recipients.

  1. After entering the test details, make sure that the box next to “Send via email and the Substack app” is checked. Then click “Start experiment now”.

  2. To see how your test is doing, click on the post on your Posts tab and click on “Test”. Throughout the test duration, the open rate for each of the experimental titles will go up.

If you prefer to manually stop the test, click on “End test early” and select which email title you’d like to send immediately to the remainder of your post recipients.

What happens when the test ends?

If you prefer to let the test run for the selected duration, you’ll receive an email notification that the title testing has finished with a high level view of open rates for each title, the timing of the test, and which title variant was the winner and automatically sent to the rest of your subscribers.

All this information can also be viewed on the posts stats page on the web version of your Substack.

After the test ends, Substack automatically selects the best-performing title and sends the post with that title to the rest of your audience. Your post’s title (and subtitle, if part of the test) will update to reflect the winning version.

How do I use surveys on Substack?

Incorporating surveys into your posts can enhance your engagement with your readers and provide valuable insight about them. To take a survey you’ve shared in a post or note, readers must be subscribed to your Substack.

Benefits of using surveys in your Substack posts can include:

Understanding Your Audience Better: Surveys can help you gain insights into the demographics and preferences of your readers and get direct feedback from them. This information can be valuable in creating more targeted and relevant content to match your readers’ interests better.

Increased Engagement: By involving your audience in the decision-making process through surveys, you can increase their engagement and investment in your content. This can lead to more active participation in your publication’s community.

Analyzing Trends Over Time: Regular surveys can help you track changes in your audience’s preferences and behaviors, allowing you to adapt your content strategy to reflect these trends.

Step 1: Create a new survey on your Substack

  1. Sign in to your Substack account and go to your Stats tab. Click on “Reader Surveys”: your.substack.com

  2. A pre-made survey called “New reader survey” will appear. Click “Edit Survey” to make changes to this default survey. If you’d like to create a new survey, click “Create new survey”.

Note: If you’ve never edited the default welcome email templates, the “New reader survey” will be automatically included in this email. This means that when a new free reader subscribes to your Substack, they’ll receive a welcome email with a survey link. You can remove this link by deleting the survey button in your “Welcome email to free subscribers” and “Welcome email to paid subscribers” templates.

Step 2: Edit the survey

Change the name of the survey

Click on the three dots and select “Edit name”.

Choose the type of question you want to ask

  1. Select “+Add question” and choose the type of question you want participants to see.
  • Text: Readers can type in their answers if you select a text response.
  • Number: Readers can type in a number if you select a number response.
  • Multiple Choice: If you select a multiple choice response, readers can choose all that apply to them.
  • Single Choice: If you select a single-choice response, readers can choose only one answer presented to them.
  • Country/Region: Readers can choose from a drop-down menu if you select a country/region.
  1. Enter the possible responses to your question. To add more responses, select “Add option”. To let people enter their own responses, select “Add Other”.

Tip: To prevent subscribers from skipping a question in the survey, check the box next to “Required”.

  1. If you’d like to change the placement of a question, click on the up or down arrow next to “Order”.

Step 3: Share the survey with your subscribers

Once you’ve finished setting up the survey questions, select “Save Changes”. You can share the survey by sending an ad-hoc email (this will not appear as a web post in your publication’s archive), copying the direct link, or inserting the survey in a post.

To send an ad-hoc email or copy a direct link to the survey, click on “Share” and select from the two options.

To share the survey in a post, start a draft and select Buttons in the editor toolbar. From the drop-down menu, select “Link to survey”.

Select which survey you’d like to insert into the post and type what you want the button to say.

Step 4: View or export results

To view your survey results, head to the Surveys page and select “View responses”. To export a .csv of responses submitted, select the three dots and select “Export results”.

Things to note:

  • If you export the results, the subscriber’s email address and/or Substack profile will be included in this file.
  • You won’t be notified by email or see a notification in your Activity bell when someone submits a survey. How do I turn off a survey?

To end a survey, you’ll need to delete it. A turn-on/turn-off option isn’t available for surveys. When you delete a survey, all questions and responses will be lost. All links to the survey in your welcome email or posts will fail.

How to publish a post from the Substack app

Publishing a new post is available on the Substack iOS and Android apps. If you’d like to learn more about how to publish a post from a desktop, please follow these steps.

On the Substack app, you can publish and send a text or video post and send it to your subscribers directly from the Substack app.

To publish a text post

  • Open the Substack app and tap the orange “plus” button on the Home screen. Select “Write an article” from the bottom menu.
  1. Start a draft post by directly typing into the editor. As you type, you’ll see various formatting and embed options.
  • Select the plus icon to add an image from your photo library or take a photo using your device. You can also add a video, button, divider, and paywall (if you have payments enabled on your Substack).
  • Select the link icon to add a link to your draft or hyperlink specific text.
  • Select the bullet points icon to format text as a bulleted or numbered list. Once you add a bulleted or numbered list, you’ll see the options to indent your lists.
  • Select the quotes icon to format text as a block quote.
  • Select the arrow icon to undo or redo previous actions.
  • Select the keyboard icon to view your draft post without the device keyboard blocking the screen.
  • To access formatting options on the app editor, highlight the text. You’ll be able to select from various text sizes and heading, hyperlinks, bolding, italics, strikethrough, and code options. Note: New drafts started in the app editor will be autosaved. Your post drafts are synced across web and the Substack app. This means that you can start a post on web, finish it on your phone, or vice versa, whenever you need.
  1. Once you’re ready to publish your post, select “Next”.

If you have payments enabled on your Substack

From the “Audience” drop-down menu, select who you’d like to email or notify via a push notification.

  • Everyone: Your post will be sent to free and paid subscribers and appear as a free post on the Substack app and the web.
  • Free subscribers only: Your post will be emailed to free subscribers and will not appear in your publication’s archives.
  • Paid subscribers only: Monthly, annual, and founding member subscribers will receive this post.
  • Founding members only: Only founding members will receive this post if you’ve enabled this subscription tier. If you have a free Substack, the post will be sent to your subscribers and appear as a free post on your publication or profile. For free Substacks, the default comment audience for your posts will be “Everyone”. You can change this on the web. For paid publications, who can comment on the post depends on whether you’ve selected the post for paid subscribers or founding members only.

The Substack app editor allows you to notify subscribers via email and push notifications. If you’d prefer to publish the post to the web, tap the toggle to the left.

Note: You can now edit published posts directly in the Substack app.

To publish a video

  1. Open the Substack app and tap the orange “plus” button on the Home screen. Select the “Video” tab.

  2. Upload a video from your device’s library.

  3. While your video is uploading, you can enter a title and text into the draft post.

  4. If you have have a free Substack, you can select to send email or push notifications to notify your subscribers about your new video.
