This graph shows the number of paid subscribers who initiate cancellations each day.

Free subscribers

If you have payments enabled on your Substack, you can see how your publication retains free subscribers over time. Track who sticks around after signing up and spot where people drop off, so you can learn what’s working and adjust. If you don’t have payments enabled, you will not see the Retention tab.

On the Retention tab, click on “Subscribers” and you’ll be able to see the following metrics:

  • Free net growth: New free subscriptions versus unsubscribes over time.
  • Free cohort analysis: The percent of free subscribers who remain subscribed each month.
  • Free churn: The number of free subscribers who unsubscribe each day.

What happens to my email list for readers who read in the Substack app?

You will always own your readers’ email addresses and be able to export them at any time if you wish. When a new reader subscribes to your Substack from the app, you’ll get their email address just like normal and be able to see their details on your Subscribers tab on Substack.com.

Where can I see how readers got to my Substack post?

When logged into your Substack account, select ‘Posts’ in the left column and click on the post you want to see more details about.

Here, a summary of the post’s performance, including where readers of that particular post came from:

What does each traffic source mean?

Email- Your post was sent as a newsletter and opened and viewed in an email sent from your publication.

Direct- When a reader types your publication URL directly into the browser and clicks on your post or when there’s an unknown source such as when a reader is using a web browser anonymously.

Other- Sources that account for <1% of views will appear as “Other”.

Popular sites- If you or other readers shares your post on social media (Facebook, Twitter, etc), we’ll display the site domain where readers clicked on the link and landed on your Substack post.

Subscriber help

How can I hide the subscriber count on my Substack profile?

If you have a Substack publication, we’ll display a subset of your subscriber count on your Substack profile.

Readers can click on the subscriber count and see a list of subscriber profiles. The profiles listed here are of people who’ve selected to publicly show their subscription to your Substack on their profile.

If you’d like to remove the subscriber count and have “See subscribers” shown instead, head to your publication’s Settings page. In the Privacy section, scroll to where it says, “Show approximate subscriber count”. Click on the toggle to move it to the left and the count will be immediately hidden.

How do I cancel a subscription and issue a refund for a paid subscriber on Substack?

To cancel a paid subscription and issue a refund, navigate to your Subscribers tab.

Locate the paid subscriber by entering their email address in the Search bar:

Click on the three dots next to their name and select “Cancel subscription.”:

You’ll then have the option to cancel their subscription with a pro-rated or full refund for their most recent subscription transaction to your Substack.

Tip: If you don’t see the option to cancel a subscriber’s subscription, it likely means they have already canceled their subscription, or it is currently on pause.

Once the paid subscriber is canceled, the reader will be notified via email about their refund and be downgraded to a free subscriber. They’ll receive free posts but won’t have access to paid-only posts or comments on paid-only posts.

To learn more about Substack’s refund policy, read this article.

Important: Publishers should issue all refunds through the Substack Subscribers dashboard. Refunds issued through the Stripe dashboard will include Substack’s 10% fee.

How do I find the billing country of a paid subscriber? (Subscriber help)

If you need to issue an invoice or report taxes based on a subscriber’s location, billing address information is stored in Stripe, not directly in your Substack dashboard.

To find a subscriber’s billing country:

  • Go to your Stripe Dashboard.
  • In the search bar at the top, enter the subscriber’s email address.
  • Click on their customer profile in the results.
  • Under Payment methods, click the dropdown arrow next to the payment method on file.
  • The billing address, including country, will appear there. Note: Substack does not collect or display billing addresses on your publication dashboard. Only Stripe retains this information. If a subscriber has not provided a billing address to Stripe (for example, if they pay via certain digital wallets), the country field may be empty.

How do I make changes to a reader’s subscription on Substack?

Navigate to your Subscribers tab and locate the subscriber by entering their email address in the Search bar:

Click on the three dots next to their name and select “Edit Subscription.”:

For a paying subscriber

  • Turn off auto-renew: their paid subscription will end after their current billing period.
  • Cancel subscription: cancel their subscription to issue a prorated or full refund, and they’ll be downgraded to a free subscriber.
  • Set to Founding: adds the Founding Tier to the subscriber’s subscription. If they are looking to remove their payment details you can do so via your Stripe dashboard, under the Payment methods section of the customer’s page.

To learn more about Substack’s refund policy, read this article.

Remove from email list

Removing a free or paid subscriber from your email list will stop the subscriber from receiving any emails from your publication moving forward. For paying subscribers, their subscription will be canceled with a prorated refund.

How do I see who is subscribed to a section on my Substack publication?

If you’ve created sections on your Substack publication, you can find which readers are subscribed to each section using the subscriber dashboard.

  1. On the Subscribers tab, scroll to the bottom of the page where all your subscribers are displayed.

  2. Click on the “Filters” button and select “+Add filter”.

  3. Select “Receiving emails for” from the first drop-down menu. From the second drop-down menu, select “includes any of”, “includes all of”, or “includes none of”.

Tip: If you want to see a list of readers who are only subscribed to one particular section, select “includes all of”.

  1. In the third drop-down menu, select the section which you’d like to see subscriber details for. Select “Apply” and a filtered list of subscribers will be generated.

If you’d like to send a targeted email to a section, follow these steps here.

Readers are saying that they’re blocked from paying to subscribe on my Substack. Why?

If your readers are being blocked from paying to subscribe to your newsletter, it’s possible that:

  • Our payments processor, Stripe, may have flagged your account and blocked it from accepting payments.
  • Payments may not be supported in the country you reside in.
  • A reader’s bank may think the charge is fraudulent and have blocked the transaction. We recommend they contact their bank as a next step and ask their bank to allow the charge to clear.

A subscriber told me they aren’t getting my emails. What should I do?

There can be a variety of reasons of why a subscriber isn’t receiving your Substack emails. In most cases, your emails may have unintentionally been marked as Spam.

We recommend sharing the two pages below with your reader to help them get started with receiving your newsletter again!

- [I subscribed to a Substack publication but am not receiving any emails. What can I do?](https://support.substack.com/hc/en-us/articles/360037488332-I-subscribed-to-a-Substack-publication-but-am-not-receiving-any-emails-What-can-I-do)
- [Will I still get emails if I have the Substack app?](https://support.substack.com/hc/en-us/articles/32266967065108-Will-I-still-get-emails-if-I-have-the-Substack-app)

What happens when a subscriber’s payment fails on my Substack?

If the subscriber’s payment fails, we’ll automatically retry and email the subscriber on your behalf, asking them to update their payment method. Our payment processor Stripe will generally retry payments 3 times automatically before a paid subscription is canceled.

If you’d like to reach out to subscribers with payment failures, we’ve seen that personal outreach from the author can further improve the customer’s willingness to update their card or call their bank if they’re experiencing issues. But even if you don’t do anything, don’t worry, we’ll keep trying on your behalf.

Note: You can filter failed payments directly on your Stripe dashboard by clicking on your Payments tab and then “Failed”.

For additional information about payment failures, please refer to the article here.

Substack Android App FAQs

How can I select which notifications to receive on the Substack Android app?

To select which push notifications you’d like to receive on the Substack Android app, follow these steps.

  1. Tap on your profile picture and select Settings. Next, tap on Notifications.

  2. On the notifications page, select “Settings” next to “Push Notifications”.

  3. You’ll be directed to the Substack app settings for your mobile device. Tap on “Notification categories”.

  4. On this page, toggle on and off which category you’d like to be notified about on the Substack Android app.

  • Reactions: when a reader likes your post (if you’re a writer) or likes a comment you left on a post.
  • Comments: when a reader comments on your post (if you’re a writer) or replies to a comment you left on a post.
  • New posts: a writer you subscribe to publishes a new post.
  • Reshare: When another Substack reader or writer restacks your note.
  • Restored: a push notification you may receive if you quit the app before it’s able to be displayed.
  • Media Player: this will appear in your device’s Notifications when you’re playing audio on a post. You can play/pause and rewind 15 seconds or skip 30 seconds.

How do I cancel my paid subscription on the Android app?

At this time, canceling a paid subscription to a publication can only be done on a web browser.

  1. When viewing a paid publication on the app, click on the three dots in the top right corner and select “Manage Subscription”.

  2. Copy the link and paste it into a new tab on your mobile browser. Once you sign in, you’ll be able to view a list of your subscriptions.

  3. Select the paid publication you want to update.

  4. Scroll to the link that says, “To cancel your paid subscription, click here”.

  5. Tap on “Cancel Subscription” to complete the process.

Note: Your paid subscriber benefits will continue until the end of the subscription plan’s billing cycle.

How do I edit my profile on the Android app?

To edit your profile on the Substack Android app, tap on your profile picture at the top right corner of your device and select Settings.

Click on “Edit profile” and you’ll be able to do the following:

  • Change your name, bio, photo, and profile theme.
  • Select which publications to display on your public Substack profile. These will appear under “Reads” on your profile and your profile may appear on a creator’s subscribers’ list on their profile.

How do I subscribe to a new publication on the Android app?

You can easily subscribe to a publication that you see on your Home feed or discover new Substacks by tapping on the magnifying glass at the top of your device.

Find a new publication by using the search bar or by clicking on a category that interests you and automatically subscribe by tapping the “+” icon.

When on a publication, the easiest way to subscribe is by tapping the Subscribe button.

What happens after I subscribe?

You’re now a free subscriber and will receive a confirmation email from Substack with additional upgrade options to become a paying subscriber (if the publication has paid posts) and a Welcome email from the author.

Any new posts will appear in your Inbox and you can access past posts by tapping on your profile picture to see your full library of subscriptions.

How do I unsubscribe from a publication on the Android app?

To unsubscribe from a free subscription

Tap your profile picture to see the library of publications you subscribe to. Select the publication you’d like to unsubscribe from.

Tap the three dots on the top right corner of the screen and select “Unsubscribe”.

To unsubscribe and cancel a paid subscription

  1. On your web browser, log into your Substack Account and head to your Settings page.

  2. Click on the publication you’d like to make a change to.

  3. Scroll to your Account actions and tap “To cancel your paid subscription, click here”.

Your paid subscriber benefits will continue until the end of the subscription plan’s billing cycle and you’ll become a free subscriber. To end your subscription, click on “Unsubscribe” on the Manage Subscription screen.

How do I view a paid post on the Substack Android app?

If you’re already a paid subscriber to a publication, you can view all paid posts on the app.

If you’re a free subscriber, you’ll see a paywall notifying you that the post is visible for paying subscribers only.

How do I become a paid subscriber?

At this time, you can’t change your subscription plan directly on the Substack app.

  • If you see a paywall on a post on a Substack that you’re subscribed to, click on “Upgrade your subscription.”
  1. A new window will open on your mobile browser with the publication’s subscription plans.

Note: You’ll be asked to enter a credit card to start your paid subscription. If you’re already a paying subscriber to another Substack publication, your card details will be pre-filled.

What does Archive mean on the Android app?

If you see a post you’d like to read at a later time in your reading queue or Inbox, you can archive the post.

To view your archive, tap on your profile picture and select “Archive”.

Here, you’ll see all the posts that you’ve archived.

  • Once a post has been archived, you can un-archive it by tapping on the post and selecting “Unarchive”.
  • Your Archive on the app is unable to be cleared.

Will I still get emails if I have the Substack Android app?

On the Substack Android app, you can choose to receive push notifications about new posts, receive email newsletters, or receive notifications about new posts both via push and email.

Tap your profile picture and select Settings.

Select “Notifications” from the menu and your notifications settings page will appear.

I want to receive email newsletters and not push notifications from the Substack Android app– what can I do?

If you’re not receiving email newsletters, it’s possible that when you downloaded the Substack app, you selected “Smart notifications”. With smart notifications, you’ll primarily receive push notifications about new posts in the app and may receive email notifications about posts you’ve missed if you haven’t opened the Substack app in a while.

To resume email delivery, tap your profile picture on the Substack app and select Settings.

On Settings, tap Notifications and scroll to the Newsletter Delivery section. Tap “Prefer email”. New posts will now be delivered via email and can be read directly in your email provider or in Inbox on the Substack app.

  • If you’d prefer to receive both email newsletters and push notifications, tap the “Both email and push” option,
  • If you’d prefer to only receive push notifications from the app, tap “Prefer push”. If push delivery fails, posts may resume sending via email.

Substack Data Privacy

How do I contact Substack if I want to exercise my privacy rights?

As provided under Article 28 EU GDPR AND 29 UK GDPR, Substack seeks to comply, and assist Publishers in complying, with Data Regulation obligations. This may include taking into account the nature of processing, the information available to Substack, and/or other actions such as deleting any personal data we have collected.

Substack is glad to assist in this regard by facilitating data subject access requests (“DSAR”). To submit a DSAR, Substack Publishers and other Substack users may contact privacy@substackinc.com.

How does Substack comply with Data Regulations?

Substack seeks to comply with applicable Data Regulations by providing public-facing policies and standard contractual clauses on our platform and in our agreements with Publishers. These policies and clauses can be found in our Privacy Policy, CCPA Policy, and Publisher Agreement (“Privacy Documents”).

For Publishers, who we consider to be Controllers (as defined under the GDPR), we have provided for Standard Contractual Clauses in our Publisher Agreement. These contractual clauses ensure appropriate data protection safeguards which can be used as a ground for data transfers from the EU (and the UK) to third countries.

Under our Privacy Policy and CCPA policy, we set forth the (i) the categories of personal data we collect, (ii) the way we process personal data; and (ii) disclosures of your rights under applicable Data Regulations.

I have a large email list and I’m concerned about the GDPR. How can I safely and lawfully import it to Substack?

Substack has implemented and utilizes several contractual and technical safeguards to protect the personal data we process. This includes measures such as our Privacy Documents, employing data encryption methods, and conducting data transfer impact assessments of third-party vendors and service providers.

However, it is ultimately the responsibility of a Publisher to ensure that they have a lawful basis to, first, import an email subscription list. Publishers should seek the advice of independent legal counsel to ensure that the importation of personal data falls within one of the six lawful basis categories. For more information on the lawful basis of importing email subscription lists, Publishers may view this resource.

Is Substack a data controller or a data processor?

For our Publishers, Substack generally considers itself to be the data processor as we process personal data on behalf of the controller who we’ve provided tools and features to control and determine their relationship with their subscribers.

We’ve done this in a few ways: (i) Publishers maintain a direct relationship between themselves and their readers; (ii) Publishers have the ability, through several settings and features, to moderate their reader’s comments and reactions; (iii) Publishers have the ability to import custom reader/subscription lists, (iv) Publishers have the ability to export these reader/subscription lists at any time; and (v) Publishers have the ability to communicate with their readers/subscribers off Substack. Substack Publishers are the data controllers of that audience, and they should ensure that they are in compliance with applicable Data Regulations.

In other instances, Substack may be the controller of personal data where we are responsible for determining the purposes and means of the processing of personal data. Specifically, this includes instances where we may collect personal data outside, and independent, of the relationship between a Publisher and their readers/subscribers. Where Substack is the controller, our use of that data will be used as provided in our Privacy Documents.

What can I find in the Substack Data Privacy section on the Help Center?

Important: The content below should not be considered as a substitution for Substack’s Terms of Use or policies found here. Articles on the Help Center (https://support.substack.com) do not modify nor set Substack’s terms and policies.

This section provides more information for our Publishers to better understand the interplay between our platform, features, and the applicable data privacy and protection laws (“Data Regulations”).

Please note that these articles are not legal advice. We cannot provide legal advice on how the GDPR specifically applies to our Publishers. Publishers should obtain independent legal counsel in the event a Publisher has specific questions about the applicability of the GDPR.

For more information on the Data Regulations which apply to Substack, you may review the following resources: EU GDPR, UK GDPR, and CCPA.

Substack iOS App FAQs

Can I change my email address on the Substack app?

At this time, changing your email address on Substack is not available on the Substack app. To change your email address on your Substack account, you’ll need to log into the web version of Substack.com and access your account Settings page (https://substack.com/settings).

Select “Edit” next to your email address and enter the new desired new email address. You’ll then receive emails from Substack to your current email address and your new email address asking you to confirm the change. You’ll be logged out of your Substack account on the web and Substack app and need to sign in with your new email address.

Note: If you enter an email address for an existing Substack account, note that all your subscriptions, publications, and other data will be transferred to that account. You’ll still need to confirm the email change request in the emails that’ll be sent to your current email address and the email address you’d like to change your account to.

Can I pick and choose which publication I want to receive app notifications about?

As long as you’re subscribed to a Substack and have push notifications enabled, you’ll receive an app notification about any new posts.

To completely turn off notifications on the Substack iOS app (which means you’ll no longer be subscribed to the publication), tap on your profile picture and tap on the Substack you want to stop receiving push notifications and/or emails from.

Then tap on the three dots in the top right corner and tap “Unsubscribe”.

Note: Turning off all notifications will unsubscribe you from the publication but will not cancel your subscription if you’re a paying subscriber. Learn more here on how to cancel your subscription.

Can my subscribers pay on the Substack iOS app?

For Substack creators with payments enabled in supported countries, subscribers will automatically be able to purchase paid subscriptions directly within the iOS app using Apple’s in-app payment system (IAP).

U.S. Only: Substack includes external links in the iOS app that take readers to the web to complete their purchase. Apple does not allow this outside the U.S., so international subscribers only see the in-app payment option when they upgrade to a paid subscription via the iOS app. Learn more here.

For creators

This update makes it easier for mobile-first readers to subscribe, and we hope that the seamless app upgrade flow will earn creators more revenue overall—but there are key differences in how payments are processed and how subscriber relationships are managed compared to the current Stripe and desktop experience.

In this guide, we’ll explain:

  • What to know about in-app payments
  • What countries do we support?
  • Adjusted Pricing for in-app payments
  • Keeping prices the same

What to know about in-app payments

Apple fees

Apple applies a service fee for in-app purchases, which is deducted from your take-home revenue along with Substack’s fee. You can choose to adjust your in-app prices to account for the fee, or keep prices the same and accept a lower per-subscriber payout.

Tip: Per Apple, “Apple provides an automated system that handles the calculation, collection, and remittance of transactional taxes (such as value added tax) and compliance requirements in more than 80 regions, including most major markets, for In-App Purchases on the App Store.”

Payout timing

When readers subscribe on the web, payouts go directly into your Stripe account. For subscriptions purchased in the App Store, readers pay Apple.

Apple then pays Substack for a month’s worth of subscriptions at a time, up to 45 days after the end of the month. Once Apple pays Substack, we’ll deposit your earnings into your Stripe account. This means that for all App Store subscriptions bought in January, you can expect to see earnings in your Stripe account by mid-March.

Substack will always deposit your earnings as soon as we receive them from Apple.

You can also view your Stripe payout history on the Earnings tab on the Stats page on the web or on the Substack app.

Tap “View Creator Stats” on the three dots on your profile. You can access a streamlined view of key metrics, including total free and paid subscribers and total revenue, with time-series charts.

Click on “Total earnings” to see a total net revenue and a breakdown of your payouts after all applicable fees. These payouts will mirror the payouts on your Stripe account and may include activity from sources other than Substack if you use your Stripe account for multiple projects or businesses.

Subscription management

  • Apple manages in-app subscriptions. You’ll still have access to the subscriber’s email address and payment history, but not their payment method.
  • Apple subscriptions cannot be paused. If you pause payments on your publication, this will not apply to subscriptions managed by Apple.
  • Subscribers cannot change their web subscriptions in the iOS app at this time. However, they can follow these steps to manage their in-app subscription on the iOS app. What if I leave Substack?

For subscribers who signed up through Apple’s in-app payment system (IAP), Apple does not transfer billing relationships between platforms. You’ll still have access to their email addresses, so you can contact them and invite them to subscribe again through another payment method.

Availability

In-app payments (IAP) in the Substack iOS app are currently available for publications using Stripe accounts in countries where Stripe is supported with some exceptions. Supported countries include:

  • United States
  • Canada and Mexico
  • United Kingdom
  • European countries, including the Nordics
  • Most Asian countries supported by Stripe
  • Most African countries supported by Stripe
  • Australia and New Zealand In the meantime, subscribers in all countries can continue to purchase paid subscriptions through the web checkout, as those payment methods remain unchanged.

Note: At this time, we only support Stripe Connect accounts and do not support Stripe Express accounts.

Enabling paid subscriptions in the Substack iOS app

This feature is automatically enabled for all publications with payments enabled, so you do not need to take any action on your end.

Select whether to adjust prices for in-app purchases or keep prices the same.

  • Adjusted pricing (Recommended): Apple charges an extra 15-30% on fees purchased through the app, so we automatically adjust your prices to be as close as possible to your existing pricing plan for your in-app subscribers.
  • Keep prices the same: In-app subscribers will pay exactly the same as subscribers on the web, as long as it’s a price point supported by the App Store. Otherwise, the price will be rounded up to the nearest supported price point. View more information on App Store price points here. The table below illustrates prices for a $10 monthly Substack subscription, comparing web prices with the two in-app pricing options: the recommended adjusted pricing or keeping prices the same.

Note: The iOS in-app payment option cannot be disabled while payments are connected. Any discounts or special offers you create on your Substack will apply only to web checkout, not to in-app subscriptions through Apple.

How can I manage my in-app subscribers?

On your Subscribers dashboard on the web version of Substack, you can view more details about the in-app subscriber by clicking on their email address. We’ll display that they subscribed via the app and when their paid subscription will automatically renew. If you have the “New paid subscriber” notification enabled on your publication’s Settings page, you’ll also receive an email and activity notification every time an in-app subscription is made.

Apple handles in-app subscriptions and subscription management directly. Readers can make changes, such as canceling their subscription or requesting a refund, directly on the App Store.

How do I cancel my paid subscription on the Substack iOS app?

At this time, canceling a paid subscription to a publication can only be done on a web browser by following these steps:

  1. Head to your Substack account Settings or substack.com and view a list of your subscriptions.

  2. Select the paid publication that you want to update.

  3. Scroll to the Account Actions section and tap where it says “To cancel your paid subscription, click here”.

  4. Tap “Cancel Subscription” to complete the process.

Note: Your paid subscriber benefits will continue until the end of the subscription plan’s billing cycle. For more detailed steps, please click here.

How do I edit my profile on the Substack iOS app?

To edit your profile on the Substack app, tap your profile and select “Edit” and you’ll be able to do the following:

  • Change your name, bio, photo, and profile theme.
  • Select which Substacks to display on your public Substack profile. These will appear under “Reads” on your profile and your profile may appear on a creator’s subscribers list on their profile.
  • Select which publications you own to display or not display on your public Substack profile.
  • Add social links to help people find you on other platforms. If you’d like to change the email address for your subscriptions or manage a subscription, you’ll need to head to your Substack account Settings page on a mobile browser or desktop.

If you’d like to hide or show what Substacks you subscribe to, follow these steps:

  1. Tap on your profile picture and then tap “Edit”.

  2. Scroll to the Subscriptions section.

  3. On the Subscription visibility screen, tap the toggles next to the Substacks you want to hide or show on your profile. Then click “Save”.

If you’d like to hide posts and notes that you like from appearing in the Likes tab on the app, follow these steps:

  • Tap on your profile and select the Settings gear icon at the bottom right of the app.
  • Tap on the Privacy & Safety section and scroll to the Privacy section. Tap the toggle to the left next to “Show likes on profile”. If you choose to hide what you read on Substack, what you write, or what you like, these will immediately be hidden on your profile on the Substack apps and on the web version of Substack once you save the changes.

How do I pay for a subscription on the Substack iOS App?

In this guide, we will go through paying for a publication on the Substack iOS App and managing your subscription.

  • Paying for a subscription on the iOS App (U.S Only)
  • Purchasing for a subscription (Outside of the U.S)
  • Managing your subscription If you’re a paid subscriber to a publication, you can read its paid posts directly in the Substack app. If you’re on a free plan, you’ll see a paywall message explaining that the post is only available to paying subscribers, along with options to upgrade.

Paying for a subscription on the Substack iOS app (U.S. only)

When you subscribe to a publication, you’ll see monthly, annual, or free plan options. Choose the plan you’d like, then click Subscribe. Selecting ‘Subscribe’ opens a new browser window where you can complete your subscription purchase on the web. This payment is processed directly by Substack and is not handled through Apple’s payment system.

Toggling to ‘Pay in app store’ uses Apple’s subscription process to complete your purchase through Apple. Prices for this option are often higher because Substack automatically adjusts them to account for Apple’s fees, ensuring publishers take home approximately the same revenue regardless of payment method.

Note: ‘Continue with in-app payments’ are processed by Apple, and all terms, conditions, and policies of Apple apply.

Once you complete the checkout process via ‘Continue with in-app payments’, you’ll receive a subscription confirmation email and receipt email from Apple. When you subscribe to a paid plan via the App Store, your plan will automatically renew.

To view paid content on the app, tap your profile picture; the publication will now appear under “Paid.”

Paying for a subscription (Outside of the U.S)

Payments made outside of the United States follow a similar payment flow in the Substack iOS app. After subscribing to a publication, international subscribers can select an upgrade option, which will take them through the iOS subscription flow to complete their purchase.

How can I make changes to my subscription on the app?

If you upgraded and became a paid subscriber directly on the app, you can make changes such as switching to an annual plan or canceling your subscription on the app.

Tap your profile picture in the top-right of the app, then Settings at the bottom. Select Payments from the menu and then tap on the publication you’d like to change.

You’ll be routed to the App Store where you can change your subscription plan.

  • To cancel your subscription, tap “Cancel Subscription”. Note: The only subscriptions that can be changed directly on the Substack iOS app are subscriptions that were purchased on the app. To manage your other paid subscriptions, open a new tab on a web browser and head to: substack.com

If you cancel a subscription, your paid subscriber benefits will continue until the end of the billing cycle. If you’d like to check the exact date when your benefits end, head to the Subscriptions section on a web browser.

The end date will appear under your Subscription status. Once your paid subscription ends, you’ll automatically become a free subscriber to that publication.

Can I request a refund for a canceled subscription?

Some purchases from the App Store may be eligible for a refund. To request a refund, follow these steps: Request a refund for apps or content that you bought from Apple

Why are the subscription plan prices on the iOS app different from those on Substack.com?

Creators can set different pricing plans in the app than they do on the web version of their publication.

If you’d like to subscribe at a lower rate, you can cancel your subscription in the App Store, and it won’t auto-renew. Then you can purchase a new subscription via the web version of the Substack publication.

We are unable to issue any refunds if you cancel on the App Store and subscribe at a lower rate on Substack.com.

What countries are in-app payments available?

In-app payments (IAP) in the Substack iOS app are currently available for publications using Stripe accounts in countries where Stripe is supported. These countries can include the United States, the United Kingdom, Canada, most European countries, including the Nordics, and most Asian countries supported by Stripe.

In the meantime, subscribers in all other countries can continue to purchase paid subscriptions through the web or mobile checkout, as those payment methods remain unchanged.

How do I subscribe to a new Substack and pay for a subscription on the iOS app?

This guide will cover how to subscribe to a Substack creator on the iOS app and how to pay for a subscription.

Subscribing to a Substack creator

You can easily subscribe to a Substack on your Home feed by tapping “Subscribe” next to their name, or you can discover new Substacks by clicking on a category that interests you at the top of your feed.

When on a Substack, the easiest way to subscribe is by tapping the Subscribe button.

What happens after I subscribe?

After you subscribe to a Substack on the Substack app, you’re now a free reader. You’ll receive a confirmation email from Substack with additional upgrade options if that creator offers paid posts and a Welcome email from them.

Any new posts will appear in your Inbox, and you can access past posts by tapping on your profile picture to see your whole library of subscriptions.

How do I pay for a subscription on the Substack iOS app?

Subscribers in the U.S. can upgrade their free subscription directly in the app. Click on the upgrade button after subscribing, and you’ll be prompted with a screen with two options.

Toggling ‘card’ opens a new browser window where you can complete your subscription purchase on the web. This payment is processed directly by Substack and is not handled through Apple’s payment system.

Selecting ‘Pay in app store’ uses Apple’s subscription process to complete your purchase. Prices for this option are often higher because Substack automatically adjusts them to account for Apple’s fees, ensuring publishers take home approximately the same revenue regardless of payment method. Please note that this payment is processed by Apple, and all terms, conditions, and policies of Apple apply.

Note: The option to continue with in-app payment will only appear for US-based subscribers. International subscribers will not see this option. The subscribe button will take international subscribers directly to Apple’s subscription process.

I already subscribed using In-App payments. How can I request a refund?

Some purchases from the App Store may be eligible for a refund. To request a refund, follow these steps: Request a refund for apps or content that you bought from Apple

How do I unsubscribe from a publication on the Substack iOS app?

To unsubscribe from a free subscription

Tap your profile picture to see the library of publications you subscribe to. Select the publication you’d like to unsubscribe from.

Tap the three dots on the top right corner of the screen and select “Unsubscribe”.

To unsubscribe and cancel a paid subscription

  1. On your web browser, log into your Substack account and head to your account Settings page.

  2. Click on the publication you’d like to make a change to.

  3. Scroll to your Account actions and tap “To cancel your paid subscription, click here”.

  4. Tap on “Cancel my subscription” to complete the process. For more information, please click here.

Note: Your paid subscriber benefits will continue until the end of the subscription plan’s billing cycle.

What does Archive mean on the Substack iOS app?

If you see a post you’d like to read at a later time in your reading queue or Inbox, you can archive the post on the Substack app.

To view your archive on the app, tap on your profile picture on the top right corner and select “Archive” icon on the bottom left.

Here, you’ll see all the posts that you’ve archived.

  • Once a post has been archived, you can un-archive it by clicking and holding on the article that is in your archive, then clicking on “unarchive”.

Will I still get emails if I have the Substack iOS app?

On the Substack iOS app, you can choose to receive push notifications about new posts, receive email newsletters, or receive notifications about new posts both via push and email.

If you’re not receiving email newsletters, it’s possible that when you downloaded the Substack app, you selected “Smart notifications”. With smart notifications, you’ll primarily receive push notifications about new posts in the app and may receive email notifications about posts you’ve missed if you haven’t opened the Substack app in a while.

How can I resume email deliveries of new posts and stop receiving push notifications from the Substack iOS app?

Tap your profile picture and select Settings.

On Settings, tap Notifications and scroll to the Newsletter Delivery section. Tap “Prefer email”. New posts will now be delivered via email and can be read directly in your email provider or in Inbox on the Substack app.

  • If you’d prefer to receive both email newsletters and push notifications, tap the “Both email and push” option.
  • If you’d prefer to only receive push notifications from the app, tap “Prefer push” on the app. If push delivery fails, posts may resume sending via email.

Video

Getting started with Live Video on Substack

Live video is a new way to reach your audience from the Substack app or your computer. We’ve designed it to be simple, fun, and seamlessly integrated with your Substack. Going live on Substack will immediately notify your subscribers, allowing you to break news as it happens, share behind-the-scenes footage, bring your audience into exclusive events, or host interactive AMAs. You can even paywall a live video to make it available only for your paid subscribers, creating an intimate viewing event.

In this guide

  • How to go live on the Substack app
  • Tips for a successful stream
  • Live video button guide
  • Frequently asked questions Tip: If you prefer to live stream from your computer, follow the steps in our detailed guide here.

How to go live

  1. Get the Substack iOS or Android app and open the app.

  2. Tap the orange “+” button on Home and select “Live”. Make sure to allow camera and microphone access.

  3. Add a title to your live video – subscribers will see this when they’re notified about the event via email or push notification.

  4. Select who can access your live video: everyone, all subscribers, or paid subscribers only.

  • Everyone: Any Substack user can access your stream and comment in the live video chat.
  • All subscribers: Any of your free and paid subscribers can access your stream.
  • Paid subscribers only: Only monthly, annual, and founding member paid subscribers can access your stream. If you don’t have payments enabled on your Substack, you will not see this option. Tip: For your first live videos, we recommend going live to “Everyone” or “All subscribers”. Once your audience gets in the habit of joining your streams, paywalling your live videos can be a great benefit for paid subscribers.
  1. Invite a co-host to go live with you! After clicking the orange “Next” button, you have the option to invite a co-host to stream with you or skip and do a solo stream. iOS and Android users can invite another creator, up to two, who’s on Substack to stream alongside them.

  1. Search for the user you want to invite to your stream. A Substack account is required for your guest before your stream goes live. If you don’t see a specific user, try searching via their full name. If that doesn’t work, try searching via their Substack handle. The Substack handle can be found in a user’s profile.

  2. If you still don’t see a user after searching by full name and handle, they may have disabled notifications and you should reach out to them via DMs (or off-platform) to make sure they turn on their live video notifications.

  3. Select your guest and tap the add button.

  1. Enter the preview room and go live!

Here, you can test your video and audio before you officially go live. If you invited a co-host, they’ll automatically join the preview room once they accept your invite.

When you’re ready to officially go live, click the orange go live button. After clicking “go live”, it takes approximately seven seconds before you’re officially live. You’ll notice a red pill at the top of your stream reading “LIVE” when you are officially live.

Tips for a successful stream

Tease your live video in advance

Live video on Substack is new to your subscribers so it’s important that you share the details about your first live video in advance so your subscribers know when the event is happening and how to join.

We recommend sending a dedicated post or chat (or both!) to your subscribers a couple of days before your first live video. Make sure to share the date and time of your live video along with directions for joining the stream.

Subscribers will need to get the app to tune into your live video and should turn on push notifications so they receive a notification when you go live.

Strong wifi connection

To ensure high quality audio and video, make sure you have a strong Wifi connection. Any location where you take FaceTime or Zoom calls is best. If you’re unsure about the quality of Wifi connection at a particular location, try FaceTiming a friend first. If the video is clear on FaceTime, you’re good to go.

Share the recording as a post

After you finish your live video, Substack automatically saves the recording as a draft post so you can send it to subscribers who weren’t able to join.

  • On desktop, head to your Substack’s Posts tab.
  • Select “Drafts” and click on your latest draft.
  • Edit the post and publish it just like any other video post. Tip: Just like a video post, you can add a paywall and use the clipping tool to edit the video.

Ken Klippenstein hosts a live video with Taylor Lorenz and publishes it as a post to his Substack

Live Video Button Guide

The buttons above allow you to share your live video link with your network and subscribers and access additional features designed to ensure a seamless live video experience.

Useful tips to note:

  • If you encounter poor or unstable internet connectivity during the live video, you may improve the connection by turning off your video or camera.
  • If your co-host disconnects or drops out during the session, simply click the icon with the person and the ‘+’ symbol to re-invite them or to invite a new co-host.

FAQs

If my Substack app crashes how can I rejoin my live video?

If your app crashes, re-open the Substack app. Your live video will exist in your reading queue on Home for up to two minutes. From there, you can re-join your live video and continue streaming to your viewers.

How can I add a co-host if I have already started my live video?

You can add a co-host after you’ve gone live on Substack. Just tap the person icon with the + sign. From there, you can invite up to two co-hosts to join you anytime during your live video. It’s a great way to keep the conversation lively and bring in new voices.

How can I set my live video chat to All, Paid or Founding subscribers only?

If you would like to set the live video chat so that Paid or Founding subscribers are the only subscribers that can chat, you may do so by going to your Substack Account Settings page. Scroll down to Notifications and under Allow live chats from, click on the drop-down menu. Select “Paid subscribers” or “Founding subscribers”.

How do I filter live video chat to show only questions?

To filter your chat to show only questions, toggle the setting in the top-right corner.

Note: This feature is currently only available on web/desktop Live streaming.

How do subscribers tune into my live video?

Once you go live, viewers can join to view the stream via:

  • Push or email notification: Most subscribers will join your live video via the push or email notification Substack sends immediately after you start your live video.
  • Reading queue: Your live video will automatically be inserted in your subscribers reading queues on the Substack app. In one tap, they can join your stream.
  • Other entry points: Subscribers can also join your live video from your publication. Soon, they’ll be able to join from your profile page and, if applicable, chat. How do I choose which audience gets a join notification if I have multiple publications?

To choose who gets a notification for your Live Video, go to the Live Video screen and click “Audience.” Pick the publication you want to send from, then use the drop-down (marked by the orange arrow in the screenshot) to select “subscribers” or “paid subscribers.”

Note: Any new subscribers who join during the live video will be added to the publication you selected.

Does Substack show a preview of my live video?

Yes, Substack shows the first 10 seconds of your live video in the reading queue so your audience can get a taste of what’s happening and join the stream.

How will I know when subscribers join my live video?

When someone joins your stream, a brief message is sent in the live video chat. Additionally, the viewer counts updates when someone new joins your stream.

How can I tell how many viewers I have on my live video?

At the top of the live video next to the eye icon, you’ll see a counter. This is the total viewer count since streaming began, not concurrent views. If you’d like to see the count for all viewers and current viewers, tap on the eye icon, and we’ll only show you the numbers.

Note: Current viewers are only available on iOS, but will be coming soon to Android.

Is there a delay between my video and what subscribers see?

There is a brief delay between your video and audio, and between what your viewers see, hear, and comment. This is standard for live video on social media platforms.

Can I leave the app while my live video is in progress?

If you leave the Substack app while your live video is running, participants will see a paused sign with no video. If you leave the stream for more than two minutes, the livestream will end.

Where can I view my scheduled, past, or cancelled live videos?

On web, head to your publication’s Dashboard and click Live Video. This will open a drop-down menu where you can edit or cancel scheduled streams, view cancelled live streams, or access recordings of past live videos.

Here, you can also make changes to the date and time of a scheduled live video. Click on the “Scheduled” tab and select the three dots next to the scheduled live video. Select “Edit” from the menu.

Select a new date or time from the calendar picker and click on “Save changes”.

Where can I see stats about my live video?

If you publish your live video recording as a post, you’ll be able to view stats about the post both on the web and the Substack app. At this time, live video stats for unpublished live video recordings are not available.

The post stats for published lived videos combine the data from the live video and the post. On the Substack iOS app, you can click on “View stats” on a post and in the Performance tab, view details such as total video plays and total watch time. The only metrics that will not be included are podcast downloads and subscription sources.

How do I disable live video and go live only using Audio?

With the new audio-only mode, you can go live without using your camera. Your audience can still listen, comment, and follow just like any regular livestream. It’s a great option for relaxed updates or if you’d rather not be on camera. Just tap the camera icon to turn video off—or back on—anytime during your stream.

What does music mode do when I livestream?

On the Substack iOS app, creators can enable music mode during livestreams to preserve the original sound of their livestream by disabling audio processing and noise cancellation.

When you go live, tap the music note icon on the right side of the screen and click “Change Wide Spectrum”.

Then select “Wide Spectrum” from the pop up window.

Can I schedule a live video?

Yes! You can schedule a live video by following these detailed steps: How do I schedule a live video on Substack?

Guide to video posts on Substack

Note: These features are only available on video posts, not videos uploaded and embedded into a regular post.

In this guide, we’ll show you how to use tools for publishing video posts that you can record or upload directly to your Substack publication.

On video posts, you can:

  • Automatically generate transcripts
  • Share audio from the video directly to your podcast RSS feed
  • Offer exclusive content to paying subscribers and a free preview of paid videos to all viewers
  • Easily share clips of your video in a note or on social media

How to get started on a video post

  1. Sign into your Substack account on the web. Click “Create” and select “Video”.

  2. In the draft post, click “Record” to record a video using your computer or “Select file” to upload a video file.

  • If you record a video, you’ll enter the Substack Recording Studio. Here, you’ll have a suite of video tools such as the ability to host guests, add a logo to your video, and share your screen. Once you’ve finished recording, make sure to click “Save to drafts” so that you can edit text and customize the post’s settings.

  • If you upload a video file, the upload will automatically begin, and the video’s settings dashboard will appear. Tip: For the fastest processing time for a video upload, follow these tips:

  • 1080p/1440p (2K) or smaller

  • H.264 video CODEC

  • Bitrate 8Mbps or below

  • Frame rate (fps) between 5 and 120

  • AAC audio CODEC

  • Recommended maximum size is 20 GB

  1. While the video upload is processing, you can write text or edit your draft body.

  2. Once the video is uploaded, the video will appear at the top of the post and cannot be moved. Select the following icons:

Podcast icon

If you have a Substack podcast setup and want to share the audio on external podcast players, slide the toggle next to “Add audio to podcast RSS feed”. If you want to replace the podcast audio with a different, higher-quality audio file, select the three dots and click “Replace”. Drag or upload the audio file you’d like to use for the podcast RSS feed.

Transcript icon

Whether published as a podcast episode or not, every video post that has audio will automatically receive an AI-generated transcript that will appear as a collapsable transcript on the post. To remove the transcript, slide the toggle to the left next to “Show on post” on the transcript settings.

Click “Edit” to change the text on the AI-generated transcript or select the up arrow to upload your own .txt, .srt, or .vtt transcript file

With the speakers features, you assign or re-assign who’s speaking by entering a name or searching for their Substack profile name. If the transcript generator identifies more speakers than your episode has, you can merge speakers.

Clips icon

The scissors icon is how you’re able to generate Video Clips. Learn more about clips here: How can I create a clip of a video on Substack?

Preview icon

Flexible paywalls offer free subscribers a taste of premium content. You can select a portion of your video to offer for free, which will also sync with the audio feed.

To create a free preview, follow these steps:

  • Click on the lock icon, and select which parts you’d like to be available to all viewers from the video timeline.
  • Select “Set free preview”.
  • When viewing the free preview, free subscribers will be prompted to upgrade to a paid subscription to view the full video. Thumbnail icon

Here, you can set a thumbnail for your post by uploading an image or selecting a still from the video.

Download icon: Select this icon to download the video or audio.

  1. Once you’re ready to publish the video post, select “Continue”.

If you have a Substack podcast setup, you’ll see the option to add the audio to your podcast RSS feed and edit the episode notes. Toggling this on lets subscribers listen to the video audio on external podcast players.

After adjusting your publishing settings such as selecting which audience can view the post, adding tags, or selecting the post preview image, select “Send to now”.

Tip: To remove a video post that’s appearing in your Podcast tab, click on the the settings button in the video post. Scroll down to “Add audio to podcast RSS feed.” Toggle this off and save the post. This should remove it from the podcast tab.

What will my viewers see?

Easier controls

Subscribers can watch your videos with ease:

  • Clicking on the “cc” icon enables closed captions or subtitles on your video posts.
  • Selecting the two screens icon allows viewers to watch videos in a floating window
  • Ability to share a specific timestamp in the video by hovering over the spot in the video timeline and clicking “Share from 0:00:0”. Tip: On the Substack app, viewers can watch your video posts. The apps feature an inline player, allowing your viewers to read and watch at the same time, and keep their place when navigating back to a video they watched partway.

Frequently asked questions

Where can I see how many times my video has been played?

On your Posts page, expand the video post to see detailed statistics. We’ll show your video play count, hours watched, details about new subscriptions, and how many times your post has been shared.

How long does it take for a video to process?

Processing times depend on the file size. While this happens, you can write a draft using the post editor. To cancel the upload, click “X” next to the video upload on the video’s settings page.

How can I connect my YouTube account to my Substack?

By syncing your YouTube account to Substack, creators can automatically upload Substack videos to a YouTube channel for extra distribution.

When you publish a video post, your videos can now be automatically uploaded to your YouTube account. In this guide, we’ll show you how to:

  • Connect your Substack and YouTube account

  • Publish a video post

  • How do I share Shorts on YouTube? Things to note:

  • Before you sync your accounts, ensure that you’ve created a YouTube channel first.

  • If you’re planning on auto-uploading a video longer than 15 minutes, make sure that your YouTube channel is verified or the upload will fail.

  • The YouTube account will be synced to your Substack profile and not the publication. If a number of creators contribute to a publication and publish videos, we recommend using a shared YouTube account or channel to ensure videos are uploaded to the right place.

Connecting your accounts

  1. Connect your Substack and YouTube account via your dashboard banner or in the Connected accounts section on your publication’s Settings page.

Note: If you don’t see the Edit button or the option to upload clips, it likely means no clips have been generated yet. No worries—you can follow this guide to learn how to create a clip manually.

  1. Grant permissions to Substack when linking your accounts.

Note: If you want to disconnect your YouTube account from your Substack, head to the Connected accounts section on your publication’s Settings page and select “Disconnect”. Any videos that were uploaded to YouTube will remain on your YouTube channel (unless you manually delete them on YouTube). If you delete a Substack video post, the auto uploaded video will also remain on YouTube.

Publishing a video

Free video

  1. When you publish a “Video post,” we will automatically check the “Auto-upload it to YouTube” box. You can choose to un-check this on a post-by-post basis.

  2. In the post’s publish settings, you’ll be able to customize the YouTube title, description, and privacy settings.

Note: By default videos are are selected to private visibility but you can set them to unlisted or public. By default, the box next to “Include Substack watermark” will be checked– this means a small watermark with a Substack logo and URL of your publication will appear in the upper-left corner of the video. To remove a watermark from appearing, un-check the box.

  1. Once you publish your post, you can check the status on the Share center or in Latest post section on Home.

Tip: If the video fails to upload, or fails at any time during the processing phase, we’ll display a failed status with a retry button.

  1. Once your video has published to YouTube, you can make further edits on YouTube studio.

Free preview of paid post

If you set up a free preview of a paid post, only the preview portion will be uploaded to YouTube video. In the description of the YouTube video, we’ll automatically add a link to your paid post on Substack.

How do I add Shorts to my YouTube connected account?

Want to share short clips on your YouTube channel? Substack now makes it easy with an auto-generated clips feature that lets you upload up to two short videos directly to your YouTube channel.

After you record a live video or upload a video to your video post, head over to the Post Editor page. Scroll down to Connected accounts and click Edit. From there, you’ll be able to choose up to two clips that were automatically generated from your video to share on YouTube Shorts.

Note: If you don’t see the Edit button or the option to upload two clips, it likely means no clips have been generated yet. No worries—you can follow this guide to learn how to create a clip manually.

After selecting up to two Shorts to upload, click Update. When you publish your post, the selected Shorts will automatically appear in your YouTube Creator Dashboard under the Shorts tab.

How do I disable Auto publishing to YouTube shorts?

Navigate to your Settings page, select Growth features from the left-hand menu, and scroll down to Enable auto-publishing clips to YouTube Shorts. Toggle the setting off to disable automatic uploads.

How can I create a clip of a video on Substack?

To create a clip of your video on Substack, you can use the AI-powered video clipping tool or make your own clips directly from the post editor.

Follow these steps:

  1. Click “Create” and select “Video”.

  2. On your video post draft, record a video directly from your device or upload a video file.

  3. When the video has finished uploading, click on the scissors icon which opens the Clips editor. A selection of AI-generated clips in Portrait or Landscape mode will be available for you to share.

  4. To edit the captions on a clip, click on the three dots and open the captions editor. Here, you can edit the placement and designs of the caption. If you want to edit the text of the caption, head to the Transcript icon and edit the text there. If you want to download a clip without captions, slide the toggle to the left next to “Burned in captions”.

  5. Select “Create” to customize your own clip. Here, you’ll be able to select where in the video you’d like the clip to start and end.

Tip: The maximum length of a manually created clip is five minutes.

What can I do with a clip?

Once you’re satisfied with a clip, click the share or download icons on the clip. Clicking “Share” will post the clip as a Note, so it can get discovered in the Substack network. Clicking “Download” will download the clip as an .mp4 file.

Once clips have been generated of your video post, you’ll receive an activity notification and a shareable clips email from Substack.

You can also easily share clips of your video post from your Substack app. Tap on a post and select “Assets”. You can share AI generated clips to Notes, Instagram Stories and Reels, and more.

How can I publish videos using the Substack Recording Studio?

Note: The Substack Recording Studio is only available on the web version of your Substack. All creators on Substack who have at least 10 free subscribers will see the Substack Recording Studio option.

The Substack Recording Studio provides creators with the same suite of video tools used in Live Video without having to go live. Record your content and publish whenever you are ready. Key features of the Substack Studio includes:

  • Solo or Guest Recording: Record by yourself or invite up to two guests

  • Custom Branding: Add your publication’s logo or wordmark directly to the video

  • Screen sharing: Present your screen to your viewers with picture-in-picture support

  • Automated thumbnails: Customize auto-generated previews or upload your own How to get started

  • Sign into your Substack account on the web. Click “Create” and select “Recording studio”.

    1. When prompted, allow Substack to access your browser’s microphone and camera.
    1. You can invite a guest and have up to three total people in the recording. Enter the creator’s Substack handle to send an invitation. Invitations will appear as a web and app pop-up, push notification, or email notification. Guests can join the video session from the Substack app (iOS or Android) or from their computer– once they accept your invite, they’ll automatically join the preview room.
    1. Click “Start recording”. The system takes approximately 20 seconds to connect and then a red “Recording” indicator will appear at the top of the screen. To stop the video recording, click “End”.
    1. Once you end the recording, a post will automatically begin generating and you will be redirected to your publication’s Home. You can save the video to your Drafts or publish it immediately. You’ll receive an email notification with a link once the draft video post is ready. Tip: If your recording is a long enough, thumbnails will auto generate for you. To customize your video thumbnail, select “Edit thumbnail” and you’ll have the options to select your own style and add a caption.

How do I share my screen?

If you’d like to share your screen during the recording, select “Share Screen”. For the best screen sharing experience, use Google Chrome as your browser. This is also available on Safari, however, it is limited to sharing windows and picture-in-picture is not supported.

If you’re using Chrome, you’ll see the options to share a tab, window, or an entire screen. If you’re using a Macbook and want to show your viewers an app such as Finder or Notes, click on “Window” and select the app from the list of open apps.

If you’d like to be on the page that you’re sharing and still see yourself, allow Substack picture-in-picture permissions. A picture-in-picture (PIP) is a feature that lets you view the entire live stream in a smaller floating window.

To end the screen sharing, click on “Stop sharing” or the video icon that has a slash across it in the PIP window.

Screen sharing with audio is coming soon.

How do I add a logo to my video?

You can overlay your brand assets directly onto your video. At the top of the video screen, click on “Brand and effects”. Slide the toggle next to “Logo” and you’ll have the option to select your publication’s logo or wordmark or upload your own asset. Select where you’d like the logo to appear to your viewers on the video: top left, top right, or bottom left.

How can I stream Substack videos on my TV?

If you have an Apple TV or a Google TV, you can now watch your favorite Substack videos directly on your screen.

In this guide, we’ll show you:

  • How to download the Substack TV app
  • How to sign into the Substack TV app
  • How to play videos on the Substack TV app

How to download the Substack TV app

For Apple TV

  1. Open the App Store: From the Apple TV home screen, navigate to and select the App Store icon.

  2. Search for the Substack app: Use the search feature and select the Substack app from the search results. Download and install the app.

  3. Open the App: Once installation is complete, the Get button will change to Open. Select it to launch the Substack app. You can also find it on your Apple TV home screen.

Tip: You need a physical Apple TV like this. The Apple TV+ app on other smart TVs does not support non-Apple content. Only a physical Apple TV supports non-Apple content.

For Google TV

  1. When you’re signed in to your Google TV, it will display Google TV on the top left or top right of the screen.

  2. On your Google TV’s home screen, navigate to Apps or the search icon (magnifying glass) and search for Substack.

  3. Click on the App and download it to your TV.

  4. Once installation is complete, select Open to launch the app, or find the new app in your ‘Apps’ section on the home screen.

Tip: Not sure if you have an Android TV? If you see a Google TV logo or Google TV sign-in on your screen, you have an Android TV. Android TVs also may show a settings or clock icon in the top right corner of the screen. You can confirm if your TV is running on Android by checking the System or Specifications in your TV’s settings. Read this article to learn more.

How to sign into the Substack TV app

For Apple TV

At this time, email login is not supported on Apple TV. The easiest way to sign in to the Substack app is to scan the QR code on the screen with your device’s camera.

Note: This is an example QR code and will not be valid. A new QR code will be generated for you when you open the Substack TV app.

If you scan the QR code

A new browser window will open on your phone. Make sure the code on the TV screen matches the code on your phone and click “Sign in to TV”.

Note: This is an example code and will not be valid. A new code will be generated for you when you scan the QR code on your TV.

If you enter the URL

A new browser window will open on your phone. Enter the code on your phone that you see on your TV and select “Continue”.

Logging in by email for Google and Android TV

To log in with Google on Android TV, you can scan the QR code as described above. We also support logging in by email. To log in by email:

  1. Navigate to the downloaded Substack app

  2. Click on sign in, then log in with your email

  3. Enter your email details and follow the sign-on flow

How to play videos on the Substack TV app

Once you’ve successfully signed into the Substack TV app, you’ll see a For You carousel of videos and Substacks that you’re subscribed to.

For you: A combination of videos from your Substacks and feed suggestions.

Subscriptions: Substacks that you’re subscribed to that have at least one video post you have access to.

When you click on one of your Substack subscriptions on the TV, it’ll open an archive of videos that they’ve published.

Things to note:

  • New videos from your favorite Substacks will automatically upload to the Substack TV app and either appear in For You or under Subscriptions.
  • At the moment, there are limited player functionalities but more improvements are coming.
  • If you want to unsubscribe from a Substack, you’ll need to do it on your computer or on the Substack iOS or Android app.

How do I add a video to a Substack post?

If you have a video you’d like to share with your subscribers, you can publish a video post or embed a video into a regular post directly from your computer.

Create a video post

With video posts on desktop, you can:

  • Automatically generate transcripts
  • Share audio from the video directly to your podcast RSS feed
  • Offer exclusive content to paying subscribers and a free preview of paid videos to all viewers
  • Easily share clips of your video in a note or on social media

  1. Sign into your Substack account and on Home select “Create” and click on “Video”.

  2. In the draft video post, click “Record” to record a video on your computer or “Select file” to upload a video.

  • If you record a video, you’ll enter the Substack Recording Studio. Here, you’ll have a suite of video tools such as the ability to host guests, add a logo to your video, and share your screen. Once you’ve finished recording, make sure to click “Save to drafts” so that you can edit text and customize the post’s settings.

  • If you upload a video file, the upload will automatically begin, and the video’s settings dashboard will appear. Tip: For the fastest processing time for a video upload, follow these tips:

  • 1080p/1440p (2K) or smaller

  • H.264 video CODEC

  • Bitrate 8Mbps or below

  • Frame rate (fps) between 5 and 120

  • AAC audio CODEC

  • Recommended maximum size is 20 GB

  1. While the video upload is processing, you can write text or edit your draft body.

  2. The video will appear at the top of the draft post and cannot be moved. With the video editor, you can do a variety of things such as editing the AI-generated transcript to creating a preview for free viewers.

Learn more here on how to edit your video on Substack.

  1. Once you’re ready to publish the video post, select “Continue”.

  2. If you have a Substack podcast setup, you’ll see the option to add a video to your podcast RSS feed and edit the show notes. Toggling this on lets subscribers listen to the video audio on external podcast players.

  3. After adjusting your publishing settings such as selecting which audience can view the video post, adding tags, or selecting the post preview image, select “Send to now”.

Tip: On the Substack app, viewers can watch your video posts. The apps feature an inline player, allowing your viewers to read and watch at the same time, and keep their place when navigating back to a video they watched partway.

Embed a video into a post

  1. On Home, select “Create” and click on “Article” from the drop-down menu.

  2. Click on the video camera icon in the editor toolbar and record or upload a video.

  3. Select a still from the video to use as a thumbnail or upload your image.

  4. The video will appear in the body of the draft and can be moved by dragging and dropping the video within the draft.

Tip: The video types accepted are 3GP, AAC, AVI, FLV, MP4, MOV, and MPEG-2; the recommended maximum size is 20 GB.

Learn more here on how to embed a video into a Substack post.

How do I embed a video in a Substack post?

On Home, select “Create” and click on “Article” from the drop-down menu. From there, you can upload a video or record one directly, whether on desktop or mobile.

Note: The video embed feature differs from the video posts feature, where you can offer free previews of paid videos. If you’d like to create a video post, select “Video” instead of “Article”.

Upload a video as an embed

  1. When you’re ready to embed a video, select the video icon from the editor toolbar.

  2. Click “Upload” and select the video you want to add from your device.

Tip: The video types accepted are 3GP, AAC, AVI, FLV, MP4, MOV, and MPEG-2; the recommended maximum size is 20 GB.

  1. The upload will automatically begin and will appear in the draft. If you’d like to change the placement of the video, you can just select and drag the video to the desired spot.

  2. Once you’ve finished your draft and are ready to publish, the post can be sent to everyone or paid subscribers.

Record a video as an embed

  1. Select “Record video” and a preview of your video will pop-out into a new screen.

  2. Click on the red circle to start recording and click on the red circle again to stop recording. Click on the microphone or video camera icons to select different input sources. To flip the video, select the two arrow.

  3. Click on “Stop” and your video recording will automatically begin uploading and appear in the draft. If you’d like to change the placement of the video, you can just select and drag the video to the desired spot.

I have payments enabled– can I embed a video behind a paywall?

To embed a video behind a paywall, insert the paywall in the draft and embed the video file by following the steps above. By using the video embed feature, your videos will be hosted on Substack and for paid subscribers only.

How does a video embed look like in an email to my readers?

If you send the post as an email to your readers, a thumbnail image of the video will appear in the email. Clicking the post’s title will direct readers to the web version of the post or the Substack app if they have that installed.

How do I go live on Substack using my computer?

Live video on Substack gives creators a real-time way to communicate and engage with their subscribers. By expanding live video to desktop on Substack, creators can now interact with their audience however they choose: directly from desktop or from the Substack app. If you prefer to go live from the Substack iOS or Android app, follow the guide here.

In this guide, we’ll show you:

  • What you need before going live
  • How to live stream from your desktop
  • What happens after your live video ends

What you need before going live

Here are our tips before going live.

  1. All creators on Substack who have at least 10 free subscribers are eligible to use live video on Substack.

  2. Think in advance about your topic and who you want your audience to be. You can choose who can watch: everyone, all subscribers, or paid subscribers only.

  3. Be sure to have a stable internet connection. You can test your speed here: speedtest.net

  4. Contributors and admins can schedule a live stream for another creator on your publication.

How to live stream from your desktop

  1. Sign into your Substack account and click “Create” then “Live” from the drop-down menu.

  2. Grant permissions to Substack to access your browser’s microphone and camera.

Note: When streaming from your computer, you can use a webcam or external microphone.

  1. On the right panel you can do the following:
  • Create a title for your live video and select who can view your live stream. If you have payments enabled on your Substack, you can limit the video to paying subscribers only.
  • Schedule a live video by clicking on the toggle next to “Schedule for later”.
  • If you have multiple Substacks, you can choose which publication to go live on each time you stream, instead of being limited to your primary publication.
  • Select if you’d like to notify your subscribers via email once the live video starts.
  1. If you want to expand your reach, we recommend inviting a co-host to join your stream. Live video on Substack supports up to three total streamers. Enter the creator’s Substack handle and we’ll send them an invitation to join.

Note: Your co-host can join the live video and stream from either desktop or the Substack app.

  1. Enter the preview room where you can test your video and audio before you officially go live. If you’ve invited a co-host, they’ll automatically join the preview room once they accept your invite. Try to frame your face within the “visible area on mobile” as this allows a better viewing experience for your mobile app viewers.

  2. Select “Go live” and your live video will connect and it’ll take approximately seven seconds before you’re officially live. You’ll notice a red pill at the top of your stream reading “LIVE” when you are officially live.

  3. To end the live video, click on “End” in the top left corner of the screen.

What happens after your live video ends

Your recording is automatically saved as a draft so that you can publish it as a post for those who missed the live video.

Frequently Asked Questions

Can I share my screen during the live video?

Screen sharing during a live video is possible if you stream from your computer. If you’d like to share your screen, enter the preview room and click on “Share screen”. For the best screen sharing experience, use Google Chrome as your browser. This is also available on Safari, however, it is limited to sharing windows and picture-in-picture is not supported.

If you’re using Chrome, you’ll see the options to share a tab, window, or an entire screen. If you’re using a Macbook and want to show your viewers an app such as Finder or Notes, click on “Window” and select the app from the list of open apps.

If you’d like to be on the page that you’re sharing and still see yourself, allow Substack picture-in-picture permissions. A picture-in-picture is a feature that lets you view the entire live stream in a smaller floating window.

To end the screen sharing, click on “Stop sharing” or the video icon that has a slash across it in the picture-in-picture window.

Tip: If you’re seeing an infinite amount of windows pop-up when screen sharing, this may be happening because you’re sharing your entire screen with the live video page visible. To fix this, click on “Stop sharing” and be sure to share a window or browser tab instead of your entire screen.

How do I limit who can participate in chat during the live video?

Each stream includes a live chat where viewers can ask questions or comment. You can customize chat access by going to your Substack Account Settings and scrolling to the Notifications section. Next to “Allow live video chats from”, select who you’d like to be able to chat during your live video.

If you notice a viewer being active in the chat and you’d prefer that they stop, you can ban them from commenting. While streaming from your computer, click on the three dots next to their profile name in chat. From the menu, select “Ban from commenting”.
