This will ban them from commenting during your live stream and also from commenting on your publication posts. To un-ban a viewer from commenting, follow the steps here.

Will my subscribers be notified about the live video?

Depending on your subscribers’ preferences, they may receive either an app push notification or an email notification once you go live.

Scheduling is available on the Substack iOS and Substack Android apps and on the web. Learn more here on how scheduling works on your computer.

If you notice a viewer in your live stream chat and would like them to join your live video, you can invite them directly from the chat. Click on the three dots next to their name and select “Invite to stream”. They’ll receive an app and desktop notification to join your live video.

How do I edit details about my scheduled live video such as the title or date?

On web, click on Live video on your publication’s Dashboard. Head to the “Scheduled” tab and click on the three dots next to the scheduled live video. Select “Edit” from the menu.

On the “Edit scheduled stream” page, you’ll be able to edit the title of the upcoming live stream, change the date and time using the calendar picker, and also select whether you’d like to notify your subscribers via email when your live video starts.

How do I join a live video that I am invited to on Substack?

Joining a live video on Substack as a guest is easy and a great way to connect with others in real time. In this guide, we’ll walk you through how to accept an invite and what to expect before you go live.

On the Substack app

Ensure that you have the Substack app installed, enabled live invites from “Everyone” or “People I follow or subscribe to”, and have live video push notifications turned on.

Head to your app Settings, tap Notifications, then select Preferences. Scroll to the Live tab and next to “Live video guest invites”, check the box next to Push. Next to “Allow live invites from”, select either “Everyone” or “People I follow or subscribe to”.

The Host can go live immediately or schedule a live video in the future. Once the Host has invited you on their end, you’ll receive a push notification about the event. You can always access the invitation in the Activity section the app.

For a live video that’s about to start, click “Accept” on the invitation. You’ll be in Preview mode where you can grant the Substack app camera and microphone permissions (if you haven’t already). If you’d like to notify your subscribers about the live video, select the toggle to on next to “Email subscribers of Joyce.” Click “Join live video” and you’ll join the host.

For a live video that’s going to happen in the future, we recommend doing a test run so that you can familiarize yourself with how live video works on Substack. On the practice run screen, you’ll need to allow the Substack app to access your camera and microphone—this helps everything run smoothly during your broadcast. Click on “Allow,” and you should be all set.

If you don’t see the screen above but see a similar one like the example below, you’ll need to go to your device settings and enable the camera and microphone.

We recommend that you follow the setup of the live video practice run to familiarize yourself with the live video layout before going live. The Host will appear in the upper section of the screen, with your video displayed below. After closing the practice run, you’ll be able to add your live video to your calendar and also share the scheduled live video invite using your phone’s share control panel.

On desktop

If you want to join a live video on Substack from your computer, first ensure that you have a Substack account and can receive live video invitations. Head to your Account Settings and scroll to the Notifications section. Next to “Allow live video guest invites from”, select from the drop-down menu “Everyone” or “People I follow or subscribe to”.

Once the Host sets up the live video and invites you, you’ll receive a pop-up notification on your screen. We recommend that prior to the live video, you sign into to your Substack account so that you can easily join the live stream. Click “Accept” and grant camera and microphone permissions to Substack. When you’re ready, select “Join preview room”.

Once you’re in the Preview Room, you can test your video and audio and prepare with your host. Try to frame your face within the “visible area on mobile” as this allows a better viewing experience for your mobile app viewers.

Once your host is ready, they’ll select “Go live” on their end and the live stream will connect and start for anyone who’s joined the stream. If you want to leave the live stream, click on “Leave” in the top left corner at any time.

Learn more here on getting started with Live Video on Substack.

Invited via SMS, text, or email?

If you receive an invitation via SMS, text, or email, tap the link in the message. That link will take you directly to the live video you’ve been invited to join. Tapping the link will automatically open the Substack App (or prompt you to download it if you don’t have it yet). From there, you’ll be walked through a simple onboarding flow—don’t worry, it’s quick and user-friendly.

The steps will look very similar to the ones outlined above, designed to help you get comfortably set up. Once you’ve gone through the onboarding process, you can test-run the live video so you can feel confident and ready before the event. Just follow the on-screen prompts, and you’ll be good to go.

How do I record in HD video on Substack?

If you stream live video on Substack or use the recording studio feature, you can now publish high-resolution (HD) videos for your readers to watch on the Substack app or web.

Things to note:

  • HD video is available on the Substack iOS and Android apps. To publish an HD video post, make sure you’re on the latest app version.
  • HD recording is on by default for anyone live streaming or recording on Substack, including guests in a recording session.
  • You can’t convert an uploaded video to HD. Only videos that were live streamed or recorded have the HD option. How does HD video work?

A high-resolution file is recorded locally on each participant’s computer or phone, then uploaded to the cloud once the session ends. Recording locally prevents pixelation, lag, and dropped frames in the file saved to your post draft. When your live stream or recording session ends, click “Keep as a draft.”

For live streams, this doesn’t affect the quality of the stream itself — it just ensures you have an HD recording available to publish afterward.

How do I publish a HD video?

Every live video or recording studio session saves both a regular (SD) and a high-resolution (HD) file. By default, the SD file is saved to your post draft. While your video is processing, you can open the draft to check its status.

Once the recording is ready, click “Switch to HD,” or click the video icon in the video editor and select “High resolution landscape” to switch between SD and HD, then click “Apply changes.” If your live stream or video recording is very short, you may notice that the HD video may be trimmed by a few seconds compared to the SD video.

Note: HD recording is on by default for all users. To opt out of the HD option, contact our Support team.

How do I schedule a live video on Substack?

With live video on Substack, you can engage with an audience in real time by going live right away or scheduling a live session in advance. Scheduling is available on the Substack iOS and Substack Android apps and on the web.

In this guide, we’ll show you how to:

  • Schedule a live video on the Substack iOS app
  • Schedule a live video on desktop
  • Let your subscribers know about the live stream
  • Make changes to your live stream

How to schedule a live video on the Substack iOS app

  1. On the Substack iOS app, tap the “+” sign on Home.

  2. Tap Live and “Right now” next to Scheduling.

  3. Your device’s date and time picker will appear automatically. Select a date and time that can be between 15 minutes or three months from now. Tap “Save”.

  4. You’ll see a confirmation message that your live video has been scheduled for a future time.

More information can be found in our Getting Started with Live Video guide here.

Starting your scheduled live video

The window to start your scheduled live video opens 15 minutes before your stream is scheduled to begin. There are three ways you can start your live video within the 15-minute start window:

  1. Open your Substack app and start the stream from your reading queue (see image).

  2. Tap the bell icon in the bottom right corner, then select “Scheduled Live Videos.” Under the “Upcoming” section, choose the Live Video you’ve scheduled to view and start the live video.

  3. Go through the regular live stream compose flow.

How to schedule a live video on desktop
  • Sign into your Substack account and select “Create” then click on “Live video”.
  • In the right dashboard, click on the toggle “Schedule for later”. Select a date and time in the future and click on “Schedule live video.”
How to let your subscribers know about your live video

If you’ve scheduled a live video on the Substack iOS app, you’ll see a confirmation message on your device. Tap on “Share now”

In your device’s share center, tap on “Copy link”.

If you’ve scheduled your live video on dekstop, a confirmation will appear where you can copy the link to the live video. You can also access the scheduled live video in the Activity section of your Substack account.

Announce your future livestream by pasting the direct link in a note or post. We also recommend using this link to share your live video on social media. You can also share specific details—such as the topic, any collaborators, or what to expect—to build anticipation and encourage subscribers to tune in.

To re-grab the share link for your live video, tap on the Activity notification on the app and then the three dots in the upper right corner of the screen. Tap “Copy link”.

Your subscribers will also receive a push, email, and Activity notification about the upcoming live video. In the email and Activity notification, they’ll see the option to add the reminder to their calendars.

How to make changes to your scheduled live stream

If something’s come up and you need to change the timing of the live video or cancel it, you can easily make changes.

On the Substack iOS app

Tap on the “+” sign in Home and select “Live”. In the upper right corner, tap on the calendar icon. Here, you’ll see all the upcoming live videos that you’ve scheduled.

Tap on the event you’d like to make changes to.

  • To change the date or time: Select scheduled date and time next to “Scheduling” and pick a new date or time. Tap “Update scheduled time.”
  • To cancel your live video: Select “Cancel live video”. On desktop

On web, click on Live video on your publication’s Dashboard. Head to the “Scheduled” tab and click on the three dots next to the scheduled live video. Select “Edit” from the menu.

Select a new date or time from the calendar picker and click on “Save changes”.

Alternatively, you can click on “Stream page” on the scheduled live video and select “Edit” to make changes to the date and time of the live stream.

FAQS

How do I share the link to my upcoming Live Video?

After scheduling your Live Video, tap the bell icon in the bottom right corner of the app. A banner titled “Scheduled Live Videos” will appear at the top of your screen—tap this banner to view your upcoming Live Video sessions. To copy the link, tap the three dots (•••) next to the session, then select “Copy Link” from the menu.

Are subscribers or viewers able to confirm if they’ll be attending an upcoming live video?

At this time, you won’t be notified or be able to see a list of attendees. Attendees can add the live video event to their calendars as well as see reminder notifications on the app as the live video event nears.

Is there a limit to how many live videos I can schedule in advance?

There are no limits to how many live videos you can schedule in advance on Substack.

How do I add an upcoming live video to my calendar?

On the web, head to your Activity section and click on “Add to Calendar” next to the upcoming live video you’d like to watch. You’ll be able to add the event to calendars such as Google, Apple, and Outlook.

How do I select a thumbnail for my live video post on Substack?

When you go live on Substack from your computer, a video post will automatically be generated once your stream ends. You’ll have the option to either auto-publish the live video post or review a draft and then manually publish it yourself.

Before your live video post is published, you can edit the thumbnail that subscribers will see, change the title, or select the audience of the video.

We’ll automatically generate a thumbnail (image and caption) for your live video post. To change the thumbnail in the auto-publish flow, select from the gallery of auto-generated options. To change the thumbnail on your draft video post, select “Edit thumbnail” in the post editor and select from the auto-generated options or upload your own image.

Note: The thumbnail pictures and captions are captured from the images and audio in your live video.

If you don’t see a “Edit thumbnail” option, this means that we weren’t able to generate any thumbnails with an image and caption. In this case, the thumbnail for your live video post will be a random picture from your video. However, you can always change the thumbnail by heading to your draft post and selecting “Edit thumbnail” in the post editor.

How do I upload my Substack video clips to LinkedIn?

When you publish a video post on Substack, you can have it automatically uploaded to your LinkedIn profile.

Things to note before you start:

  • Ensure that you’ve created a LinkedIn account.
  • Your LinkedIn account will be synced to your Substack profile and not your publication. If a number of creators contribute to a single publication, we recommend using a shared LinkedIn account to ensure videos are uploaded to the right place.

Connecting your accounts

  1. Connect your Substack and LinkedIn accounts in the Connected Accounts section on your publication’s Settings page.

You can also connect your LinkedIn account on the Publish page after you finish your draft in the post editor and select “Continue”.

  1. Click “Connect” or “Sign In” and you’ll see a list of permissions that Substack would like to access on your LinkedIn account. Click “Allow”. If you’re not logged into your LinkedIn account, you’ll be asked to log in first.

Note: If you’d like to disconnect your LinkedIn and Substack accounts, you can do so on your LinkedIn settings.

How do I send clips from my Substack videos to my LinkedIn connected account?

Sharing clips from your video posts on your LinkedIn channel can drive subscribers back to your Substack. Substack’s auto-generated clips feature makes this effortless once you have connected to LinkedIn.

  • After you record a live video or upload a video to your video post, finish your draft and click “Continue”.
  • On the Publish page, scroll down to LinkedIn in the Connected accounts section and click Edit. From there, you’ll be able to choose up to two clips that were automatically generated from your video to share on LinkedIn. Note: If you don’t see the Edit button or the option to upload two clips, it likely means no clips have been generated for this specific video.

  1. After selecting up to two clips to upload, click Update. When you publish your post on Substack, the selected clips will automatically upload to LinkedIn as well.

  2. Once published, you’ll see clickable links for the clips in the post page share center. These links will take you directly to LinkedIn where you can edit the post description.

Tip: It can take a few minutes for the Substack video posts to publish on LinkedIn.

Live video on Substack via RTMP

Note: This feature is in beta and only available for select creators who use live video on Substack.

You can stream to the Substack app as well as your Substack website using Streamyard and OBS via our RTMP integration.

You’ll need to decide whether you want to stream a portrait video or a landscape video. This guide will show you how to do both.

As long as you can stream to MUX and insert a stream key, this feature will work by following the steps below.

Streaming a landscape video via RTMP

  • Generate a stream key and server URL in your writer dashboard.
  • Use this stream key and server URL to connect a 3rd party streaming tool like Streamyard to Substack.
  • Once the stream key and server URL are specified, simply go live or schedule the live for a future date/time. The stream will show up at the top of your publication website as well as in the app. In the app, viewers can rotate their phones for a more immersive experience. Watch a video here to see how this process works on Streamyard:

Streaming a portrait video via RTMP

First, you’ll need to set the video dimensions:

  1. Open OBS and go to Preferences.

  2. Select Video.

  3. Adjust the base and output resolution to 1080x1920. Do not set it to 1920x1080.

  4. Save these changes.

Next, head to Substack to follow these steps:

  1. On your desktop dashboard, select “Create” and then “Live video”.

  2. On the right panel of the screen, click on “Go live with stream key”.

  3. Enter a title, select your audience, and click “Generate stream key” if you’re ready to go live or select “Schedule stream” if you’d like to stream at a later time in the future.

  4. When you’re ready to go live, copy the stream key that’s been generated and head to OBS. Click on Stream found in your settings.

Tip: Contributors, admins, owners, and hosts can view and copy stream keys directly.

  1. Click on Service. From the drown-down menu, select “Show all” and then “MUX”.

  2. Enter the stream key you generated from your live video post on your Substack publication.

  3. Click OK.

  4. Start the stream.

Watch a video here to see the setup steps in action:

How do I schedule an RTMP live video?

First, create a stream key from your writer dashboard by clicking “Create” and then “New live video”. Go to the bottom right of the screen and select “Go live with stream key.” Turn on the scheduling toggle. Make sure you have correctly selected the user you want to go live and have specified your desired email preferences. Once you finish scheduling, you’ll get a unique link for your stream that you can promote to socials.

To retrieve your stream key and server url, click on Live video on your publication’s Dashboard. Head to the “Scheduled” tab and click on “Stream page”. Click “Update stream” and the scheduled live video’s stream key and server URL will appear on the next screen.

On the Streamyard/OBS side, We recommend streaming with a normal landscape setup. On Streamyard, you’ll need to add a custom RTMP destination and paste in your stream key and server url.

When it’s time to go live, simply go live from Streamyard and you’ll be live on Substack (and all notification emails will automatically send).

Note: You will need to create a new stream key and server URL every time you plan to stream to Substack.

How do I edit or cancel a scheduled RTMP live video?

On web, click on Live video on your publication’s Dashboard. Head to the “Scheduled” tab and click on the three dots next to the scheduled live video. Select “Cancel” to cancel the upcoming RTMP live video or select “Edit” to change the date or time of the upcoming RTMP live video.

How do I view comments on my RTMP live stream?

To see comments, open the live stream in a separate tab as a viewer. Comments don’t show up in Streamyard, so this is the only way to view them in real time.

How can I go live with a co-host on my RTMP live stream?

On your desktop dashboard, click “Create” and select “Live video”. On the bottom right panel, select “Go live with stream key”. In the pop-up, slide the toggle to the right next to “Invite co-hosts” and select “Continue”.

You’ll be prompted to search for your co-host on Substack: search using their profile name or Substack handle. Only users who have the Substack iOS or Android app can be invited to be your co-host on your RTMP live stream.

Once you enter a profile name or Substack handle and find the person you want to co-host with, check the box next to their name and select “Invite X co-host and generate stream key”.

An email invitation will be sent to your co-host with details about the live event. A new web page will open where your co-host will be able to accept or decline the invite. If they accept the invite, they’ll also have the options to send calendar invite emails and/or an email notification to their subscribers about the upcoming live stream.

Tip: Co-hosts can also view the invite in their Activity notification section on web.

As the main host of the live stream, you’ll be able to control the live stream such as starting and ending it.

When the live stream begins, you and your co-host will appear as live on Substack and subscribers will get notifications if you’ve selected to notify them when creating the live video.

Your live video audience one of the following options: everyone, subscribers, or paying subscribers. For example, if the audience is only paying subscribers, then paying subscribers to either Substack will be allowed to watch the live stream and free subscribers from either Substack will not be able to view the live stream.

When the stream is over, both you and the co-host(s) will get the recording in your email inbox and a draft post of the stream will automatically be created so that you can publish and send it to your subscribers at a later time.

If you’ve scheduled a RTMP live stream and decide you want to invite a co-host, you can do that by navigating to the Live video section on your publication’s Dashboard. Head to the “Scheduled” tab and select the three dots next to the stream. Click on “Invite co-hosts” and you’ll go through the invitation flow.

Your account

How can I export my invoice data for tax purposes?

You can export subscription invoice data for tax purposes by following these steps:

  1. On your publication’s Settings page, click on Payments in the left navigation bar.

  2. Under Advanced, select “Request a new report”.

  3. From here, you can request a report of all invoices created within a time period of your choosing. Once you’ve selected a start and end date, click “Request report”.

  4. A .csv file containing your invoice data will be generated which you can download. Once the report is ready, you’ll be notified by email. Alternatively, you can view any requested exports or reports at this link:

your.substack.com

  1. In the report, you can a a breakdown of fees, currency, and the type of subscription plan for each subscriber.

For more information on filing taxes for payments you’ve received from subscriptions, check out this article here.

How do I change my email sender name?

  • Sign into your Substack account and navigate to your publication’s Settings page. Click on Emails in the left navigation bar.
  • Under “Email Sender Name”, edit your name and click “Save”.

How do I file taxes on the money I’ve made on Substack?

You can manage your tax forms from your Stripe account dashboard (e.g., download a 1099 tax form if you’re a U.S. citizen).

Stripe issues your tax forms. As required by the IRS, the amount listed on your 1099 form reflects the total gross volume that you’ve processed, and includes things like disputes, refunds, Stripe fees, and Substack fees.

Note: You will need to deduct Stripe’s and Substack’s fees from your total. It does not include authorization holds or anything where money has not yet been exchanged.

To figure out your net volume (i.e., the amount that you personally earned), you will need to determine your deductions, such as Stripe fees and “Application fees” (i.e., Substack’s fees). More information is available on Stripe’s website, including where to find these numbers in your dashboard.

How do I see a list of publications that I write or contribute to?

To see a list of your publications:

  • Sign into your Substack account and click on your profile icon.
  • From the drop-down menu, select Settings.
  1. Under “Publications”, you’ll see a list of all the publications you write or contribute to.

What can I do if I’m having issues with my custom domain and can’t access my Substack publication?

If you can’t access your Substack publication because your custom domain is broken or you’re no longer using it, visit our support chat widget here and ask for a human support specialist to disable your custom domain and restore account access.

Even if you feel you have temporarily lost access to your Substack dashboard due to your custom domain, you can access your publication settings by logging in and going directly to this URL: “[yoursubdomain].substack.com/publish/settings”

From the Domain section of the settings page, you can select Disable Custom Domain to remove your custom domain and revert to the default Substack domain.

Miscellaneous

How do I report trademark infringement?

Substack does not allow trademark infringement on its platform. If you believe something on Substack infringes on your trademark ownership, you may submit a notice of trademark infringement.

To take action on an infringing use of a trademark, we generally require a valid trademark registration or proof of trademark trademark ownership. Once proof of ownership is provided, the publisher will be given 30 days to remove or correct the allegedly infringing use of the trademark, or else provide credible reasons as to why the reported use is not infringing.

If the publisher fails to justify its use of the allegedly infringing material within 30 days and the allegedly infringing trademark is not otherwise removed or corrected, Substack will unpublish the allegedly infringing content and notify the publisher.

** Please note that, in most cases, Substack does not take action on allegations of trademark infringement arising solely from the use of a subdomain unless the subdomain appears to have been registered in bad faith for cybersquatting purposes. **
